I read this brief and businesslike communication several times during the day; and after dinner that night, in order to meditate upon it in solitude, I left the house and wandered off in the direction of the village.

I was midway between house and village when I became aware that I was being followed. The night was dark, and the wind moving in the treetops emphasized the loneliness of the country road. Both time and place were such as made it peculiarly unpleasant to hear stealthy footsteps on the road behind me.

Uncertainty in such cases is the unnerving thing. I turned sharply, and began to walk back on tiptoe in the direction from which I had come.

I had not been mistaken. A moment later a dark figure loomed up out of the darkness, and the exclamation which greeted me, as I made my presence known, showed that I had taken him by surprise.

There was a momentary pause. I expected the man, whoever he might be, to run, but he held his ground. Indeed, he edged forward.

===Chinese===
白天里，我把这份简短而公事公办的通信翻来覆去读了好几遍；当晚饭后，为了独自对它细细参详，我出了宅子，朝村子的方向信步走去。

走到宅子与村子之间的一半，我察觉自己被人跟上了。夜色深黑，风在树梢间走动，把乡间道路的孤寂衬得愈发逼人。这般的时辰、这般的地段，使得身后路面上一阵蹑手蹑脚的脚步声，听来格外地不受用。

这类局面里，最挫人胆气的是悬而未决。我猛地转过身去，踮起脚尖，朝来路摸了回去。

我没有听错。片刻之后，一条黑影从黑暗里凸现出来；待我一亮身形，迎头招呼我的那声惊呼表明，我杀了他一个措手不及。

双方僵了片刻。我原以为那人——不管他是谁——会撒腿就跑，可他钉住不动。非但不动，还往前蹭。

===Original===
“Get back!” I said, and allowed my stick to rasp suggestively on the road before raising it in readiness for any sudden development. It was as well that he should know it was there.

The hint seemed to wound rather than frighten him.

“Aw, cut out the rough stuff, bo,” he said reproachfully in a cautious, husky undertone. “I ain’t goin’ to start anything.”

I had an impression that I had heard the voice before, but I could not place it.

“What are you following me for?” I demanded. “Who are you?”

“Say, I want a talk wit youse. I took a slant at youse under de lamppost back dere, an’ I seen it was you, so I tagged along. Say, I’m wise to your game, sport.”

===Chinese===
「退回去！」我说着，先让手杖在路面上意味深长地刮了一记，然后提起来举好，以防任何突发的事态。最好让他知道，它就在那儿。

这一下点拨，似乎没吓着他，倒伤了他的心。

「嗐，收起你那套动粗的把戏吧，哥们儿，」他责怪地说，用一副小心翼翼的沙哑低嗓。「我没打算挑起什么事儿。」

我有个印象：这嗓音从前听过，只是想不起落在哪儿。

「你跟着我做什么？」我诘问。「你是谁？」

「嘿，我想同你唠一唠。方才在那边路灯底下，我斜眼瞄了你一瞄，认出是你，就一路缀了上来。嘿，你那套把戏，我门儿清，老哥。」

===Original===
I had identified him by this time. Unless there were two men in the neighbourhood of Sanstead who hailed from the Bowery, this must be the man I had seen at the Feathers who had incurred the disapproval of Miss Benjafield.

“I haven’t the faintest idea what you mean,” I said. “What is my game?”

His voice became reproachful again.

“Ah chee!” he protested. “Quit yer kiddin’! What was youse rubberin’ around de house for last night if you wasn’t trailin’ de kid?”

“Was it you who ran into me last night?” I asked.

“Gee! I t’ought it was a tree. I came near takin’ de count.”

===Chinese===
到这时我已认出了他。除非桑斯特德这一带窝着两位鲍厄里（Bowery）出身的汉子，否则这位必是我在羽饰旅馆见过、且招得本杰菲尔德小姐大不以为然的那一位。

「我全然不明白你的意思，」我说。「我那套把戏是什么？」

他的嗓音又带上了委屈。

「嗐咦！」他抗议道。「别耍人了！昨儿夜里你要不是在盯那小子的梢，绕着宅子东张西望地打探什么？」

「昨儿夜里撞上我的，可是你？」我问。

「乖乖！我还当是棵树呢。差一点儿就吃了读秒。」

===Original===
“I did take it. You seemed in a great hurry.”

“Hell!” said the man simply, and expectorated.

“Say,” he resumed, having delivered this criticism on that stirring episode, “dat’s a great kid, dat Nugget. I t’ought it was a Black Hand soup explosion when he cut loose. But, say, let’s don’t waste time. We gotta get together about dat kid.”

“Certainly, if you wish it. What do you happen to mean?”

“Aw, quit yer kiddin’!” He expectorated again. He seemed to be a man who could express the whole gamut of emotions by this simple means. “I know you!”

“Then you have the advantage of me, though I believe I remember seeing you before. Weren’t you at the Feathers one Wednesday evening, singing something about a dog?”

===Chinese===
「读秒我可是结结实实吃了个足。你倒像有天大的急事。」

「妈的！」这人言简意赅地说，随即吐了口唾沫。

「嘿，」他对那桩惊心动魄的插曲发表完这番评语，又续上话头，「那小子可真了不得，那个小金块。他一撒开嗓，我还当是黑手党（Black Hand）的炸药『汤』炸了锅呢。不过，嘿，咱们别糟践工夫。这小子的事，咱们得凑到一块儿盘算。」

「行啊，只要你乐意。你究竟是想说什么？」

「嗐，别耍人了！」他又吐了口唾沫。瞧他那架势，是个单凭这一样朴素的手段，就能把人类情感的整个音域表达出来的人。「你那点底细，我清楚！」

「那么你占了我的先——不过我想我记起来见过你。某个星期三晚上，你是不是在羽饰旅馆，唱过一支关于狗的什么歌？」

===Original===
“Sure. Dat was me.”

“What do you mean by saying that you know me?”

“Aw, quit yer kiddin’, Sam!”

There was, it seemed to me, a reluctantly admiring note in his voice.

“Tell me, who do you think I am?” I asked patiently.

“Ahr ghee! You can’t string me, sport. Smooth Sam Fisher, is who you are, bo. I know you.”

===Chinese===
「可不。那就是我。」

「你说你认得我，这话作何讲？」

「嗐，别耍人了，山姆！」

我听出，他的嗓音里有一缕勉勉强强的钦佩。

「告诉我，你当我是谁？」我耐心地问。

「嗐咦！你耍不着我，老哥。你是圆滑山姆·费舍尔，没跑了，哥们儿。我认得你。」

===Original===
I was too surprised to speak. Verily, some have greatness thrust upon them.

“I hain’t never seen youse, Sam,” he continued, “but I know it’s you. And I’ll tell youse how I doped it out. To begin with, there ain’t but you and your bunch and me and my bunch dat knows de Little Nugget’s on dis side at all. Dey sneaked him out of New York mighty slick. And I heard that you had come here after him. So when I runs into a guy dat’s trailin’ de kid down here, well, who’s it going to be if it ain’t youse? And when dat guy talks like a dude, like they all say you do, well, who’s it going to be if it ain’t youse? So quit yer kiddin’, Sam, and let’s get down to business.”

“Have I the pleasure of addressing Mr. Buck MacGinnis?” I said. I felt convinced that this could be no other than that celebrity.

“Dat’s right. Dere’s no need to keep up anyt’ing wit me, Sam. We’re bote on de same trail, so let’s get down to it.”

“One moment,” I said. “Would it surprise you to hear that my name is Burns, and that I am a master at the school?”

He expectorated admirably.

===Chinese===
我惊讶得说不出话来。诚哉斯言：有些人的伟大，是硬生生塞到他们头上来的。

「我没见过你本人，山姆，」他接着说，「可我知道准是你。我告诉你我是怎么盘算出来的。头一条：晓得小金块到了大西洋这一边的，普天下只有你们一伙和我们一伙。他们把他从纽约偷运出来的时候，手脚那个利索。跟着我又听说，你追到这儿来了。那么，叫我撞见一个在这地界盯那小子梢的家伙，嘿——不是你还能是谁？再说那家伙开起腔来一副公子哥儿调，人人都说你就好这一口，嘿——不是你还能是谁？所以别耍人了，山姆，咱们谈正事。」

「此刻同我说话的，可否有幸正是巴克·麦金尼斯先生？」我说。我笃定，除那位名流之外，再无第二人选。

「让你说着了。跟我用不着端着，山姆。咱们俩踩的是同一条踪迹，那就直奔正题吧。」

「且慢，」我说。「倘若听说我姓伯恩斯、是这学校的一名教员，你会不会吃惊？」

他吐了一口漂亮的唾沫。

===Original===
“Hell, no!” he said. “Gee, it’s just what you would be, Sam. I always heard youse had been one of dese rah-rah boys oncest. Say, it’s mighty smart of youse to be a perfessor. You’re right in on de ground floor.”

His voice became appealing.

“Say, Sam, don’t be a hawg. Let’s go fifty-fifty in dis deal. My bunch and me has come a hell of a number of miles on dis proposition, and dere ain’t no need for us to fall scrappin’ over it. Dere’s plenty for all of us. Old man Ford’ll cough up enough for everyone, and dere won’t be any fuss. Let’s sit in togedder on dis Nugget t’ing. It ain’t like as if it was an ornery two-by-four deal. I wouldn’t ask youse if it wasn’t big enough fir de whole bunch of us.”

As I said nothing, he proceeded.

“It ain’t square, Sam, to take advantage of your having education. If it was a square fight, and us bote wit de same chance, I wouldn’t say; but you bein’ a dude perfessor and gettin’ right into de place like dat ain’t right. Say, don’t be a hawg, Sam. Don’t swipe it all. Fifty-fifty! Does dat go?”

===Chinese===
「妈的，不吃惊！」他说。「乖乖，可不正是你干得出来的，山姆。我老听人说，你早年间也是大学里喊『拉拉』的少爷们中间的一个。嘿，你扮个教书匠，可精明到了家。你这是一脚就踏进了最底层，占尽了先机。」

他的嗓音转为恳求。

「嘿，山姆，别当贪心猪。这票买卖咱们对半分。为这么档子事，我同我这伙人跑了他妈的一老远路，犯不上为它滚在一处厮打。油水多的是，够咱们人人有份。老福特会吐出够每个人份的钱，还闹不出一点乱子。小金块这档子事，咱们合伙一块儿坐进去。这可不是那种上不得台面的三瓜俩枣小买卖。要不是肥得够咱们全伙分，我犯得上开口求你？」

见我不搭腔，他接着往下说。

「这可不公道，山姆，仗着自己念过书占便宜。要是一场光明正大的较量，咱们俩机会均等，我无话可说；可你当着个公子哥儿教书匠，就这么直通通混进了宅子，这就不是事儿。嘿，别当贪心猪，山姆。别想一口全包圆。对半分！这买卖成不成？」

===Original===
“I don’t know,” I said. “You had better ask the real Sam. Good night.”

I walked past him and made for the school gates at my best pace. He trotted after me, pleading.

“Sam, give us a quarter, then.”

I walked on.

“Sam, don’t be a hawg!”

He broke into a run.

===Chinese===
「我不知道，」我说。「你最好去问问正牌的山姆。晚安。」

我从他身旁走过，拿出看家的脚程直奔校门。他一路小跑地追上来，嘴里央告着。

「山姆，那就赏个两毛五吧。」

我只管走。

「山姆，别当贪心猪！」

他撒腿跑了起来。

===Original===
“Sam!” His voice lost its pleading tone and rasped menacingly.

“Gee, if I had me canister, youse wouldn’t be so flip! Listen here, you big cheese! You t’ink youse is de only t’ing in sight, huh? Well, we ain’t done yet. You’ll see yet. We’ll fix you! Youse had best watch out.”

I stopped and turned on him. “Look here, you fool,” I cried. “I tell you I am not Sam Fisher. Can’t you understand that you have got hold of the wrong man? My name is Burns﻿—*Burns*.”

He expectorated﻿—scornfully this time. He was a man slow by nature to receive ideas, but slower to rid himself of one that had contrived to force its way into what he probably called his brain. He had decided on the evidence that I was Smooth Sam Fisher, and no denials on my part were going to shake his belief. He looked on them merely as so many unsportsmanlike quibbles prompted by greed.

“Tell it to Sweeney!” was the form in which he crystallized his scepticism.

“May be you’ll say youse ain’t trailin’ de Nugget, huh?”

===Chinese===
「山姆！」他的嗓音丢了央告腔，砂纸似的磨出威胁。

「乖乖，我要是带着我的家伙，你还敢这么油嘴！你听好了，大人物！你当你自己是眼面前独一份，嗯？哼，咱们的事儿还没完。有你瞧的。我们要收拾你！你最好放机灵点儿。」

我收住脚，转身冲他。「听着，你这蠢货，」我喊道。「我告诉你，我不是山姆·费舍尔。你难道不明白，你认错了人？我姓伯恩斯——*伯恩斯*。」

他啐了一口——这一回是鄙夷的啐法。他这个人生性接收念头迟缓，可要把一个好不容易挤进他多半称之为脑子的事物里去的念头再挤出去，那就更迟缓了。他已凭种种迹象断定我是圆滑山姆·费舍尔，我这边再怎么否认，也休想撼动他的信念半分。在他眼里，那些否认不过是贪心作祟，一串不讲体育精神的遁词罢了。

「跟斯威尼（Sweeney）说去吧！」——他便是用这个句式，给自己的怀疑定了型。

「兴许你还要说，你没在盯小金块的梢，嗯？」

===Original===
It was a home-thrust. If truth-telling has become a habit, one gets slowly off the mark when the moment arrives for the prudent lie. Quite against my will, I hesitated. Observant Mr. MacGinnis perceived my hesitation and expectorated triumphantly.

“Ah ghee!” he remarked. And then with a sudden return to ferocity, “All right, you Sam, you wait! We’ll fix you, and fix you good! See? Dat goes. You t’ink youse kin put it across us, huh? All right, you’ll get yours. You wait!”

And with these words he slid off into the night. From somewhere in the murky middle distance came a scornful “Hawg!” and he was gone, leaving me with a settled conviction that, while I had frequently had occasion, since my expedition to Sanstead began, to describe affairs as complex, their complexity had now reached its height. With a watchful Pinkerton’s man within, and a vengeful gang of rivals without, Sanstead House seemed likely to become an unrestful place for a young kidnapper with no previous experience.

The need for swift action had become imperative.

===Chinese===
这一记正扎在心窝上。倘若说真话已然成了习惯，那么当谨慎的谎言该上场的时刻来到，人起跑就会慢上半拍。全然违背我的本意，我犹豫了。明察秋毫的麦金尼斯先生看出了我的犹豫，得胜似的啐了一口。

「嗐咦！」他评点了一句。随即骤然回凶：「好哇，你这山姆，你等着！我们要收拾你，好好地收拾你！瞧见没有？这话算数。你当你能涮我们一把，嗯？好哇，有你好果子吃。你等着！」

丢下这些话，他一滑便滑进了夜色。从昏蒙的中距离某处，飘来一声鄙夷的「贪心猪！」，人便没了踪影，只留下我抱定一个业已坐实的信念：自打我这趟桑斯特德远征开场，尽管我不止一次有过缘由，把局面形容成复杂，如今这复杂总算攀上了顶峰。宅里守着一个眼观六路的平克顿（Pinkerton）的人，宅外围着一帮寻仇的对头匪伙——对这么一个毫无历练的年轻绑匪来说，桑斯特德宅瞧着要成一块不得安生的地界了。

火速行动的必要，已成燃眉。

### II / II

===Original===
White, the butler, looking singularly unlike a detective﻿—which, I suppose, is how a detective wants to look﻿—was taking the air on the football field when I left the house next morning for a before-breakfast stroll. The sight of him filled me with a desire for firsthand information on the subject of the man Mr. MacGinnis supposed me to be and also of Mr. MacGinnis himself. I wanted to be assured that my friend Buck, despite appearances, was a placid person whose bark was worse than his bite.

White’s manner, at our first conversational exchanges, was entirely that of the butler. From what I came to know of him later, I think he took an artistic pride in throwing himself into whatever role he had to assume.

At the mention of Smooth Sam Fisher, however, his manner peeled off him like a skin, and he began to talk as himself, a racy and vigorous self vastly different from the episcopal person he thought it necessary to be when on duty.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，我出宅门做早饭前的散步，男管家怀特正在足球场上透气——那副模样，丝毫不像个侦探，而我想，侦探要的正是这副模样。一看见他，我心里便生出一股渴望：想就两桩事弄些第一手的消息——麦金尼斯先生认定我所是的那一位，以及麦金尼斯先生本人。我想讨一个放心：我的朋友巴克尽管样子骇人，实是个性子平和的人物——叫得凶，咬得轻。

最初几个回合的交谈里，怀特的那副做派，完全是管家的做派。照我日后对他的了解，我想，无论要他担纲什么角色，他都要整个身心扑将进去，并以此为一种艺术上的自豪。

然而，「圆滑山姆·费舍尔」一出口，他那副做派便像一层皮似的从身上褪了下来，他开始用本来面目讲话——一个泼辣痛快、元气淋漓的本来面目，同他当值时自认必须扮出的那位主教式人物，相去不啻天壤。

===Original===
“White,” I said, “do you know anything of Smooth Sam Fisher?”

He stared at me. I suppose the question, led up to by no previous remark, was unusual.

“I met a gentleman of the name of Buck MacGinnis﻿—he was our visitor that night, by the way﻿—and he was full of Sam. Do you know him?”

“Buck?”

“Either of them.”

“Well, I’ve never seen Buck, but I know all about him. There’s pepper to Buck.”

===Chinese===
「怀特，」我说，「圆滑山姆·费舍尔的事，你可知道些什么？」

他直眼瞧我。我想，前头没有任何铺垫，这问题问得是突兀了些。

「我碰上过一位巴克·麦金尼斯先生——对了，那晚来的客人就是他——他一嘴都是山姆。你认识他吗？」

「巴克？」

「两位随便哪一位。」

「唔，巴克我从来没见过，可他的底细我一清二楚。巴克身上有股子辣味。」

===Original===
“So I should imagine. And Sam?”

“You may take it from me that there’s more pepper to Sam’s little finger than there is to Buck’s whole body. Sam could make Buck look like the last run of shad, if it came to a showdown. Buck’s just a common roughneck. Sam’s an educated man. He’s got brains.”

“So I gathered. Well, I’m glad to hear you speak so well of him, because that’s who I’m supposed to be.”

“How’s that?”

“Buck MacGinnis insists that I am Smooth Sam Fisher. Nothing I can say will shift him.”

White stared. He had very bright humorous brown eyes. Then he began to laugh.

===Chinese===
「这我料想得到。那么山姆呢？」

「你只管信我的话：山姆小拇指上的辣味，就比巴克全身的加起来还多。真到了摊牌那一步，山姆能把巴克整治得像汛期末尾那批鲱鱼——蔫得不成鱼形。巴克不过是个寻常的莽汉。山姆可是个受过教育的人。他有脑子。」

「这我听出来了。唔，听你把他夸成这样，我很高兴，因为人家认定的我，正是他。」

「这是怎么说？」

「巴克·麦金尼斯一口咬定，我就是圆滑山姆·费舍尔。我说什么也扳不动他。」

怀特瞪眼望着我。他有一双亮晶晶、笑意盈盈的棕色眼睛。跟着他放声大笑。

===Original===
“Well, what do you know about that?” he exclaimed. “Wouldn’t that jar you!”

“It would. I may say it did. He called me a hog for wanting to keep the Little Nugget to myself, and left threatening to ‘fix me.’ What would you say the verb ‘to fix’ signified in Mr. MacGinnis’s vocabulary?”

White was still chuckling quietly to himself.

“He’s a wonder!” he observed. “Can you beat it? Taking you for Smooth Sam!”

“He said he had never seen Smooth Sam. Have you?”

“Lord, yes.”

===Chinese===
「嚯，你说这叫什么事！」他喊道。「这还能不惊着你！」

「能。不妨说已经惊着了。他骂我贪心猪，说我想把小金块独吞，临走还撂下狠话，要『收拾』（fix）我。依你看，在麦金尼斯先生的词汇表里，『收拾』（fix）这个动词当作何解？」

怀特兀自低低地笑个不停。

「他真是个奇才！」他评说道。「服不服？竟把你认成了圆滑山姆！」

「他说他从来没见过圆滑山姆。你见过吗？」

「老天，见过。」

===Original===
“Does he look like me?”

“Not a bit.”

“Do you think he’s over here in England?”

“Sam? I know he is.”

“Then Buck MacGinnis was right?”

“Dead right, as far as Sam being on the trail goes. Sam’s after the Nugget to get him this time. He’s tried often enough before, but we’ve been too smart for him. This time he allows he’s going to bring it off.”

===Chinese===
「他长得像我么？」

「一点也不。」

「你觉得他到英国这边来了么？」

「山姆？我知道他来了。」

「那么巴克·麦金尼斯倒是说对了？」

「对得不能再对——就山姆已上了踪迹这一点而言。这一回，山姆是冲着把小金块弄到手来的。从前他试的回数够多了，可我们回回都比他精。这一回，他放话要干成一票。」

===Original===
“Then why haven’t we seen anything of him? Buck MacGinnis seems to be monopolizing the kidnapping industry in these parts.”

“Oh, Sam’ll show up when he feels good and ready. You can take it from me that Sam knows what he is doing. Sam’s a special pet of mine. I don’t give a flip for Buck MacGinnis.”

“I wish I had your cheery disposition! To me Buck MacGinnis seems a pretty important citizen. I wonder what he meant by ‘fix’?”

White, however, declined to leave the subject of Buck’s more gifted rival.

“Sam’s a college man, you know. That gives him a pull. He has brains, and can use them.”

“That was one of the points on which Buck MacGinnis reproached me. He said it was not fair to use my superior education.”

===Chinese===
「那怎么我们连他的影子都没瞧见？这一带的绑架行业，巴克·麦金尼斯像是一家独占了。」

「嗐，山姆自有露面的时候——等他自觉火候十足、心甘情愿了，自然会露。你只管信我的话：山姆干的事，山姆心里有数。山姆是我的心头宝。巴克·麦金尼斯么，我半个子儿也不押。」

「我真想有你这副乐天派的脾气！在我看，巴克·麦金尼斯可是位了不得的要人。我纳闷，他那个『收拾』（fix）到底是什么意思？」

然而怀特不肯撇下巴克那位天分更高的对手这个话题。

「山姆是上过大学的，你知道。这给他添了一分优势。他有脑子，还会使。」

「那正是巴克·麦金尼斯数落我的一条罪状。他说，拿我占优势的教育压人，不公道。」

===Original===
He laughed.

“Buck’s got no sense. That’s why you find him carrying on like a porch-climber. It’s his only notion of how to behave when he wants to do a job. And that’s why there’s only one man to keep your eye on in this thing of the Little Nugget, and that’s Sam. I wish you could get to know Sam. You’d like him.”

“You seem to look on him as a personal friend. I certainly don’t like Buck.”

“Oh, Buck!” said White scornfully.

We turned towards the house as the sound of the bell came to us across the field.

“Then you think we may count on Sam’s arrival, sooner or later, as a certainty?” I said.

===Chinese===
他笑了。

「巴克没脑子。所以你才见他那副做派，活像个翻阳台的毛贼。他要办一票买卖时该怎么行事，他脑子里就只有这么一个概念。也正因为这样，小金块这档子事里，值得你盯牢的只有一个人，那就是山姆。我巴不得你能同山姆结识结识。你会喜欢他的。」

「你简直把他当私人朋友看。巴克我可是喜欢不起来。」

「嗐，巴克！」怀特鄙夷地说。

铃声穿过球场传到我们耳边，我们转身朝宅子走去。

「那么你认为，山姆迟早驾到，这可以算板上钉钉了？」我说。

===Original===
“Surest thing you know.”

“You will have a busy time.”

“All in the day’s work.”

“I suppose I ought to look at it in that way. But I do wish I knew exactly what Buck meant by ‘fix.’ ”

White at last condescended to give his mind to the trivial point.

“I guess he’ll try to put one over on you with a sandbag,” he said carelessly. He seemed to face the prospect with calm.

===Chinese===
「这还用说，钉得死死的。」

「那你可有得忙了。」

「都是分内的事。」

「我想我也该这么看。可我实在盼望知道，巴克说的『收拾』（fix）究竟指什么。」

怀特总算屈尊分了一点心思，给这个鸡毛蒜皮的疑点。

「我猜，他会想法子拿沙袋冷不丁闷你一家伙，」他随口说道。面对这般前景，他似乎心境平和。

===Original===
“A sandbag, eh?” I said. “It sounds exciting.”

“And feels it. I know. I’ve had some.”

I parted from him at the door. As a comforter he had failed to qualify. He had not eased my mind to the slightest extent.

===Chinese===
「沙袋，呃？」我说。「听着怪刺激。」

「挨着也怪刺激。我知道。我挨过几家伙。」

我在门口同他作别。作为一名宽慰者，他没能取得参赛资格。我的心事，他一分一毫也没能替我宽解。

### VII / VII

===Original===

Looking at it now I can see that the days which followed Audrey’s arrival at Sanstead marked the true beginning of our acquaintanceship. Before, during our engagement, we had been strangers, artificially tied together, and she had struggled against the chain. But now, for the first time, we were beginning to know each other, and were discovering that, after all, we had much in common.

It did not alarm me, this growing feeling of comradeship. Keenly on the alert as I was for the least sign that would show that I was in danger of weakening in my loyalty to Cynthia, I did not detect one in my friendliness for Audrey. On the contrary, I was hugely relieved, for it seemed to me that the danger was past. I had not imagined it possible that I could ever experience towards her such a tranquil emotion as this easy friendliness. For the last five years my imagination had been playing round her memory, until I suppose I had built up in my mind some almost superhuman image, some goddess. What I was passing through now, of course, though I was unaware of it, was the natural reaction from that state of mind. Instead of the goddess, I had found a companionable human being, and I imagined that I had effected the change myself, and by sheer force of will brought Audrey into a reasonable relation to the scheme of things.

I suppose a not too intelligent moth has much the same views with regard to the lamp. His last thought, as he enters the flame, is probably one of self-congratulation that he has arranged his dealings with it on such a satisfactory commonsense basis.

===Chinese===

如今回头去看，我看得分明：奥黛丽来到桑斯特德之后的那段日子，才真正开始了我们的相识。从前，订亲的那段日子里，我们本是两个陌生人，被人为地拴在一处，而她一直挣扎着反抗那条锁链。可如今，破天荒头一遭，我们开始认识彼此了，并且发现：说到底，我们竟有许多相通之处。

这份与日俱增的同志情谊，并没有叫我惊慌。我瞪大眼睛严加戒备，唯恐出现一丝半点的迹象，表明我对辛西娅的忠诚有软化之虞；可是在我对奥黛丽的这份友善里，我一丝迹象也没侦出来。恰恰相反，我大大地松了口气，因为在我看来，危险已经过去了。我从没想到过，我对她居然还能怀有这般平静的一种情感——这样一份从容的友情。过去五年里，我的想象力一直在她的记忆四周盘桓嬉戏，直到——我想——我在心里搭起了一个近乎超人的形象，一尊女神。我此刻经历的，当然，虽然我自己浑然不觉，正是对那种心境的自然反弹。女神没有了，我找到的是一个宜于做伴的凡人；而我竟以为，这变化是我亲手促成的，以为我单凭意志的力量，就把奥黛丽安放进了万事格局中一个合情合理的位置。

我猜想，一只脑筋不太灵光的飞蛾，对那盏灯的看法大概也与此相仿。它扑进火焰里去的当口，最后一个念头多半是暗自庆幸：自己竟同这盏灯把关系打理得如此叫人满意、如此合乎常识。

===Original===

And then, when I was feeling particularly safe and complacent, disaster came.

The day was Wednesday, and my “afternoon off,” but the rain was driving against the windows, and the attractions of billiards with the marker at the Feathers had not proved sufficient to make me face the two-mile walk in the storm. I had settled myself in the study. There was a noble fire burning in the grate, and the darkness lit by the glow of the coals, the dripping of the rain, the good behaviour of my pipe, and the reflection that, as I sat there, Glossop was engaged downstairs in wrestling with my class, combined to steep me in a meditative peace. Audrey was playing the piano in the drawing-room. The sound came to me faintly through the closed doors. I recognized what she was playing. I wondered if the melody had the same associations for her that it had for me.

The music stopped. I heard the drawing-room door open. She came into the study.

===Chinese===

接着，正当我自觉格外安稳、格外志得意满的时候，大难临头了。

那天是星期三，轮到我「下午歇班」，可是雨点正抽打着窗户，而同羽饰旅馆的记分员打台球的那点吸引力，又不足以诱我顶着风暴走那两英里路。我便在书房里安顿下来。炉格里烧着一炉气派的好火，煤炭的红光照亮了昏暗，雨声淅沥，烟斗表现乖顺，再加想到我坐在此地的当儿，格拉索普正在楼下同我那个班摔跤角力——这一切合在一起，把我浸进了一种冥想般的安宁。奥黛丽在客厅里弹钢琴。琴声隔着关死的门隐隐传来。我听出了她弹的是什么。我不知道这支曲子在她心里，是否也牵出它在我心里牵出的那些联想。

琴声停了。我听见客厅的门开了。她走进书房来。

===Original===

“I didn’t know there was anyone here,” she said. “I’m frozen. The drawing-room fire’s out.”

“Come and sit down,” I said. “You don’t mind the smoke?”

I drew a chair up to the fire for her, feeling, as I did so, a certain pride. Here I was, alone with her in the firelight, and my pulse was regular and my brain cool. I had a momentary vision of myself as the Strong Man, the strong, quiet man with the iron grip on his emotions. I was pleased with myself.

She sat for some minutes, gazing into the fire. Little spurts of flame whistled comfortably in the heart of the black-red coals. Outside the storm shrieked faintly, and flurries of rain dashed themselves against the window.

“It’s very nice in here,” she said at last.

“Peaceful.”

===Chinese===

「我不知道这儿有人，」她说。「我冻僵了。客厅的炉火灭了。」

「来坐吧，」我说。「你不介意烟味吧？」

我替她把一把椅子拖到炉火跟前，做着这事的当儿，心里怀着几分得意。瞧我此刻：同她单独待在炉火光里，脉息平稳，头脑清凉。刹那之间，我看见自己幻成了那个「强人」——坚强、沉静、用铁腕攥住自己感情的男子汉。我对自己很满意。

她坐了几分钟，凝望着炉火。黑红煤块的中心，一小股一小股火苗舒舒服服地嘘叫着。外面风暴隐隐地呼啸，一阵阵骤雨扑打在窗上。

「这里面真舒服。」她终于说。

「清静。」

===Original===

I filled my pipe and re-lit it. Her eyes, seen for an instant in the light of the match, looked dreamy.

“I’ve been sitting here listening to you,” I said. “I liked that last thing you played.”

“You always did.”

“You remember that? Do you remember one evening﻿—no, you wouldn’t.”

“Which evening?”

“Oh, you wouldn’t remember. It’s only one particular evening when you played that thing. It sticks in my mind. It was at your father’s studio.”

===Chinese===

我给烟斗装上烟丝，重新点着。火柴的光里，她的眼睛现了一瞬，看上去如梦似幻。

「我一直坐在这儿听你弹，」我说。「你最后弹的那支曲子，我喜欢。」

「你从前就一直喜欢。」

「这你还记得？那你还记得有一天傍晚——不，你不会记得的。」

「哪个傍晚？」

「唉，你不会记得的。那不过是顶特别的一个傍晚，你弹了那支曲子。它粘在我脑子里忘不掉。那是在你父亲的画室里。」

===Original===

She looked up quickly.

“We went out afterwards and sat in the park.”

I sat up thrilled.

“A man came by with a dog,” I said.

“Two dogs.”

===Chinese===

她霍地抬起头来。

「后来我们出去了，坐在公园里。」

我激动得坐直了身子。

「有个人牵着一条狗打旁边走过。」我说。

「两条狗。」

===Original===

“One surely!”

“Two. A bulldog and a fox-terrier.”

“I remember the bulldog, but﻿—by Jove, you’re right. A fox-terrier with a black patch over his left eye.”

“Right eye.”

“Right eye. They came up to us, and you﻿—”

“Gave them chocolates.”

===Chinese===

「肯定是一条！」

「两条。一条斗牛犬，一条猎狐梗。」

「我记得那条斗牛犬，可是——我的老天爷，你说得对。一条猎狐梗，左眼上一块黑斑。」

「右眼。」

「右眼。它们走到我们跟前，后来你——」

「给它们吃了巧克力。」

===Original===

I sank back slowly in my chair.

“You’ve got a wonderful memory,” I said.

She bent over the fire without speaking. The rain rattled on the window.

“So you still like my playing, Peter?”

===Chinese===

我慢慢陷回椅子里。

「你的记性真了不起。」我说。

她俯身向着炉火，一言不发。雨点笃笃地敲着窗子。

「这么说，你还是喜欢我弹琴，彼得？」

===Original===

“I like it better than ever; there’s something in it now that I don’t believe there used to be. I can’t describe it﻿—something﻿—”

“I think it’s knowledge, Peter,” she said quietly. “Experience. I’m five years older than I was when I used to play to you before, and I’ve seen a good deal in those five years. It may not be altogether pleasant seeing life, but﻿—well, it makes you play the piano better. Experience goes in at the heart and comes out at the fingertips.”

It seemed to me that she spoke a little bitterly.

“Have you had a bad time, Audrey, these last years?” I said.

“Pretty bad.”

===Chinese===

「我比从前更喜欢了；如今这里头有一样东西，我相信从前是没有的。我说不上来——某种——」

「我想那是认识，彼得，」她轻声说。「是阅历。比起从前弹给你听的时候，我大了五岁，这五年里我见识了不少东西。见识人生未必全是愉快的事，可是——唉，它能让你的钢琴弹得更好。阅历从心口进去，从指尖出来。」

我觉得她的语气里带着一丝苦味。

「这些年你过得不好吧，奥黛丽？」我说。

「很不好。」

===Original===

“I’m sorry.”

“I’m not﻿—altogether. I’ve learned a lot.”

She was silent again, her eyes fixed on the fire.

“What are you thinking about?” I said.

“Oh, a great many things.”

“Pleasant?”

===Chinese===

「我很抱歉。」

「我倒不——不全然这么觉得。我学到了很多东西。」

她又沉默了，眼睛定定地望着炉火。

「你在想什么？」我说。

「哦，许许多多的事。」

「想得开心么？」

===Original===

“Mixed. The last thing I thought about was pleasant. That was, that I am very lucky to be doing the work I am doing now. Compared with some of the things I have done﻿—”

She shivered.

“I wish you would tell me about those years, Audrey,” I said. “What were some of the things you did?”

She leaned back in her chair and shaded her face from the fire with a newspaper. Her eyes were in the shadow.

“Well, let me see. I was a nurse for some time at the Lafayette Hospital in New York.”

“That’s hard work?”

===Chinese===

「五味杂陈。最后想的那一件倒是开心的。那就是：如今做着这份工作，我真是走运。比起我干过的某些事来——」

她打了个寒噤。

「我倒愿意你给我讲讲那些年，奥黛丽，」我说。「你都干过些什么？」

她仰靠进椅子里，拿一张报纸遮着脸，挡开炉火。她的眼睛落在阴影里。

「呃，让我想想。有一阵子，我在纽约的拉斐特医院（Lafayette Hospital）当护士。」

「那活儿累人吧？」

===Original===

“Horribly hard. I had to give it up after a while. But﻿—it teaches you.﻿ ﻿… You learn.﻿ ﻿… You learn﻿—all sorts of things. Realities. How much of your own trouble is imagination. You get real trouble in a hospital. You get it thrown at you.”

I said nothing. I was feeling﻿—I don’t know why﻿—a little uncomfortable, a little at a disadvantage, as one feels in the presence of someone bigger than oneself.

“Then I was a waitress.”

“A waitress?”

===Chinese===

「累得吓人。干了一阵，我只好撂下。可是——它教你。……你学。……你学到——各种各样的东西。实实在在的东西。你自家的烦恼，有多少纯是想象出来的。医院里给你的是真正的烦恼。它是劈头盖脸朝你砸过来的。」

我没作声。我心里觉得——不知道为什么——有几分不自在，有几分落于下风，就像人站在一个比自己高大的家伙面前时的那种感觉。

「后来我当过女招待。」

「女招待？」

===Original===

“I tell you I did everything. I was a waitress, and a very bad one. I broke plates. I muddled orders. Finally I was very rude to a customer and I went on to try something else. I forget what came next. I think it was the stage. I travelled for a year with a touring company. That was hard work, too, but I liked it. After that came dressmaking, which was harder and which I hated. And then I had my first stroke of real luck.”

“What was that?”

“I met Mr. Ford.”

“How did that happen?”

===Chinese===

「我告诉你，我什么都干过。我当过女招待，而且是顶蹩脚的一个。我摔碎盘子，把点菜单搅成一团乱麻。末了我对一位顾客非常无礼，便接着去另试别的营生。接下来是什么我忘了。我想是舞台。我跟着一个巡回剧团跑了一年码头。那也是苦活，可我喜欢。再往后是做女装裁缝，更苦，而且我恨透了它。然后，我头一遭交上了真正的好运。」

「什么好运？」

「我遇见了福特先生。」

「那是怎么遇上的？」

===Original===

“You wouldn’t remember a Miss Vanderley, an American girl who was over in London five or six years ago? My father taught her painting. She was very rich, but she was wild at that time to be Bohemian. I think that’s why she chose Father as a teacher. Well, she was always at the studio, and we became great friends, and one day, after all these things I have been telling you of, I thought I would write to her, and see if she could not find me something to do. She was a *dear*.” Her voice trembled, and she lowered the newspaper till her whole face was hidden. “She wanted me to come to their home and live on her forever, but I couldn’t have that. I told her I must work. So she sent me to Mr. Ford, whom the Vanderleys knew very well, and I became Ogden’s governess.”

“Great Scott!” I cried. “What!”

She laughed rather shakily.

“I don’t think I was a very good governess. I knew next to nothing. I ought to have been having a governess myself. But I managed somehow.”

“But Ogden?” I said. “That little fiend, didn’t he worry the life out of you?”

===Chinese===

「你不会记得一位范德利（Vanderley）小姐吧，五六年前来伦敦的一位美国姑娘？我父亲教过她画画。她非常有钱，可那时候她一门心思要做波西米亚人。我想就是为了这个，她才挑了父亲当老师。反正，她整天泡在画室里，我们成了顶好的朋友；后来有一天——就是我方才对你说过的那些事全都过去之后——我想我不妨给她写封信，看她能不能替我寻个事由。她是个*可人疼*的人。」她的声音颤了颤，报纸往下压了压，直到整张脸都藏了进去。「她要我去她家里住，靠她养活一辈子，可我不能那样。我告诉她我必须工作。于是她把我荐给了福特先生——范德利一家跟他很熟——我就当上了奥格登的家庭女教师。」

「我的天哪！」我叫道。「什么！」

她笑得有些发颤。

「我想我算不上一个很合格的家庭女教师。我差不多什么都不懂。按说，该是给我自己请一位家庭女教师才对。可我总算对付过来了。」

「可是奥格登呢？」我说。「那个小恶魔，他没把你烦个半死？」

===Original===

“Oh, I had luck there again. He happened to take a mild liking to me, and he was as good as gold﻿—for him; that’s to say, if I didn’t interfere with him too much, and I didn’t. I was horribly weak; he let me alone. It was the happiest time I had had for ages.”

“And when he came here, you came too, as a sort of ex-governess, to continue exerting your moral influence over him?”

She laughed.

“More or less that.”

We sat in silence for a while, and then she put into words the thought which was in both our minds.

===Chinese===

「哦，这方面我又交了好运。他碰巧对我有几分淡淡的喜欢，于是他乖得像金子——照他自己的标准；也就是说，只要我别过分去干涉他，而我恰好也不干涉。我软弱得吓人；他也就不来扰我。那是我多年来最最快活的一段时光。」

「那么他到这儿来的时候，你也跟了来，算是做个前家庭女教师，继续对他施展你的道德感化力？」

她笑了。

「差不多是这么回事。」

我们默默坐了一会儿，然后她把两人心里共有的那个念头说出了口。

===Original===

“How odd it seems, you and I sitting together chatting like this, Peter, after all﻿—all these years.”

“Like a dream!”

“Just like a dream﻿ ﻿… I’m so glad.﻿ ﻿… You don’t know how I’ve hated myself sometimes for﻿—for﻿—”

“Audrey! You mustn’t talk like that. Don’t let’s think of it. Besides, it was my fault.”

She shook her head.

“Well, put it that we didn’t understand one another.”

===Chinese===

「多么奇怪啊，彼得——你和我这样坐在一起闲谈，在隔了这——这么多年之后。」

「像一场梦！」

「正像一场梦……我真高兴。……你不知道我有时候多么恨我自己，为了——为了——」

「奥黛丽！千万别这么说。咱们别想它了。再说，那回是我的错。」

她摇了摇头。

「那么，就算是我们当年互不了解吧。」

===Original===

She nodded slowly.

“No, we didn’t understand one another.”

“But we do now,” I said. “We’re friends, Audrey.”

She did not answer. For a long time we sat in silence. And then the newspaper must have moved﻿—a gleam from the fire fell upon her face, lighting up her eyes; and at the sight something in me began to throb, like a drum warning a city against danger. The next moment the shadow had covered them again.

I sat there, tense, gripping the arms of my chair. I was tingling. Something was happening to me. I had a curious sensation of being on the threshold of something wonderful and perilous.

===Chinese===

她缓缓点了点头。

「是啊，我们当年互不了解。」

「可如今我们了解了，」我说。「我们是朋友，奥黛丽。」

她没有回答。我们默默坐了许久。后来，那张报纸一定是挪动了一下——一线炉火的光落到她脸上，照亮了她的眼睛；一看见这光景，我体内的某样东西便开始搏动，像一面敲给全城听的鼓，警告大难将至。下一刻，阴影又把那双眼睛盖住了。

我坐在那里，浑身绷紧，两手死死攥住椅子的扶手。我周身酥麻地颤着。有什么事情正在我身上发生。我有一种奇怪的感觉：自己正站在某个美妙而凶险的东西的门槛上。

===Original===

From downstairs there came the sound of boys’ voices. Work was over, and with it this talk by the firelight. In a few minutes somebody, Glossop, or Mr. Abney, would be breaking in on our retreat.

We both rose, and then﻿—it happened. She must have tripped in the darkness. She stumbled forward, her hand caught at my coat, and she was in my arms.

It was a thing of an instant. She recovered herself, moved to the door, and was gone.

But I stood where I was, motionless, aghast at the revelation which had come to me in that brief moment. It was the physical contact, the feel of her, warm and alive, that had shattered forever that flimsy structure of friendship which I had fancied so strong. I had said to Love, “Thus far, and no farther,” and Love had swept over me, the more powerful for being checked. The time of self-deception was over. I knew myself.

===Chinese===

楼下传来男孩子们的声音。功课结束了，这场炉火边的谈话也随着它一同结束。再过几分钟，就会有哪个人——格拉索普，或是阿布尼先生——闯进我们这个清静角落里来。

我们两人都站起身，接着——事情就发生了。她准是在黑暗里绊了一下。她朝前一栽，一只手抓向我的外衣，人已经在我怀里了。

那只是一瞬间的事。她站稳身子，走向门口，人就不见了。

可我站在原地，一动不动，为那短短一瞬里降临到我身上的启示惊骇万状。是身体的接触，是她的触感——温暖，鲜活——把那座友谊的脆弱建筑永远击碎了，而我原以为它坚固得很。我曾对爱情说过：「到此为止，不许再进。」而爱情把我整个席卷而过，因受阻而愈加势不可当。自欺的时日结束了。我认清了自己。

## VIII / VIII

### I / I

===Original===
That Buck MacGinnis was not the man to let the grass grow under his feet in a situation like the present one, I would have gathered from White’s remarks if I had not already done so from personal observation. The world is divided into dreamers and men of action. From what little I had seen of him I placed Buck MacGinnis in the latter class. Every day I expected him to act, and was agreeably surprised as each twenty-four hours passed and left me still unfixed. But I knew the hour would come, and it did.

I looked for frontal attack from Buck, not subtlety; but, when the attack came, it was so excessively frontal that my chief emotion was a sort of paralysed amazement. It seemed incredible that such peculiarly Wild Western events could happen in peaceful England, even in so isolated a spot as Sanstead House.

It had been one of those interminable days which occur only at schools. A school, more than any other institution, is dependent on the weather. Every small boy rises from his bed of a morning charged with a definite quantity of devilry; and this, if he is to sleep the sound sleep of health, he has got to work off somehow before bedtime. That is why the summer term is the one a master longs for, when the intervals between classes can be spent in the open. There is no pleasanter sight for an assistant-master at a private school than that of a number of boys expending their venom harmlessly in the sunshine.

On this particular day, snow had begun to fall early in the morning, and, while his pupils would have been only too delighted to go out and roll in it by the hour, they were prevented from doing so by Mr. Abney’s strict orders. No schoolmaster enjoys seeing his pupils running risks of catching cold, and just then Mr. Abney was especially definite on the subject. The Saturnalia which had followed Mr. MacGinnis’ nocturnal visit to the school had had the effect of giving violent colds to three lords, a baronet, and the younger son of an honourable. And, in addition to that, Mr. Abney himself, his penetrating tenor changed to a guttural croak, was in his bed looking on the world with watering eyes. His views, therefore, on playing in the snow as an occupation for boys were naturally prejudiced.

===Chinese===
巴克·麦金尼斯这个人，在眼下这种局面里绝不是肯让草在脚底下长起来的主儿——这话就算没有怀特的话为证，我凭亲自观察也早看明白了。世人分为两种：空想家与行动家。就我对他的那点有限见闻来看，我把巴克·麦金尼斯归入后一类。我天天料着他动手，而每熬过一个二十四小时，发现自己居然还原封未动，便都觉得是一份额外的惊喜。但我知道那个钟点终归要来，它也当真来了。

我料想从巴克那里等到的是正面强攻，而非斗智斗巧；可等攻势当真到来，它正面得如此彻底，弄得我压倒一切的情绪竟成了一种麻痹般的错愕。这样地道的大西部做派的事件，竟能发生在太平的英格兰——哪怕在桑斯特德宅这样一个与世隔绝的所在——简直叫人难以置信。

那是没完没了的一天——这种日子只有学校里才有。学校这门机构，比任何别的机构都更仰仗天气。每个小男孩清晨从床上爬起来，周身都携带着一份额度确定的淘气；这份淘气，他若想睡上健康孩子那种酣沉的觉，就得在熄灯前设法发泄个干净。正因如此，教师们巴望的才是夏季学期——课间的空当可以到露天里去消磨。对私立学校的助理教师来说，再没有比一群男孩在阳光下无害地宣泄毒液更悦目的光景了。

偏偏这一天，雪一清早就下了起来；学生们本巴不得出去在雪里一滚就是几个钟头，却被阿布尼先生的严令拦住了。没有哪位校长乐意眼看自己的学生冒伤风的险，而在那个当口，阿布尼先生在这个题目上的态度尤其斩钉截铁。麦金尼斯先生夜访学校之后那场萨图恩节式的狂欢，落下的结果是：三位勋爵、一位从男爵和一位可敬人士的次子，全都染上了凶猛的重感冒。不但如此，阿布尼先生自己也躺倒在床上，他那穿透力极强的男高音变成了喉音的嘎声，一双迎风流泪的眼睛观照着世界。因此，关于玩雪是否宜于充当男孩们的消遣，他的见解自然先入为主地偏颇了。

===Original===
The result was that Glossop and I had to try and keep order among a mob of small boys, none of whom had had any chance of working off his superfluous energy. How Glossop fared I can only imagine. Judging by the fact that I, who usually kept fair order without excessive effort, was almost overwhelmed, I should fancy he fared badly. His classroom was on the opposite side of the hall from mine, and at frequent intervals his voice would penetrate my door, raised to a frenzied fortissimo.

Little by little, however, we had won through the day, and the boys had subsided into comparative quiet over their evening preparation, when from outside the front door there sounded the purring of the engine of a large automobile. The bell rang.

I did not, I remember, pay much attention to this at the moment. I supposed that somebody from one of the big houses in the neighbourhood had called, or, taking the lateness of the hour into consideration, that a motoring party had come, as they did sometimes﻿—Sanstead House standing some miles from anywhere in the middle of an intricate system of byroads﻿—to inquire the way to Portsmouth or London. If my class had allowed me, I would have ignored the sound. But for them it supplied just that break in the monotony of things which they had needed. They welcomed it vociferously.

===Chinese===
结果便是，格拉索普和我只得设法在一群小男孩中间维持秩序，而这些孩子没有一个得到过发泄过剩精力的机会。格拉索普境况如何，我只能想象了。要知道，我平日不费吹灰之力便能维持相当的秩序，这回却几乎招架不住；由此推断，他的日子想必更不好过。他的教室在大厅另一头，与我的相对，他的嗓门每隔一阵便穿透我的房门，拔高到癫狂的最强音。

然而，一点一点地，我们总算把这一天熬了过去，男孩们也在晚间自习上方才归于相对的安静；就在这时，正门外传来一辆大型汽车引擎呜呜的低鸣。门铃响了。

我记得，当时我没大留意这个。我猜是邻近哪所大宅里的人来访；或者，考虑到时刻已晚，是哪一帮驾车出游的人——桑斯特德宅孤零零地立在一张盘根错节的小路网正中，离哪儿都有好几英里——跑来问去朴次茅斯（Portsmouth）或伦敦的路，这种事从前也不是没有过。倘若由得我做主，我本不愿理会这声音。可对孩子们来说，它恰好补上了他们渴盼已久的那一道单调里的裂缝。他们嚷嚷着欢迎它。

===Original===
A voice: “Sir, please, sir, there’s a motor outside.”

Myself (austerely): “I know there’s a motor outside. Get on with your work.”

Various voices: “Sir, have you ever ridden in a motor?”

“Sir, my father let me help drive our motor last Easter, sir.”

“Sir, who do you think it is?”

An isolated genius (imitating the engine): “Pr-prr! Pr-prr! Pr-prr!”

===Chinese===
一个声音：「先生，求您了先生，外头有一辆汽车。」

本人（严厉地）：「我知道外头有辆汽车。接着做功课。」

七嘴八舌的声音：「先生，您坐过汽车吗？」

「先生，去年复活节我爸爸还让我帮着开我们家的汽车来着，先生。」

「先生，您猜外头是谁？」

一位孤立的天才（模仿引擎声）：「噗——噜噜！噗——噜噜！噗——噜噜！」

===Original===
I was on the point of distributing bad marks (the schoolmaster’s standby) broadcast, when a curious sound checked me. It followed directly upon the opening of the front door. I heard White’s footsteps crossing the hall, then the click of the latch, and then﻿—a sound that I could not define. The closed door of the classroom deadened it, but for all that it was audible. It resembled the thud of a falling body, but I knew it could not be that, for in peaceful England butlers opening front doors did not fall with thuds.

My class, eager listeners, found fresh material in the sound for friendly conversation.

“Sir, what was that, sir?”

“Did you hear that, sir?”

“What do you think’s happened, sir?”

“Be quiet,” I shouted. “Will you be﻿—”

===Chinese===
我正要大发一通扣分（校长的看家法宝），忽地一声古怪的响动拦住了我。那声音紧跟着前门的打开而来。我听见怀特的脚步穿过大厅，接着是门闩咔哒一响，然后——一种我无法名状的声响。教室关着的门消了它的音，饶是如此，它仍听得见。它像一具躯体倒地的闷响，可我知道不会是那个，因为在太平的英格兰，开门的男管家是不会咚的一声倒下去的。

我班上那群竖着耳朵的听众，又从这声响里找到了亲切交谈的新鲜素材。

「先生，那是什么呀，先生？」

「您听见了吗，先生？」

「您猜出什么事了，先生？」

「安静，」我吼道。「你们能不能——」

===Original===
There was a quick footstep outside, the door flew open, and on the threshold stood a short, sturdy man in a motoring coat and cap. The upper part of his face was covered by a strip of white linen, with holes for the eyes, and there was a Browning pistol in his hand.

It is my belief that, if assistant-masters were allowed to wear white masks and carry automatic pistols, keeping order in a school would become child’s play. A silence such as no threat of bad marks had ever been able to produce fell instantaneously upon the classroom. Out of the corner of my eye, as I turned to face our visitor, I could see small boys goggling rapturously at this miraculous realization of all the dreams induced by juvenile adventure fiction. As far as I could ascertain, on subsequent inquiry, not one of them felt a tremor of fear. It was all too tremendously exciting for that. For their exclusive benefit an illustration from a weekly paper for boys had come to life, and they had no time to waste in being frightened.

As for me, I was dazed. Motor bandits may terrorize France, and desperadoes hold up trains in America, but this was peaceful England. The fact that Buck MacGinnis was at large in the neighbourhood did not make the thing any the less incredible. I had looked on my affair with Buck as a thing of the open air and the darkness. I had figured him lying in wait in lonely roads, possibly, even, lurking about the grounds; but in my most apprehensive moments I had not imagined him calling at the front door and holding me up with a revolver in my own classroom.

And yet it was the simple, even the obvious, thing for him to do. Given an automobile, success was certain. Sanstead House stood absolutely alone. There was not even a cottage within half a mile. A train broken down in the middle of the Bad Lands was not more cut off.

Consider, too, the peculiar helplessness of a school in such a case. A school lives on the confidence of parents, a nebulous foundation which the slightest breath can destroy. Everything connected with it must be done with exaggerated discretion. I do not suppose Mr. MacGinnis had thought the thing out in all its bearings, but he could not have made a sounder move if he had been a Napoleon. Where the owner of an ordinary country-house raided by masked men can raise the countryside in pursuit, a schoolmaster must do precisely the opposite. From his point of view, the fewer people that know of the affair the better. Parents are a jumpy race. A man may be the ideal schoolmaster, yet will a connection with melodrama damn him in the eyes of parents. They do not inquire. They are too panic-stricken for that. Golden-haired Willie may be receiving the finest education conceivable, yet if men with Browning pistols are familiar objects at his shrine of learning they will remove him. Fortunately for schoolmasters it is seldom that such visitors call upon them. Indeed, I imagine Mr. MacGinnis’s effort to have been the first of its kind.

I do not, as I say, suppose that Buck, whose forte was action rather than brain-work, had thought all this out. He had trusted to luck, and luck had stood by him. There would be no raising of the countryside in his case. On the contrary, I could see Mr. Abney becoming one of the busiest persons on record in his endeavour to hush the thing up and prevent it getting into the papers. The man with the pistol spoke. He sighted me﻿—I was standing with my back to the mantelpiece, parallel with the door﻿—made a sharp turn, and raised his weapon.

===Chinese===
门外一阵急促的脚步，房门砰地弹开，门槛上站着一个穿驾车大衣、戴驾车帽的矮壮汉子。他脸的上半部蒙着一条白布，布上开了两个眼洞，手里握着一支勃朗宁手枪（Browning pistol）。

我深信，倘若允许助理教师戴白面具、挎自动手枪，在学校里维持秩序便会沦为儿戏。一种连扣分的威吓都不曾造出过的寂静，霎时罩住了教室。我转身面对来客时，眼角余光里望得见小男孩们正瞪圆了眼、如痴如醉地盯着这一幕——少年历险小说滋养出的全部梦想，就这样奇迹般成了真。据我后来查问的结果，他们当中没有一个觉出过半丝恐惧的颤栗。这一切实在太够劲、太刺激了，恐惧根本排不上号。一本男孩周刊上的插图专为他们独享，活了过来，他们哪还有工夫浪费在害怕上。

至于我，我是懵的。汽车强盗尽可以在法国逞凶，亡命之徒尽可以在美国劫火车，可这里是太平的英格兰。巴克·麦金尼斯就在左近逍遥法外，这个事实也没让这件事减少半分难以置信。我一直把我与巴克的这桩公案看作露天里、黑暗中的事。我设想过他埋伏在僻静的路上，甚至，说不定在宅子场地上潜行；可哪怕在我最惴惴不安的时刻，我也不曾设想过他登门拜访，在我自己的教室里拿左轮枪抵住我。

然而这恰恰是他所能做的最简单、甚至最明白不过的一步。只要有辆汽车，成功便是十拿九稳。桑斯特德宅孤零零地立着，方圆半英里内连一间村舍都没有。一列抛锚在荒原地带（Bad Lands）正中的火车，也不见得比这里更与世隔绝。

再想想学校在这类事例中那种独特的束手无策。学校靠家长的信任过活，而那是一团星云状的地基，一丝最轻微的气息便能把它吹散。凡与学校沾边的事，都必须以夸张的分寸感去办。我并不以为麦金尼斯先生把这件事的方方面面都推演透了，可就算他是拿破仑（Napoleon）本人，也下不出比这更稳妥的一步棋。寻常乡间宅邸遭蒙面人袭击，宅主还能发动全村追捕；校长却必须反其道而行。在他的立场上，知情的人越少越好。家长是个一惊一乍的种族。一个男人纵然是十全十美的理想校长，只要与惊险情节剧沾上一点干系，便会在家长眼里万劫不复。他们不加查问。他们惊慌得顾不上查问。金发的威利（Willie）受的也许是想象得出最好的教育，可只要挎勃朗宁手枪的汉子成了他求学圣殿里的熟面孔，家长就会把他领走。托校长们的福，这类访客极少登他们的门。真的，我估摸麦金尼斯先生这一手，怕是开山的第一遭。

我说过，我不信巴克——他的长处在于动手，不在于动脑——把这一切都琢磨透了。他全凭运气，而运气也果然站在他一边。他这一案是不会有全村大追捕的了。恰恰相反，我料得见阿布尼先生就要跻身史上最忙碌的人物之列，为捂住这件事、不让它见报而奔走不歇。持枪那人开腔了。他把我锁定在准星里——我背靠壁炉架站着，与门平行——急一个转身，举起了武器。

===Original===
“Put ’em up, sport,” he said.

It was not the voice of Buck MacGinnis. I put my hands up.

“Say, which of dese is de Nugget?”

He half turned his head to the class.

“Which of youse kids is Ogden Ford?”

The class was beyond speech. The silence continued.

===Chinese===
「举起手来，小子。」他说。

那不是巴克·麦金尼斯的嗓音。我把双手举了起来。

「嘿，这些娃里头哪个是小金块？」

他朝教室半侧过头。

「你们这帮小崽子，哪个是奥格登·福特？」

全班已经说不出话来。寂静延续着。

===Original===
“Ogden Ford is not here,” I said.

Our visitor had not that simple faith which is so much better than Norman blood. He did not believe me. Without moving his head he gave a long whistle. Steps sounded outside. Another, short, sturdy form, entered the room.

“He ain’t in de odder room,” observed the newcomer. “I been rubberin’!”

This was friend Buck beyond question. I could have recognized his voice anywhere!

“Well dis guy,” said the man with the pistol, indicating me, “says he ain’t here. What’s de answer?”

“Why, it’s Sam!” said Buck. “Howdy, Sam? Pleased to see us, huh? We’re in on de ground floor, too, dis time, all right, all right.”

===Chinese===
「奥格登·福特不在这儿。」我说。

我们的访客没有那种远胜诺曼底血统的淳朴信念。他不信我的话。他头也不动，吹了一声长长的呼哨。门外响起脚步。又一条矮壮的身影进了屋。

「另一间屋里也没有他，」新来的那位说道。「我一路都扒着门缝瞧来着！」

这无疑是巴克老兄本人了。他的嗓音，我到哪儿都听得出来！

「这个家伙，」持枪人指了指我，说，「硬说他不在这儿。你说怎么办？」

「咦，这不是山姆吗！」巴克说。「好啊，山姆？见到咱们高兴吧，嗯？这回咱们也抢到了头一份，错不了，错不了。」

===Original===
His words had a marked effect on his colleague.

“Is dat Sam? Hell! Let me blow de head off’n him!” he said, with simple fervour; and, advancing a step nearer, he waved his disengaged fist truculently. In my role of Sam I had plainly made myself very unpopular. I have never heard so much emotion packed into a few words.

Buck, to my relief, opposed the motion. I thought this decent of Buck.

“Cheese it,” he said curtly.

The other cheesed it. The operation took the form of lowering the fist. The pistol he kept in position.

Mr. MacGinnis resumed the conduct of affairs.

===Chinese===
他的话在同事身上产生了立竿见影的效果。

「他是山姆？见鬼！让我把他的脑袋掀下来！」他说，语气质朴而热烈；说着又逼近一步，挑衅地挥了挥空着的那只拳头。看来，在我扮演的山姆这个角色上，我显然把自己弄得极不受人待见。这么多情绪塞进寥寥几个词里，我还从来没听见过。

令我宽慰的是，巴克否决了这项动议。我觉得巴克这一点够意思。

「住手。」他简短地说。

那一位住了手。这一操作采取了放下拳头的形式。枪口则原地架着。

麦金尼斯先生重新主持大局。

===Original===
“Now den, Sam,” he said, “come across! Where’s de Nugget?”

“My name is not Sam,” I said. “May I put my hands down?”

“Yep, if you want the top of your damn head blown off.”

Such was not my desire. I kept them up.

“Now den, you Sam,” said Mr. MacGinnis again, “we ain’t got time to burn. Out with it. Where’s dat Nugget?”

Some reply was obviously required. It was useless to keep protesting that I was not Sam.

===Chinese===
「好了，山姆，」他说，「交出来吧！小金块在哪儿？」

「我不姓山姆，」我说。「我可以把手放下来吗？」

「可以啊，只要你不怕天灵盖给轰掉。」

我不存这份心思。便仍旧举着。

「行了，你这名叫山姆的，」麦金尼斯先生又说，「我们没工夫穷耗。痛快说。那小金块在哪儿？」

显然总得回上一句。再一口咬定自己不姓山姆，也无济于事了。

===Original===
“At this time in the evening he is generally working with Mr. Glossop.”

“Who’s Glossop? Dat dough-faced dub in de room over dere?”

“Exactly. You have described him perfectly.”

“Well, he ain’t dere. I bin rubberin’. Aw, quit yer foolin’, Sam, where is he?”

“I couldn’t tell you just where he is at the present moment,” I said precisely.

“Ahr chee! Let me swot him one!” begged the man with the pistol; a most unlovable person. I could never have made a friend of him.

===Chinese===
「这个时辰，他一般在格拉索普先生那儿用功。」

「格拉索普是谁？那边屋里那个面团脸的笨蛋？」

「正是。你把他形容得十全十美。」

「可他不在那儿。我一直扒着门缝瞧来着。哦，少装蒜了山姆，他到底在哪儿？」

「眼下一刻他究竟在哪儿，我无可奉告。」我一字一板地说。

「哎呀喂！让我给他一拳！」持枪人恳求道——一个顶不讨人喜欢的人。我跟他八辈子也做不成朋友。

===Original===
“Cheese it, you!” said Mr. MacGinnis.

The other cheesed it once more, regretfully.

“You got him hidden away somewheres, Sam,” said Mr. MacGinnis. “You can’t fool me. I’m com’ t’roo dis joint wit a fine-tooth comb till I find him.”

“By all means,” I said. “Don’t let me stop you.”

“You? You’re coming wit me.”

“If you wish it. I shall be delighted.”

===Chinese===
「住手，你！」麦金尼斯先生说。

那一位怏怏地又住了手。

「你把他藏在哪个旮旯了，山姆，」麦金尼斯先生说。「你糊弄不了我。我要拿一把细篦子，把这地界从头到尾篦一遍，非把他找出来不可。」

「请便，」我说。「别让我耽搁了你。」

「你？你跟我一块儿走。」

「悉听尊便。我不胜荣幸。」

===Original===
“An’ cut out dat dam’ sissy way of talking, you rummy,” bellowed Buck, with a sudden lapse into ferocity. “Spiel like a regular guy! Standin’ dere, pullin’ dat dude stuff on me! Cut it out!”

“Say, why *mayn’t* I hand him one?” demanded the pistol-bearer pathetically. “What’s your kick against pushin’ his face in?”

I thought the question in poor taste. Buck ignored it.

“Gimme dat canister,” he said, taking the Browning pistol from him. “Now den, Sam, are youse goin’ to be good, and come across, or ain’t you﻿—which?”

“I’d be delighted to do anything you wished, Mr. MacGinnis,” I said, “but﻿—”

“Aw, hire a hall!” said Buck disgustedly. “Step lively, den, an’ we’ll go t’roo de joint. I t’ought youse ’ud have had more sense, Sam, dan to play dis fool game when you know you’re beat. You﻿—”

===Chinese===
「还有，把你那套该死的娘娘腔收起来，你这醉鬼，」巴克吼道，忽而又露出凶相。「说话像个正经汉子！杵在那儿，拿公子哥儿的做派寒碜我！给我收起来！」

「我说，我怎么*就*不能给他一下？」持枪人悲愤地质问道。「把他那张脸捣烂，你有什么不乐意的？」

我觉得这个问题有失品味。巴克未予理会。

「把那管炮筒子给我，」他说着，从他手里接过勃朗宁手枪。「好了山姆，你是乖乖听话、乖乖交人呢，还是不听——你挑一样？」

「凡是你愿意的事，我无不乐于效劳，麦金尼斯先生，」我说，「不过——」

「哦，租个讲堂讲演去吧！」巴克嫌恶地说。「那脚下就麻利点，咱们把这地界走一遍。我还当你多少有点脑子呢，山姆，明知输定了，还玩这套傻把戏。你——」

===Original===
Shooting pains in my shoulders caused me to interrupt him.

“One moment,” I said. “I’m going to put my hands down. I’m getting cramp.”

“I’ll blow a hole in you if you do!”

“Just as you please. But I’m not armed.”

“Lefty,” he said to the other man, “feel around to see if he’s carryin’ anyt’ing.”

Lefty advanced and began to tap me scientifically in the neighbourhood of my pockets. He grunted morosely the while. I suppose, at this close range, the temptation to “hand me one” was almost more than he could bear.

===Chinese===
肩膀上一阵阵针扎似的剧痛，叫我打断了他。

「稍等，」我说。「我要把手放下来了。我抽筋了。」

「你敢放，我就在你身上凿个窟窿！」

「悉听尊便。不过我可没带家伙。」

「左撇子（Lefty），」他对另一人说，「上上下下摸摸，看他身上带没带东西。」

左撇子走上前来，开始在我口袋一带以科学的手法对我拍拍打打。他一边拍一边阴沉地哼哼。我估摸着，离得这么近，「给他一下」的诱惑他几乎按捺不住了。

===Original===
“He ain’t got no gun,” he announced gloomily.

“Den youse can put ’em down,” said Mr. MacGinnis.

“Thanks,” I said.

“Lefty, youse stay here and look after dese kids. Get a move on, Sam.”

We left the room, a little procession of two, myself leading, Buck in my immediate rear administering occasional cautionary prods with the faithful “canister.”

===Chinese===
「他没带枪。」他郁郁地宣布。

「那你可以把手放下了。」麦金尼斯先生说。

「多谢。」我说。

「左撇子，你留在这儿看住这帮小崽子。快点走，山姆。」

我们出了屋，一支两人的小队伍：我打头，巴克紧贴在我身后，不时用那管忠心耿耿的「炮筒子」发出几记告诫性的捅戳。

### II / II
===Original===
The first thing that met my eyes as we entered the hall was the body of a man lying by the front door. The light of the lamp fell on his face and I saw that it was White. His hands and feet were tied. As I looked at him, he moved, as if straining against his bonds, and I was conscious of a feeling of relief. That sound that had reached me in the classroom, that thud of a falling body, had become, in the light of what had happened later, very sinister. It was good to know that he was still alive. I gathered﻿—correctly, as I discovered subsequently﻿—that in his case the sandbag had been utilized. He had been struck down and stunned the instant he opened the door.

There was a masked man leaning against the wall by Glossop’s classroom. He was short and sturdy. The Buck MacGinnis gang seemed to have been turned out on a pattern. Externally, they might all have been twins. This man, to give him a semblance of individuality, had a ragged red moustache. He was smoking a cigar with the air of the warrior taking his rest.

“Hello!” he said, as we appeared. He jerked a thumb towards the classroom. “I’ve locked dem in. What’s doin’, Buck?” he asked, indicating me with a languid nod.

===Chinese===
我们进了大厅，扑进我眼帘的头一样东西，是前门边躺着的一具躯体。灯光落在他脸上，我看清那是怀特。他手脚都被捆着。我正看着他，他动了动，像是在挣他的绳索，我心头顿觉一宽。先前传到我教室里的那声响、那躯体倒地的闷响，如今对照后来发生的一切，已变得十分凶险。知道他还活着，总归是件好事。我推断——后来发现推断得不错——他这一案用的是沙袋。他开门的一瞬，便被打翻击晕了。

格拉索普的教室旁边，靠墙立着一个蒙面人。矮个子，壮实。巴克·麦金尼斯帮看来是一个模子里倒出来的。论外表，他们个个都像双胞胎。这一位，为着给自己添一点个性的影子，蓄了一部蓬乱的红胡子。他正抽着雪茄，一副休憩武人的神气。

「喂！」我们一露面，他说。他朝教室竖了竖大拇指。「我把他们锁里头了。这是唱的哪出，巴克？」他朝我懒懒一点头，问道。

===Original===
“We’re going t’roo de joint,” explained Mr. MacGinnis. “De kid ain’t in dere. Hump yourself, Sam!”

His colleague’s languor disappeared with magic swiftness.

“Sam! Is dat Sam? Here, let me beat de block off’n him!”

Few points in this episode struck me as more remarkable than the similarity of taste which prevailed, as concerned myself, among the members of Mr. MacGinnis’s gang. Men, doubtless of varying opinions on other subjects, on this one point they were unanimous. They all wanted to assault me.

Buck, however, had other uses for me. For the present, I was necessary as a guide, and my value as such would be impaired were the block to be beaten off me. Though feeling no friendlier towards me than did his assistants, he declined to allow sentiment to interfere with business. He concentrated his attention on the upward journey with all the earnestness of the young gentleman who carried the banner with the strange device in the poem.

Briefly requesting his ally to cheese it﻿—which he did﻿—he urged me on with the nozzle of the pistol. The red-moustached man sank back against the wall again with an air of dejection, sucking his cigar now like one who has had disappointments in life, while we passed on up the stairs and began to draw the rooms on the first floor.

===Chinese===
「我们要把这地界搜一遍，」麦金尼斯先生解释道。「小崽子不在那屋里。挪你的屁股，山姆！」

他同事的那股懒散，以魔法般的速度消失了。

「山姆！他是山姆？来，让我把这厮的脑袋瓜敲下来！」

这段插曲里，几乎没有哪一点比这更叫我瞩目了：麦金尼斯先生帮里的诸位成员，论到我本人，趣味竟然空前一致。这些人旁的题目上想必见仁见智，独独在这一个问题上全票通过。他们个个都想揍我。

不过，巴克对我另有用处。眼下我还少不得充当向导，脑袋瓜一旦被敲下来，我这份利用价值便要折损。他对我虽不比他那两位帮手更友好半分，却不肯让感情妨碍正事。他一门心思专注在上行的征途上，那股认真劲儿，直追诗里那位扛着奇异铭文旗帜的少年。

他简短地请他的盟友住手——对方照办了——然后用枪口催我上路。红胡子汉子重新颓然靠回墙上，这会儿嘬着雪茄，活像个生平屡遭失意的人；我们则继续拾级而上，开始清查二楼各屋。

===Original===
These consisted of Mr. Abney’s study and two dormitories. The study was empty, and the only occupants of the dormitories were the three boys who had been stricken down with colds on the occasion of Mr. MacGinnis’s last visit. They squeaked with surprise at the sight of the assistant-master in such questionable company.

Buck eyed them disappointedly. I waited with something of the feelings of a drummer taking a buyer round the sample room.

“Get on,” said Buck.

“Won’t one of those do?”

“Hump yourself, Sam.”

===Chinese===
这几间包括阿布尼先生的书房和两间宿舍。书房是空的，宿舍里仅有的住户，是麦金尼斯先生上次来访时被感冒击倒的那三个男孩。他们看见助理教师混在这样一群来路不明的伙伴中间，惊得吱吱直叫。

巴克失望地打量他们。我立在一旁，心情颇似领着买主参观样品间的推销员。

「接着走。」巴克说。

「这几位里就没有一个中用的？」

「挪你的屁股，山姆。」

===Original===
“Call me Sammy,” I urged. “We’re old friends now.”

“Don’t get fresh,” he said austerely. And we moved on.

The top floor was even more deserted than the first. There was no one in the dormitories. The only other room was Mr. Abney’s; and, as we came opposite it, a sneeze from within told of the sufferings of its occupant.

The sound stirred Buck to his depths. He “pointed” at the door like a smell-dog.

“Who’s in dere?” he demanded.

===Chinese===
「叫我山米（Sammy）吧，」我劝道。「咱们如今是老朋友了。」

「别放肆，」他正色说。我们接着往前走。

顶楼比二楼更是人迹寥寥。宿舍里空无一人。剩下唯一的房间是阿布尼先生的；我们刚走到它对面，屋里一声喷嚏，道出了房中人的苦楚。

这声响激荡了巴克的灵魂深处。他像条闻味儿的猎犬，直挺挺朝那扇门「定了点」。

「里头是谁？」他质问。

===Original===
“Only Mr. Abney. Better not disturb him. He has a bad cold.”

He placed a wrong construction on my solicitude for my employer. His manner became excited.

“Open dat door, you,” he cried.

“It’ll give him a nasty shock.”

“G’wan! Open it!”

No one who is digging a Browning pistol into the small of my back will ever find me disobliging. I opened the door﻿—knocking first, as a mild concession to the conventions﻿—and the procession passed in.

===Chinese===
「只有阿布尼先生。最好别打扰他。他得了重感冒。」

他把我对东家的这份关怀错误地解读了。他的态度兴奋起来。

「把门打开，你。」他叫道。

「那会把他吓一大跳的。」

「少废话！开门！」

谁拿勃朗宁手枪捅着我的后腰，谁就永远别想发现我有半点不配合。我打开了门——先敲了敲门，算是向礼俗做出的温和让步——随后这支队伍开了进去。

===Original===
My stricken employer was lying on his back, staring at the ceiling, and our entrance did not at first cause him to change this position.

“Yes?” he said thickly, and disappeared beneath a huge pocket-handkerchief. Muffled sounds, as of distant explosions of dynamite, together with earthquake shudderings of the bedclothes, told of another sneezing-fit.

“I’m sorry to disturb you,” I began, when Buck, ever the man of action, with a scorn of palaver, strode past me, and, having prodded with the pistol that part of the bedclothes beneath which a rough calculation suggested that Mr. Abney’s lower ribs were concealed, uttered the one word, “Sa-a-ay!”

Mr. Abney sat up like a jack-in-the-box. One might almost say that he shot up. And then he saw Buck.

I cannot even faintly imagine what were Mr. Abney’s emotions at that moment. He was a man who, from boyhood up, had led a quiet and regular life. Things like Buck had appeared to him hitherto, if they appeared at all, only in dreams after injudicious suppers. Even in the ordinary costume of the Bowery gentleman, without such adventitious extras as masks and pistols, Buck was no beauty. With that hideous strip of dingy white linen on his face, he was a walking nightmare.

===Chinese===
我那位染恙的东家正仰面躺着，两眼瞪着天花板；我们进来，起初也没能叫他变换这个姿势。

「什么事？」他含混地说，随即没入一条巨大的手帕之下。一阵闷响，好似远方炸药的连番爆炸，伴着被褥的地震般颤动，报告了又一轮喷嚏的发作。

「抱歉打扰您，」我刚起了个头，巴克——他永远是行动家，最看不起空谈寒暄——已经大步越过了我，拿枪往被褥上捅了一记，捅的位置，据粗略测算，底下应当正藏着阿布尼先生的下肋骨，随即吐出一个词：「听——着——！」

阿布尼先生像盒中弹出的弹簧玩偶一样坐了起来。几乎可以说，他是射起来的。然后他看见了巴克。

阿布尼先生那一刻的心情，我连雾里看花都想象不出。他这个人，从少年时代起过的就是恬静规律的日子。巴克这一类东西，此前若曾在他的世界里露过面，也只在吃错了晚餐之后的梦里。即便不添面具手枪这类身外的行头，只穿鲍厄里街（Bowery）绅士的寻常装束，巴克也谈不上仪表堂堂；脸上蒙着那条昏暗的白布之后，他就是一场会走路的噩梦。

===Original===
Mr. Abney’s eyebrows had risen and his jaw had fallen to their uttermost limits. His hair, disturbed by contact with the pillow, gave the impression of standing on end. His eyes seemed to bulge like a snail’s. He stared at Buck, fascinated.

“Say, you, quit rubberin’. Youse ain’t in a dime museum. Where’s dat Ford kid, huh?”

I have set down all Mr. MacGinnis’s remarks as if they had been uttered in a bell-like voice with a clear and crisp enunciation; but, in doing so, I have flattered him. In reality, his mode of speech suggested that he had something large and unwieldy permanently stuck in his mouth; and it was not easy for a stranger to follow him. Mr. Abney signally failed to do so. He continued to gape helplessly till the tension was broken by a sneeze.

One cannot interrogate a sneezing man with any satisfaction to oneself. Buck stood by the bedside in moody silence, waiting for the paroxysm to spend itself.

I, meanwhile, had remained where I stood, close to the door. And, as I waited for Mr. Abney to finish sneezing, for the first time since Buck’s colleague Lefty had entered the classroom the idea of action occurred to me. Until this moment, I suppose, the strangeness and unexpectedness of these happenings had numbed my brain. To precede Buck meekly upstairs and to wait with equal meekness while he interviewed Mr. Abney had seemed the only course open to me. To one whose life has lain apart from such things, the hypnotic influence of a Browning pistol is irresistible.

===Chinese===
阿布尼先生的眉毛升到了顶，下巴落到了底，双双抵达各自的极限。他的头发因与枕头打过交道而纷乱，给人根根倒竖的印象。他的眼睛鼓得像蜗牛。他目不转睛地盯着巴克，魂都被勾住了。

「说你呢，别瞪眼了。你这不是在十美分博物馆（dime museum）里展览。福特家那小崽子在哪儿，嗯？」

我记录麦金尼斯先生的全部言论时，就当它们是以一种钟鸣般的嗓音、清脆利落的吐字说出来的；但这么写，其实是抬举了他。实际上，他说话的方式令人觉得他嘴里永久性地堵着一件又大又不灵便的东西；生人要听懂他，颇为不易。阿布尼先生便全然没能听懂。他继续无助地张着嘴，直到一阵喷嚏打破了僵局。

审问一个正打喷嚏的人，是审不出什么快意的。巴克闷闷地立在床边，一言不发，等这阵发作自行耗尽。

这期间，我一直站在原地，紧挨着门。等着阿布尼先生打完喷嚏的当口，自巴克的同事左撇子闯进教室以来，「行动」这个念头头一回复萌了。在这之前，我想，这一连串变故的离奇与猝不及防已经麻醉了我的脑子。乖乖地在巴克前头上楼，再同样乖乖地立等他会见阿布尼先生，在我看来是仅有的路。对一个平生与这类事物绝缘的人，勃朗宁手枪的催眠之力是不可抗拒的。

===Original===
But now, freed temporarily from this influence, I began to think; and, my mind making up for its previous inaction by working with unwonted swiftness, I formed a plan of action at once.

It was simple, but I had an idea that it would be effective. My strength lay in my acquaintance with the geography of Sanstead House and Buck’s ignorance of it. Let me but get an adequate start, and he might find pursuit vain. It was this start which I saw my way to achieving.

To Buck it had not yet occurred that it was a tactical error to leave me between the door and himself. I supposed he relied too implicitly on the mesmeric pistol. He was not even looking at me.

The next moment my fingers were on the switch of the electric light, and the room was in darkness.

There was a chair by the door. I seized it and swung it into the space between us. Then, springing back, I banged the door and ran.

===Chinese===
可现在，暂且脱开了这股魔力，我开始思考了；我的头脑为先前的怠工补课，运转得异乎寻常地快，一套行动方案应声而成。

方案简单，但我预感它会奏效。我的优势在于：我熟稔桑斯特德宅的地理，而巴克对此一无所知。只要让我抢出足够的先手，他的追捕尽可以徒劳。而这个先手，我已看出门道去抢了。

巴克还不曾想到，把我留在他与门之间是个战术失误。我猜，他对那支催眠的枪依赖得太彻底了。他甚至连看都没看我一眼。

下一刻，我的手指已经搭上电灯的开关，房间坠入黑暗。

门边有一把椅子。我抄起它，抡进我俩之间的空当。随即向后一跃，摔上门，撒腿就跑。

===Original===
I did not run without a goal in view. My objective was the study. This, as I have explained, was on the first floor. Its window looked out on to a strip of lawn at the side of the house ending in a shrubbery. The drop would not be pleasant, but I seemed to remember a waterspout that ran up the wall close to the window, and, in any case, I was not in a position to be deterred by the prospect of a bruise or two. I had not failed to realize that my position was one of extreme peril. When Buck, concluding the tour of the house, found that the Little Nugget was not there﻿—as I had reason to know that he would﻿—there was no room for doubt that he would withdraw the protection which he had extended to me up to the present in my capacity of guide. On me the disappointed fury of the raiders would fall. No prudent consideration for their own safety would restrain them. If ever the future was revealed to man, I saw mine. My only chance was to get out into the grounds, where the darkness would make pursuit an impossibility.

It was an affair which must be settled one way or the other in a few seconds, and I calculated that it would take Buck just those few seconds to win his way past the chair and find the door-handle.

I was right. Just as I reached the study, the door of the bedroom flew open, and the house rang with shouts and the noise of feet on the uncarpeted landing. From the hall below came answering shouts, but with an interrogatory note in them. The assistants were willing, but puzzled. They did not like to leave their posts without specific instructions, and Buck, shouting as he clattered over the bare boards, was unintelligible.

I was in the study, the door locked behind me, before they could arrive at an understanding. I sprang to the window.

The handle rattled. Voices shouted. A panel splintered beneath a kick, and the door shook on its hinges.

And then, for the first time, I think, in my life, panic gripped me, the sheer, blind fear which destroys the reason. It swept over me in a wave, that numbing terror which comes to one in dreams. Indeed, the thing had become dreamlike. I seemed to be standing outside myself, looking on at myself, watching myself heave and strain with bruised fingers at a window that would not open.

===Chinese===
我这一跑并非无的放矢。我的目标是书房。如前所述，书房在二楼。它的窗子朝向宅子侧面一条狭长的草坪，草坪尽头是一片灌木林。往下跳的滋味不会好受，但我仿佛记得，窗边贴墙立着一根水落管；何况无论如何，区区两三块淤青的前景，已经吓不住此刻的我。我并非没有看清，我的处境凶险至极。等巴克巡完宅子，发现小金块并不在其中——而我有十足的理由知道必然如此——那么毫无疑问，他此前以向导身份发给我的那份保护，就要收回去了。袭击者们的失望之怒，将尽数落在我头上。到那时，任何对自身安全的审慎考虑都拦不住他们。倘若未来曾向人类泄露过，我这一份是看得清清楚楚的了。我唯一的机会，是跑到宅外场地上去，那儿的夜色会把追捕变成一件不可能的事。

这是一桩必须在几秒钟之内见分晓的事；我测算下来，巴克从椅子旁边杀出来、摸到门把手，恰好也需要这几秒。

我算得不错。我刚到书房，卧室的门便砰然弹开，满宅回荡着喊声和光秃秃楼梯平台上的脚步声。楼下大厅里应起了喊声，却带着疑问的调子。帮手们有心效劳，只是摸不着头脑。没有明令，他们不愿擅离岗位，而巴克一边喊一边在秃木板上噼啪猛跑，喊的什么没人听得懂。

不等他们达成谅解，我已进了书房，反锁上门。我扑向窗子。

把手咔咔作响。人声呐喊。一块门板在一脚之下迸裂，门在铰链上摇晃。

接着，我想，是我生平头一遭，恐慌攫住了我——那种纯粹的、盲目的、摧毁理性的恐惧。它像一道浪从我心头扫过，就是梦里来袭的那种麻木的恐怖。真的，事情已经变得像梦了。我仿佛站在自己身外，旁观着我自己，看着自己用蹭破的手指，又推又撬地对付一扇死活不肯开的窗。

### III / III
===Original===
The armchair critic, reviewing a situation calmly and at his ease, is apt to make too small allowances for the effect of hurry and excitement on the human mind. He is cool and detached. He sees exactly what ought to have been done, and by what simple means catastrophe might have been averted.

He would have made short work of my present difficulty, I feel certain. It was ridiculously simple. But I had lost my head, and had ceased for the moment to be a reasoning creature. In the end, indeed, it was no presence of mind but pure good luck which saved me. Just as the door, which had held out gallantly, gave way beneath the attack from outside, my fingers, slipping, struck against the catch of the window, and I understood why I had failed to raise it.

I snapped the catch back, and flung up the sash. An icy wind swept into the room, bearing particles of snow. I scrambled on to the windowsill, and a crash from behind me told of the falling of the door.

The packed snow on the sill was drenching my knees as I worked my way out and prepared to drop. There was a deafening explosion inside the room, and simultaneously something seared my shoulder like a hot iron. I cried out with the pain of it, and, losing my balance, fell from the sill.

===Chinese===
安乐椅上的批评家，安闲从容地复盘一个局面时，往往把仓促与激动对人心的效力估得过低。他冷静，超然。他看得一清二楚：当初本该怎么做，凭多么简单的法子，那场大祸本可以避开。

我眼前的这一难题，若交到他手里，三下五除二便解决了，这一点我深信不疑。它简单得可笑。可我已经慌了神，那一刻早已不是理性的动物了。到头来，救了我的其实不是什么临危不乱，而是纯粹的运气。恰在那扇久经苦战、无愧荣誉的门被外面的攻势轰开的一刻，我滑落的手指撞上了窗子的搭扣，我这才明白，方才为什么推它不开。

我啪地打开搭扣，猛推起窗扇。一股寒风卷着雪粒扫进屋来。我手脚并用地爬上窗台，身后一声轰响，宣告了那扇门的阵亡。

窗台上积实的雪浸透我的膝盖，我正一点点挪出身子、准备往下跳，屋里一声震耳的爆响，同时有什么东西像烧红的烙铁一样灼过我的肩膀。我痛得叫出声来，身体一失衡，从窗台上栽了下去。

===Original===
There was, fortunately for me, a laurel bush immediately below the window, or I should have been undone. I fell into it, all arms and legs, in a way which would have meant broken bones if I had struck the hard turf. I was on my feet in an instant, shaken and scratched and, incidentally, in a worse temper than ever in my life before. The idea of flight, which had obsessed me a moment before, to the exclusion of all other mundane affairs, had vanished absolutely. I was full of fight, I might say overflowing with it. I remember standing there, with the snow trickling in chilly rivulets down my face and neck, and shaking my fist at the window. Two of my pursuers were leaning out of it, while a third dodged behind them, like a small man on the outskirts of a crowd. So far from being thankful for my escape, I was conscious only of a feeling of regret that there was no immediate way of getting at them.

They made no move towards travelling the quick but trying route which had commended itself to me. They seemed to be waiting for something to happen. It was not long before I was made aware of what this something was. From the direction of the front door came the sound of one running. A sudden diminution of the noise of his feet told me that he had left the gravel and was on the turf. I drew back a pace or two and waited.

It was pitch dark, and I had no fear that I should be seen. I was standing well outside the light from the window.

The man stopped just in front of me. A short parley followed.

===Chinese===
算我走运，窗子正下方有一丛月桂树，不然我可就完了。我手脚乱舞地栽了进去，若是摔在硬草皮上，这一下非断几根骨头不可。我转眼便跳了起来，浑身发抖，满身划伤，而且——顺带一提——脾气之坏，平生未曾有过。方才还盘踞我心头、排除一切俗务的那个「逃跑」的念头，此刻荡然无存。我满腔都是斗志，简直可以说斗志四溢。我记得自己站在那儿，雪水顺着脸和脖子往下淌成一道道冰凉的小溪，我朝那扇窗挥舞拳头。追兵中有两个正探身窗外，第三个躲在他们背后，活像人群外围的小个子。我不但没有为自己脱险而庆幸，心里只剩一股遗憾：一时想不出立刻冲上去揍他们的路径。

他们无意走那条路——那条路快则快矣，滋味却不好受，方才深得我心。他们似乎在等着什么发生。没等多久，我便知道了等的是什么。正门方向传来一个人奔跑的声响。他的脚步声陡然一轻，我知道他已离开砾石道，踏上了草皮。我退后一两步，静候。

四下漆黑，我不怕被人看见。我站的地方远离窗内射出的光。

那人在我面前站住了。接着是一番短短的交谈。

===Original===
“Can’tja see him?”

The voice was not Buck’s. It was Buck who answered. And when I realized that this man in front of me, within easy reach, on whose back I was shortly about to spring, and whose neck I proposed, under Providence, to twist into the shape of a corkscrew, was no mere underling, but Mr. MacGinnis himself, I was filled with a joy which I found it hard to contain in silence.

Looking back, I am a little sorry for Mr. MacGinnis. He was not a good man. His mode of speech was not pleasant, and his manners were worse than his speech. But, though he undoubtedly deserved all that was coming to him, it was nevertheless bad luck for him to be standing just there at just that moment. The reactions after my panic, added to the pain of my shoulder, the scratches on my face, and the general misery of being wet and cold, had given me a reckless fury and a determination to do somebody, whoever happened to come along, grievous bodily hurt, such as seldom invades the bosoms of the normally peaceful. To put it crisply, I was fighting mad, and I looked on Buck as something sent by Heaven.

He had got as far, in his reply, as “Naw, I can’t﻿—” when I sprang.

===Chinese===
「你瞧不见他吗？」

这嗓音不是巴克的。应声的才是巴克。待我意识到，眼前这个人——就在我伸手可及之处、我片刻之后便要扑上他的背、我打算蒙上天保佑把他的脖子拧成一把开塞钻的人——不是什么小喽啰，而是麦金尼斯先生本人，我心头顿时涨满一股欢喜，直觉得难以默不作声地按捺住。

如今回想，我对麦金尼斯先生倒有点过意不去。他不是个好人。他说话的方式不讨人喜欢，举止比谈吐更糟。可是，尽管那顿教训他无疑受之无愧，偏偏站在那个地点、赶上那个时辰，于他也实在是运气太坏。恐慌过后的反应，加上肩膀的疼痛、脸上的划伤、又湿又冷的全面凄惶，已把我浇铸成一腔无所顾忌的狂怒，外加一桩铁了心的打算：不管撞上来的是谁，都要叫他受一顿结结实实的外伤——这股狠劲，轻易不进平和百姓的胸膛。说得干脆些，我已杀红了眼，而我把巴克看作上苍派来的礼物。

他答话答到「呸，我瞧不——」时，我扑了上去。

===Original===
I have read of the spring of the jaguar, and I have seen some very creditable flying-tackles made on the football field. My leap combined the outstanding qualities of both. I connected with Mr. MacGinnis in the region of the waist, and the howl he gave as we crashed to the ground was music to my ears.

But how true is the old Roman saying, *Surgit amari aliquid*. Our pleasures are never perfect. There is always something. In the programme which I had hastily mapped out, the upsetting of Mr. MacGinnis was but a small item, a mere preliminary. There were a number of things which I had wished to do to him, once upset. But it was not to be. Even as I reached for his throat I perceived that the light of the window was undergoing an eclipse. A compact form had wriggled out on to the sill, as I had done, and I heard the grating of his shoes on the wall as he lowered himself for the drop.

There is a moment when the pleasantest functions must come to an end. I was loath to part from Mr. MacGinnis just when I was beginning, as it were, to do myself justice; but it was unavoidable. In another moment his ally would descend upon us, like some Homeric god swooping from a cloud, and I was not prepared to continue the battle against odds.

I disengaged myself﻿—Mr. MacGinnis strangely quiescent during the process﻿—and was on my feet in the safety of the darkness just as the reinforcement touched earth. This time I did not wait. My hunger for fight had been appeased to some extent by my brush with Buck, and I was satisfied to have achieved safety with honour.

===Chinese===
美洲豹的扑击我在书上读过，橄榄球场上几记拿得出手的飞身擒抱我也亲眼见过。我这一跃，兼收二者之长。我在腰部一带与麦金尼斯先生结结实实接上了头，我们轰然倒地时他发出的那声嚎叫，在我听来不啻仙乐。

然而那句古罗马老话说得多么真切：*Surgit amari aliquid*（欢愉之中，苦味自生）。我们的快乐从来没有圆满的。总有点什么。在我仓促拟定的节目单上，掀翻麦金尼斯先生不过是小小一项，区区一个序幕。一旦掀翻，我还有一长串想在他身上办的事。可天不遂人愿。我的手刚够向他的喉咙，我便察觉窗内的灯光正经历一场日食。一条紧凑的身影已经扭上窗台，一如方才的我，我听得见他往下滑、预备往下跳时鞋底蹭墙的声响。

再愉快的职责，也有必须收场的时刻。我与麦金尼斯先生才刚要——不妨这么说——施展开自己的身手，便要分手，实在依依难舍；然而势不得已。再过片刻，他的盟友便要扑到我们头上，赛过荷马史诗里自云端俯冲而下的天神，而我无意以寡敌众，把战斗进行下去。

我抽身而起——这过程中麦金尼斯先生出奇地安静——恰在增援双脚触地的一刻，我已立在黑暗的庇护之下。这一回我不等了。与巴克这一场遭遇，多少餍足了我的斗瘾，而能带着荣誉安然脱身，我也算心满意足。

===Original===
Making a wide detour I crossed the drive and worked my way through the bushes to within a few yards of where the automobile stood, filling the night with the soft purring of its engines. I was interested to see what would be the enemy’s next move. It was improbable that they would attempt to draw the grounds in search of me. I imagined that they would recognize failure and retire whence they had come.

I was right. I had not been watching long, before a little group advanced into the light of the automobile’s lamps. There were four of them. Three were walking, the fourth, cursing with the vigour and breadth that marks the expert, lying on their arms, of which they had made something resembling a stretcher.

The driver of the car, who had been sitting woodenly in his seat, turned at the sound.

“Ja get him?” he inquired.

“Get nothing!” replied one of the three moodily. “De Nugget ain’t dere, an’ we was chasin’ Sam to fix him, an’ he laid for us, an’ what he did to Buck was plenty.”

===Chinese===
我兜了个大圈子，穿过车道，又在灌木丛里摸索着挪到离汽车几步远的地方。那车正以引擎轻柔的低鸣灌满夜色。我颇有兴致，想看看敌人下一步如何动作。他们再想拉网搜遍场地来抓我，已不大可能。我料想他们会承认失败，从哪儿来回哪儿去。

我料对了。没守望多久，一小队人马便走进了车灯的光圈。一共四个。三个步行，第四个躺在他们交叠的手臂上——那几条手臂已被捆扎成一副担架似的东西——正以行家特有的气魄与广度破口大骂。

汽车司机原本木然坐在座位上，闻声转过脸来。

「逮着了没？」他问。

「逮个屁！」三人中一个没好气地答道。「小金块不在那儿，我们去追山姆想收拾他，他倒埋伏好了候着咱们，他给巴克那一顿，可真够受的。」

===Original===
They placed their valuable burden in the tonneau, where he lay repeating himself, and two of them climbed in after him. The third seated himself beside the driver.

“Buck’s leg’s broke,” he announced.

“Hell!” said the chauffeur.

No young actor, receiving his first round of applause, could have felt a keener thrill of gratification than I did at those words. Life may have nobler triumphs than the breaking of a kidnapper’s leg, but I did not think so then. It was with an effort that I stopped myself from cheering.

“Let her go,” said the man in the front seat.

The purring rose to a roar. The car turned and began to move with increasing speed down the drive. Its drone grew fainter, and ceased. I brushed the snow from my coat and walked to the front door.

===Chinese===
他们把那件贵重的包袱安置进后座，他在那儿躺定，翻来覆去地重复着那套话，随后两人爬上去坐在他旁边。第三个在司机身旁落座。

「巴克的腿断了。」他宣布。

「见鬼！」司机说。

青年演员头一回博得满堂彩，其内心的雀跃，也未必赛得过我听到这句话时的那阵酥麻。人生也许还有比打断绑匪的腿更崇高的胜利，可我那时不这么想。我费了好大劲才按住自己，没有喝出彩来。

「开车吧。」前座那人说。

低鸣拔高成咆哮。汽车掉过头，沿着车道越走越快。嗡嗡声渐弱，渐止。我掸掉大衣上的雪，朝正门走去。

===Original===
My first act on entering the house, was to release White. He was still lying where I had seen him last. He appeared to have made no headway with the cords on his wrists and ankles. I came to his help with a rather blunt pocketknife, and he rose stiffly and began to chafe the injured arms in silence.

“They’ve gone,” I said.

He nodded.

“Did they hit you with a sandbag?”

He nodded again.

“I broke Buck’s leg,” I said, with modest pride.

===Chinese===
进宅后我的头一件事，是给怀特松绑。他还躺在我最后见到他的地方。看他手腕脚踝上的绳索，似乎毫无进展。我用一把相当钝的小折刀助了他一臂之力，他僵硬地站起身，默默揉搓着受难的胳膊。

「他们走了。」我说。

他点点头。

「他们是用沙袋打你的？」

他又点点头。

「我摔断了巴克的腿。」我说，带着克制的自豪。

===Original===
He looked up incredulously. I related my experiences as briefly as possible, and when I came to the part where I made my flying tackle, the gloom was swept from his face by a joyful smile. Buck’s injury may have given its recipient pain, but it was certainly the cause of pleasure to others. White’s manner was one of the utmost enthusiasm as I described the scene.

“That’ll hold Buck for a while,” was his comment. “I guess we shan’t hear from *him* for a week or two. That’s the best cure for the headache I’ve ever struck.”

He rubbed the lump that just showed beneath his hair. I did not wonder at his emotion. Whoever had wielded the sandbag had done his work well, in a manner to cause hard feelings on the part of the victim.

I had been vaguely conscious during this conversation of an intermittent noise like distant thunder. I now perceived that it came from Glossop’s classroom, and was caused by the beating of hands on the door-panels. I remembered that the red-moustached man had locked Glossop and his young charges in. It seemed to me that he had done well. There would be plenty of confusion without their assistance.

I was turning towards my own classroom when I saw Audrey on the stairs and went to meet her.

===Chinese===
他抬起头，一脸难以置信。我把经过尽量简短地讲了一遍，讲到飞身擒抱那一段时，愁云从他脸上被一抹欢喜的笑容扫荡一空。巴克的伤或许给伤者本人带去了疼痛，却无疑给旁人带去了快乐。我描绘那一幕时，怀特的态度热忱到了极点。

「这够让巴克安分一阵子了，」这是他的评语。「我估摸着一两个星期之内，*他*不会再上门了。治头痛，这是我所撞见过最灵的方子。」

他揉了揉头发底下刚显出来的那个鼓包。他的激动我毫不奇怪。不论抡沙袋的是哪一位，活儿干得漂亮，其手法足以在受害者一方激起恶感。

这段对话期间，我一直隐隐觉出一种断断续续的闷响，像远雷。此刻我看出来了：它来自格拉索普的教室，是巴掌拍在门板上造成的。我想起那位红胡子汉子把格拉索普和他年幼的弟子们锁在了里面。依我看，他这一手干得不坏。没有他们帮腔，乱子也已经够多了。

我正要转身回自己的教室，看见奥黛丽在楼梯上，便迎了过去。

===Original===
“It’s all right,” I said. “They’ve gone.”

“Who was it? What did they want?”

“It was a gentleman named MacGinnis and some friends. They came after Ogden Ford, but they didn’t get him.”

“Where is he? Where is Ogden?”

Before I could reply, babel broke loose. While we had been talking, White had injudiciously turned the key of Glossop’s classroom which now disgorged its occupants, headed by my colleague, in a turbulent stream. At the same moment my own classroom began to empty itself. The hall was packed with boys, and the din became deafening. Everyone had something to say, and they all said it at once.

===Chinese===
「没事了，」我说。「他们走了。」

「来的是什么人？他们要干什么？」

「是一位名叫麦金尼斯的先生，还带着几位朋友。他们是冲奥格登·福特来的，不过没得手。」

「他在哪儿？奥格登在哪儿？」

不等我回答，巴别塔轰然崩塌了。就在我们说话的当口，怀特失于考虑，拧开了格拉索普教室的锁，那间屋便把它的住户倾泻而出，由我同事领头，汇成一股汹涌的洪流。同一时刻，我自己的教室也开始倾空。大厅里挤满了男孩，喧嚣震耳欲聋。人人都有一肚子话，而且人人同时开讲。

===Original===
Glossop was at my side, semaphoring violently.

“We must telephone,” he bellowed in my ear, “for the police.”

Somebody tugged at my arm. It was Audrey. She was saying something which was drowned in the uproar. I drew her towards the stairs, and we found comparative quiet on the first landing.

“What were you saying?” I asked.

“He isn’t there.”

===Chinese===
格拉索普到了我身边，猛烈地打旗语。

「咱们得打电话，」他冲我耳朵吼，「叫警察。」

有人拽我的胳膊。是奥黛丽。她在说什么，被喧嚣淹没了。我把她拉向楼梯，在二楼的楼梯平台上，我们觅得了相对的安静。

「你刚要说什么？」我问。

「他不在。」

===Original===
“Who?”

“Ogden Ford. Where is he? He is not in his room. They must have taken him.”

Glossop came up at a gallop, springing from stair to stair like the chamois of the Alps.

“We must telephone for the police!” he cried.

“I have telephoned,” said Audrey, “ten minutes ago. They are sending some men at once. Mr. Glossop, was Ogden Ford in your classroom?”

===Chinese===
「谁？」

「奥格登·福特。他在哪儿？他不在他房间里。他们准是把他带走了。」

格拉索普飞驰而至，从一级楼梯跃上另一级，恰似阿尔卑斯山的岩羚羊。

「咱们得打电话叫警察！」他喊道。

「我已经打过了，」奥黛丽说，「十分钟前。他们马上派人来。格拉索普先生，奥格登·福特在你教室里吗？」

===Original===
“No, Mrs. Sheridan. I thought he was with you, Burns.”

I shook my head.

“Those men came to kidnap him, Mr. Glossop,” said Audrey.

“Undoubtedly the gang of scoundrels to which that man the other night belonged! This is preposterous. My nerves will not stand these repeated outrages. We must have police protection. The villains must be brought to justice. I never heard of such a thing! In an English school!”

Glossop’s eyes gleamed agitatedly behind their spectacles. Macbeth’s deportment when confronted with Banquo’s ghost was stolid by comparison. There was no doubt that Buck’s visit had upset the smooth peace of our happy little community to quite a considerable extent.

===Chinese===
「不在，谢里丹太太。我原以为他跟你在一起，伯恩斯。」

我摇摇头。

「那些人是来绑架他的，格拉索普先生。」奥黛丽说。

「毋庸置疑，正是前几夜那个人所属的那伙恶棍！这简直荒谬绝伦。我的神经经不起这样接二连三的暴行。我们必须有警察保护。恶徒必须绳之以法。闻所未闻！在一所英国学校里！」

格拉索普的眼睛在镜片后面激动地闪光。相形之下，麦克白直面班柯（Banquo）鬼魂时的仪态都算得上镇定了。毫无疑问，巴克的来访，已把我们这个快乐小天地一池平滑的安宁，搅得相当可以了。

===Original===
The noise in the hall had increased rather than subsided. A belated sense of professional duty returned to Glossop and myself. We descended the stairs and began to do our best, in our respective styles, to produce order. It was not an easy task. Small boys are always prone to make a noise, even without provocation. When they get a genuine excuse like the incursion of men in white masks, who prod assistant-masters in the small of the back with Browning pistols, they tend to eclipse themselves. I doubt whether we should ever have quieted them, had it not been that the hour of Buck’s visit had chanced to fall within a short time of that set apart for the boys’ tea, and that the kitchen had lain outside the sphere of our visitors’ operations. As in many English country houses, the kitchen at Sanstead House was at the end of a long corridor, shut off by doors through which even pistol-shots penetrated but faintly. Our excellent cook had, moreover, the misfortune to be somewhat deaf, with the result that, throughout all the storm and stress in our part of the house, she, like the lady in Goethe’s poem, had gone on cutting bread and butter; till now, when it seemed that nothing could quell the uproar, there rose above it the ringing of the bell.

If there is anything exciting enough to keep the Englishman or the English boy from his tea, it has yet to be discovered. The shouting ceased on the instant. The general feeling seemed to be that inquiries could be postponed till a more suitable occasion, but not tea. There was a general movement in the direction of the dining-room.

Glossop had already gone with the crowd, and I was about to follow, when there was another ring at the front-door bell.

I gathered that this must be the police, and waited. In the impending inquiry I was by way of being a star witness. If anyone had been in the thick of things from the beginning it was myself.

===Chinese===
大厅里的喧嚣有增无减。职业责任感迟迟归位，总算回到了格拉索普和我身上。我们下楼，各按各的路数，竭力制造秩序。这不是件容易差事。小男孩本来就动辄喧哗，哪怕无人招惹。一旦得了像样的由头——比如一帮白面具汉子闯入宅内、还拿勃朗宁手枪捅助理教师的后腰——他们便要超水平发挥了。若不是巴克的来访碰巧落在离男孩们吃茶点规定的时辰不远，又若不是厨房恰好处在我们这几位访客行动范围之外，我真怀疑我们究竟静得下他们来。同英国许多乡间宅邸一样，桑斯特德宅的厨房在一条长走廊的尽头，隔着几道门，连枪声传过去都只剩微响。何况我们那位出色的厨师还不幸有些耳背，结果是：宅子我们这一头风雨飘摇的整个过程中，她像歌德（Goethe）诗里的那位夫人一样，自顾自地切她的面包黄油；直到此刻，眼看再没有什么能压下这场喧嚣了，铃声明朗朗地升了起来，盖过了一切。

世上若有哪桩事够刺激，能拦住英国人或英国男孩不吃茶点，那桩事尚待发现。喊声霎时止住。公众情绪似乎是：查问尽可推迟到一个更合适的时辰，茶点却不可以。餐厅的方向刮起了一场全员移动。

格拉索普已随大流去了，我正要跟上，前门铃又响了一声。

我料想这必是警察到了，便等着。在即将展开的查问中，我算是要出任明星证人。若说有谁从头到尾都在事件的旋涡中心，那就是我本人。

===Original===
White opened the door. I caught a glimpse of blue uniforms, and came forward to do the honours.

There were two of them, no more. In response to our urgent appeal for assistance against armed bandits, the Majesty of the Law had materialized itself in the shape of a stout inspector and a long, lean constable. I thought, as I came to meet them, that they were fortunate to have arrived late.
