I could see Lefty and the red-moustached man, thwarted in their designs on me, making dreadful havoc among the official force, as here represented.

White, the simple butler once more, introduced us.

“This is Mr. Burns, one of the masters at the school,” he said, and removed himself from the scene. There never was a man like White for knowing his place when he played the butler.

The inspector looked at me sharply. The constable gazed into space.

“H’m!” said the inspector.

===Chinese===
怀特开了门。我瞥见蓝色的制服，便上前尽地主之谊。

一共来了两位，仅此而已。面对我们抗击武装强盗的紧急求援，法律的威严化身为一位肥硕的巡官和一位精瘦颀长的警员，莅临现场。我迎上前去时心想：他们来得晚，实在算是运气。我仿佛看得见左撇子和红胡子汉子——倘若他们加害于我的图谋得逞——在这一支官方部队（即眼前所代表的这两位）中间，掀起怎样一场骇人的血洗。

怀特又做回了那个本分的男管家，替我们引见。

「这位是伯恩斯先生，学校里的一位教师。」说完，他便从场景中自行退场。扮管家的时候，怀特对自家分寸的把握，天下无出其右。

巡官锐利地打量我。警员凝望虚空。

「唔！」巡官说。

===Original===
Mentally I had named them Bones and Johnson. I do not know why, except that they seemed to deserve it.

“You telephoned for us,” said Bones accusingly.

“We did.”

“What’s the trouble? What﻿—got your notebook?﻿—has been happening?”

Johnson removed his gaze from the middle distance and produced a notebook.

“At about half past five﻿—” I began.

===Chinese===
我心里已经给他们起好了名字：骨头（Bones）和约翰逊（Johnson）。为什么起这两个名字，我说不上来，只知道他们看起来当之无愧。

「你们打电话叫我们来的。」骨头带着兴师问罪的口气说。

「是我们打的。」

「出了什么乱子？什么——笔记本拿好了没？——到底出了什么事？」

约翰逊把目光从中景移开，掏出一个笔记本。

「大约五点半的时候——」我开始说。

===Original===
Johnson moistened his pencil.

“At about half past five an automobile drove up to the front door. In it were five masked men with revolvers.”

I interested them. There was no doubt of that. Bones’s healthy colour deepened, and his eyes grew round. Johnson’s pencil raced over the page, wobbling with emotion.

“Masked men?” echoed Bones.

“With revolvers,” I said. “Now aren’t you glad you didn’t go to the circus? They rang the front-door bell; when White opened it, they stunned him with a sandbag. Then﻿—”

Bones held up a large hand.

===Chinese===
约翰逊舔了舔铅笔头。

「大约五点半，一辆汽车开到了正门前。车里是五个带左轮枪的蒙面人。」

我引起他们的兴趣了。这一点毫无疑义。骨头那健康的脸色深了几度，眼睛瞪圆了。约翰逊的铅笔在纸页上疾驰，激动得直打颤。

「蒙面人？」骨头应声重复。

「带着左轮枪，」我说。「现在你们该庆幸今晚没去看马戏了吧？他们按了正门的铃；怀特一开门，他们就用沙袋把他打晕了。然后——」

骨头举起一只大手。

===Original===
“Wait!”

I waited.

“Who is White?”

“The butler.”

“I will take his statement. Fetch the butler.”

Johnson trotted off obediently.

===Chinese===
「且慢！」

我便等着。

「怀特是谁？」

「男管家。」

「我要录取他的口供。去把男管家带来。」

约翰逊乖乖地小跑去了。

===Original===
Left alone with me, Bones became friendlier and less official.

“This is as queer a start as ever I heard of, Mr. Burns,” he said. “Twenty years I’ve been in the force, and nothing like this has transpired. It beats cockfighting. What in the world do you suppose men with masks and revolvers was after? First idea I had was that you were making fun of me.”

I was shocked at the idea. I hastened to give further details.

“They were a gang of American crooks who had come over to kidnap Mr. Elmer Ford’s son, who is a pupil at the school. You have heard of Mr. Ford? He is an American millionaire, and there have been several attempts during the past few years to kidnap Ogden.”

At this point Johnson returned with White. White told his story briefly, exhibited his bruise, showed the marks of the cords on his wrists, and was dismissed. I suggested that further conversation had better take place in the presence of Mr. Abney, who, I imagined, would have something to say on the subject of hushing the thing up.

===Chinese===
「伯恩斯先生，这样离奇的开局，我还是头一回听说，」他说。「我干这一行二十年，这样的事从没出过。这可比斗鸡还稀奇。你说说，戴面具挎左轮的人究竟是图什么来的？我头一个念头，是你在寻我的开心。」

这个念头令我震惊。我赶紧补充了更多细节。

「他们是一伙美国骗子，漂洋过海来绑架埃尔默·福特先生的儿子——那孩子是本校的学生。福特先生你听说过吧？他是美国百万富翁，过去这几年，绑架奥格登的图谋已经有过好几起了。」

这时约翰逊带着怀特回来了。怀特简短地讲了经过，出示了他的淤伤，又亮出手腕上的绳痕，随后获准退下。我提议，接下去的谈话最好挪到阿布尼先生面前进行——我料想，在把这件事捂严实这个题目上，他会有几句话要说。

我们上了楼。书房那扇破门耽搁了我们一阵，也惹得约翰逊的铅笔又痉挛了一回。处理完这项，我们便向阿布尼先生的房间进发。

===Original===
We went upstairs. The broken door of the study delayed us a while and led to a fresh spasm of activity on the part of Johnson’s pencil. Having disposed of this, we proceeded to Mr. Abney’s room.

Bones’s authoritative rap upon the door produced an agitated “Who’s that?” from the occupant. I explained the nature of the visitation through the keyhole and there came from within the sound of moving furniture. His one brief interview with Buck had evidently caused my employer to ensure against a second by barricading himself in with everything he could find suitable for the purpose. It was some moments before the way was clear for our entrance.

“Cub id,” said a voice at last.

Mr. Abney was sitting up in bed, the blankets wrapped tightly about him. His appearance was still disordered. The furniture of the room was in great confusion, and a poker on the floor by the dressing-table showed that he had been prepared to sell his life dearly.

“I ab glad to see you, Idspector,” he said. “Bister Burds, what is the expladation of this extraordinary affair?”

===Chinese===
骨头威严地叩了叩门，屋里传出一声惊慌的「是谁？」。我从钥匙孔里说明了来客的性质，屋内随即响起搬动家具的声音。同巴克那一场简短的会晤，显然已促使我的东家未雨绸缪：凡是屋里找得到的合用家什，全被他搬来堵了门，保险不再有第二次。过了好一会儿，门口方才疏通。

「请近。」终于，里面传出一个声音。

阿布尼先生在床上坐着，毯子裹得严严实实。他的仪表依旧狼狈。房里的家具乱作一团，梳妆台边地板上的一根拨火棍表明，他已做好打算，要让自己的生命卖个好价钱。

「我很高兴见到你，巡倌，」他说。「伯德斯先生，这桩离奇事情的解德是什么？」

===Original===
It took some time to explain matters to Mr. Abney, and more to convince Bones and his colleague that, so far from wanting a hue and cry raised over the countryside and columns about the affair in the papers, publicity was the thing we were anxious to avoid. They were visibly disappointed when they grasped the position of affairs. The thing, properly advertised, would have been the biggest that had ever happened to the neighbourhood, and their eager eyes could see glory within easy reach. Mention of a cold snack and a drop of beer, however, to be found in the kitchen, served to cast a gleam of brightness on their gloom, and they vanished in search of it with something approaching cheeriness, Johnson taking notes to the last.

They had hardly gone when Glossop whirled into the room in a state of effervescing agitation.

“Mr. Abney, Ogden Ford is nowhere to be found!”

Mr. Abney greeted the information with a prodigious sneeze.

“What do you bead?” he demanded, when the paroxysm was over. He turned to me. “Bister Burds, I understood you to﻿—ah﻿—say that the scou’drels took their departure without the boy Ford.”

===Chinese===
向阿布尼先生说明情况，花去了一些时间；说服骨头和他的同事，又花去了更多——要让他们相信：我们非但不想闹到全村鸣锣追捕、报纸连篇累牍，恰恰相反，风声张扬正是我们避之唯恐不及的东西。待他们弄清局面，两人肉眼可见地失望了。这桩事若好好宣传一番，将是本乡本土有史以来最轰动的新闻，他们热切的眼里分明看见荣耀唾手可得。不过，提一句厨房里备有冷盘小吃和一点啤酒，便给他们的愁云投下了一线光明，于是两人近乎欢欣地寻吃食去了，约翰逊到最后一刻还在记笔记。

他们前脚刚走，格拉索普后脚便旋进屋来，一副沸腾激荡的状态。

「阿布尼先生，奥格登·福特哪儿都找不到了！」

阿布尼先生以一个惊天动地的喷嚏迎接了这条消息。

「你这话是什么意德？」发作过去后，他质问道。他转向我。「伯德斯先生，我记得你——啊——说过，那帮恶棍离开时没有带走福特家的孩子。」

===Original===
“They certainly did. I watched them go.”

“I have searched the house thoroughly,” said Glossop, “and there are no signs of him. And not only that, the Boy Beckford cannot be found.”

Mr. Abney clasped his head in his hands. Poor man, he was in no condition to bear up with easy fortitude against this succession of shocks. He was like one who, having survived an earthquake, is hit by an automobile. He had partly adjusted his mind to the quiet contemplation of Mr. MacGinnis and friends when he was called upon to face this fresh disaster. And he had a cold in the head, which unmans the stoutest. Napoleon would have won Waterloo if Wellington had had a cold in the head.

“Augustus Beckford caddot be fou’d?” he echoed feebly.

“They must have run away together,” said Glossop.

===Chinese===
「确实没有。我亲眼看着他们走的。」

「我把宅子彻底搜了一遍，」格拉索普说，「他杳无踪迹。还不止如此，贝克福德家那孩子也找不到了。」

阿布尼先生双手抱住了脑袋。可怜的人，接二连三的打击之下，他已没有多少从容的坚毅可供支取了。他像一个大难不死的地震幸存者，转眼又给汽车撞了。他刚把心思勉强调匀，进入对麦金尼斯先生及其朋友的静观状态，却又被叫来直面这场新灾。何况他还害着伤风，而伤风最能卸掉猛士的武装。当年威灵顿（Wellington）若是害着伤风，拿破仑早就打赢滑铁卢（Waterloo）了。

「奥古斯塔斯·贝克福德找不德了？」他有气无力地应声道。

「他们准是一起跑掉了。」格拉索普说。

## X / X

===Original===

When Sam had left, which he did rather in the manner of a heavy father in melodrama, shaking the dust of an erring son’s threshold off his feet, I mixed myself a highball, and sat down to consider the position of affairs. It did not take me long to see that the infernal boy had double-crossed me with a smooth effectiveness which Mr. Fisher himself might have envied. Somewhere in this great city, as Sam had observed, he was hiding. But where? London is a vague address.

I wondered what steps Sam was taking. Was there some underground secret service bureau to which persons of his profession had access? I doubted it. I imagined that he, as I proposed to do, was drawing the city at a venture in the hope of flushing the quarry by accident. Yet such was the impression he had made upon me as a man of resource and sagacity, that I did not relish the idea of his getting a start on me, even in a venture so uncertain as this. My imagination began to picture him miraculously inspired in the search, and such was the vividness of the vision that I jumped up from my chair, resolved to get on the trail at once. It was hopelessly late, however, and I did not anticipate that I should meet with any success.

Nor did I. For two hours and a half I tramped the streets, my spirits sinking more and more under the influence of failure and a blend of snow and sleet which had begun to fall; and then, tired out, I went back to my rooms, and climbed sorrowfully into bed.
===Chinese===
山姆走的时候，那副做派俨然情节剧里的严厉老父，把误入歧途的儿子门槛上的尘土从脚上抖落干净——他走后，我给自己调了一杯海波酒（highball），坐下来通盘考虑眼下的局面。没费多大工夫我便看明白了：那个可恶的小子出卖了我，卖得圆滑利落，连费舍尔先生本人怕都要艳羡。诚如山姆所言，他正藏在这座大城的某个角落。可是在哪儿呢？伦敦这个地址，未免太笼统了。

我纳闷山姆在采取什么步骤。干他们那一行的，是不是有什么地下的秘密勤务机构可以出入？我深表怀疑。我猜想，他正同我打算做的一样，在满城信手排搜，指望碰巧把猎物惊出窝来。可是，他留给我的印象是一个机变百出、精明老到的人物，一想到他抢到我的前头，哪怕在这般没准头的营生里，我心里也老大不是滋味。我的想象力开始把他描画成搜寻之中得了神启的人物，而这幅幻象又栩栩如生，逼得我从椅子里一跃而起，决意立刻上道追踪。不过时辰已经晚得没了指望，我并不指望自己能有什么收获。

我果然一无所获。我在街上走了两个半钟头，失败，加上初落的雨夹雪，把我的兴致越压越低；末了，筋疲力尽，我回到寓所，愁眉苦脸地爬上床去。

===Original===

It was odd to wake up and realize that I was in London. Years seemed to have passed since I had left it. Time is a thing of emotions, not of hours and minutes, and I had certainly packed a considerable number of emotional moments into my stay at Sanstead House. I lay in bed, reviewing the past, while Smith, with a cheerful clatter of crockery, prepared my breakfast in the next room.

A curious lethargy had succeeded the feverish energy of the previous night. More than ever the impossibility of finding the needle in this human bundle of hay oppressed me. No one is optimistic before breakfast, and I regarded the future with dull resignation, turning my thoughts from it after a while to the past. But the past meant Audrey, and to think of Audrey hurt.

It seemed curious to me that in a life of thirty years I should have been able to find, among the hundreds of women I had met, only one capable of creating in me that disquieting welter of emotions which is called love, and hard that that one should reciprocate my feeling only to the extent of the mild liking which Audrey entertained for me.
===Chinese===
醒来发觉自己身在伦敦，感觉怪得很。自我离开伦敦，仿佛已过去好些年。时间是情绪造出来的东西，不是钟点攒出来的，而我在桑斯特德宅盘桓的这一程，确实塞进了为数可观的情绪时刻。我躺在床上回顾既往，史密斯在隔壁屋里快活地把瓷器弄得叮当乱响，为我预备早饭。

昨夜那阵发热似的干劲过后，接踵而来的是一种古怪的倦怠。要在这硕大的一捆人肉干草里找出一根针，这份绝无可能，比以往任何时候都更沉重地压在我心头。早饭之前没有谁是乐观的，我怀着麻木的听天由命望着未来，过了一会儿，索性把心思从未来挪向了过去。可过去意味着奥黛丽，而想奥黛丽，是疼的。

我觉得奇怪：活了三十年，在我结识过的成百个女人里头，竟只有一个能在我的心头搅起那名为爱情的、令人不安的一团乱麻；而更叫人难堪的是，独独这一个，回敬我的感情，也不过止于奥黛丽对我怀有的那点淡淡的喜欢。

===Original===

I tried to analyse her qualifications for the place she held in my heart. I had known women who had attracted me more physically, and women who had attracted me more mentally. I had known wiser women, handsomer women, more amiable women, but none of them had affected me like Audrey. The problem was inexplicable. Any idea that we might be affinities, soul-mates destined for each other from the beginning of time, was disposed of by the fact that my attraction for her was apparently in inverse ratio to hers for me. For possibly the millionth time in the past five years I tried to picture in my mind the man Sheridan, that shadowy wooer to whom she had yielded so readily. What quality had he possessed that I did not? Wherein lay the magnetism that had brought about his triumph?

These were unprofitable speculations. I laid them aside until the next occasion when I should feel disposed for self-torture, and got out of bed. A bath and breakfast braced me up, and I left the house in a reasonably cheerful frame of mind.

To search at random for an individual unit among London’s millions lends an undeniable attraction to a day in town. In a desultory way I pursued my investigations through the morning and afternoon, but neither of Ogden nor of his young friend Lord Beckford was I vouchsafed a glimpse. My consolation was that Smooth Sam was probably being equally unsuccessful.
===Chinese===
我试图分析她凭什么资格占住我心中那个位置。论肉身更吸引我的女人，我认识过；论头脑更吸引我的，也认识过。我认识过更聪明的女人、更好看的女人、更和蔼的女人，可她们没有一个像奥黛丽这样拿捏得住我。这个问题没法解释。若说我们是天造地设的知音，是时间之始便彼此命定的灵魂伴侣，那么她被我吸引的程度，显然与我被她吸引的程度成反比——这个事实便把那念头勾销了。五年来的大概第一百万次，我试着在心里描画谢里丹其人，那个她轻易便委身的影子般的求婚者。他具备什么我不具备的品质？成就了他那场胜利的磁力，又藏在何处？

这些都是无利可图的玄想。我把它们搁置一旁，留待下次想自我折磨的时候再拿出来，随后下了床。一个澡、一顿早饭让我重新振作，我怀着一副相当愉快的心境出了门。

在伦敦的几百万人口里漫无目标地搜寻一个单独的个体，给进城的一天平添了不可否认的魅力。整个上午和下午，我有一搭没一搭地推进我的侦查，可是奥格登也好，他的小朋友贝克福德勋爵也好，我连一眼都无缘得见。聊以自慰的是，圆滑山姆此刻多半也同样一无所获。

===Original===

Towards the evening there arose the question of return to Sanstead. I had not gathered whether Mr. Abney had intended to set any time-limit on my wanderings, or whether I was not supposed to come back except with the deserters. I decided that I had better remain in London, at any rate for another night, and went to the nearest post office to send Mr. Abney a telegram to that effect.

As I was writing it, the problem which had baffled me for twenty-four hours, solved itself in under a minute. Whether my powers of inductive reasoning had been under a cloud since I left Sanstead, or whether they were normally beneath contempt, I do not know. But the fact remains, that I had completely overlooked the obvious solution of my difficulty. I think I must have been thinking so exclusively of the Little Nugget that I had entirely forgotten the existence of Augustus Beckford. It occurred to me now that, by making inquiries at the latter’s house, I should learn something to my advantage. A boy of the Augustus type does not run away from school without a reason. Probably some party was taking place tonight at the ancestral home, at which, tempted by the lawless Nugget, he had decided that his presence was necessary.

I knew the house well. There had been a time, when Lord Mountry and I were at Oxford, when I had spent frequent weekends there. Since then, owing to being abroad, I had seen little of the family. Now was the moment to reintroduce myself. I hailed a cab.

Inductive reasoning had not played me false. There was a red carpet outside the house, and from within came the sounds of music.
===Chinese===
将近傍晚，回不回桑斯特德的问题冒了上来。我没有弄清，阿布尼先生究竟打算不打算给我的流浪设一个期限，还是说，非带着那两个逃兵，我便不得回去。我拿定主意，还是留在伦敦的好，至少再留一夜，于是到最近的邮局，给阿布尼先生拍了一封申明此意的电报。

正写着，那个难倒我二十四小时的问题，不到一分钟便自己解开了。我那归纳推理的本事，究竟是离开桑斯特德以来一直阴云罩顶，还是压根就不值一哂，我说不好。但事实俱在：我竟把那个明摆着的解决办法整个忽略了。我想，我准是把心思全放在小金块一个人身上，把奥古斯塔斯·贝克福德的存在忘了个干净。此刻我才想起，到后者的府上打探一番，准能问出于我有利的消息。奥古斯塔斯这一类型的孩子，不会无缘无故从学校出走。多半是今晚祖宅里要开什么聚会，他受不住那个无法无天的小金块的撺掇，认定自己非到场不可。

那座宅子我很熟。有一阵子，芒特里勋爵和我在牛津（Oxford）同窗，我常去那里度周末。此后由于身在海外，我同这一家便少见了。此刻正是重新登门的大好时机。我招了一辆马车。

归纳推理没有骗我。宅门外铺着红地毯，屋里传出音乐声。

===Original===

Lady Wroxham, the mother of Mountry and the vanishing Augustus, was one of those women who take things as they come. She did not seem surprised at seeing me.

“How nice of you to come and see us,” she said. “Somebody told me you were abroad. Ted is in the south of France in the yacht. Augustus is here. Mr. Abney, his schoolmaster, let him come up for the night.”

I perceived that Augustus had been playing a bold game. I saw the coaching of Ogden behind these dashing falsehoods.

“You will hardly remember Sybil. She was quite a baby when you were here last. She is having her birthday-party this evening.”

“May I go in and help?” I said.

“I wish you would. They would love it.”
===Chinese===
沃克瑟姆夫人（Lady Wroxham）——芒特里和那位失踪的奥古斯塔斯的母亲——是那种凡事来了便接着受的女人。看见我，她似乎并不惊讶。

「你来看我们，真好，」她说。「有人告诉我你出了国。特德（Ted）坐游艇到法国南部去了。奥古斯塔斯在这儿。他的校长阿布尼先生准他上伦敦来过一夜。」

我看出来了，奥古斯塔斯玩的是一手大胆的牌。这一套天花乱坠的谎话背后，有奥格登的调教。

「你多半不记得西比尔（Sybil）了。你上回来的时候，她还十足是个奶娃娃。她今晚开生日会。」

「我能进去帮把手吗？」我说。

「求之不得。他们准高兴。」

===Original===

I doubted it, but went in. A dance had just finished. Strolling towards me in his tightest Eton suit, his face shining with honest joy, was the errant Augustus, and close behind him, wearing the blasé air of one for whom custom has staled the pleasures of life, was the Little Nugget.

I think they both saw me at the same moment. The effect of my appearance on them was illustrative of their respective characters. Augustus turned a deep shade of purple and fixed me with a horrified stare. The Nugget winked. Augustus halted and shuffled his feet. The Nugget strolled up and accosted me like an old friend.

“Hello!” he said. “How did you get here? Say, I was going to try and get you on the phone some old time and explain things. I’ve been pretty much on the jump since I hit London.”

“You little brute!”
===Chinese===
我对此存疑，但还是进去了。一支舞刚跳完。正朝我踱来的，是那位出走的奥古斯塔斯，穿着他那身最紧的伊顿装（Eton suit），脸上放着老实的欢喜光；紧跟在他身后的便是小金块，一副腻烦的老江湖神气，仿佛人生的种种乐趣，在他身上早被习惯磨得走了味。

我想他们是同一刻看见我的。我的露面对他们二位产生的效果，恰可用来图解各自的性格。奥古斯塔斯涨成了一副深紫色，惊恐万状地把我瞪住。小金块挤了挤眼睛。奥古斯塔斯站住不走了，两只脚在地上蹭来蹭去。小金块踱上前来，像老朋友似的同我搭话。

「喂！」他说。「你怎么摸到这儿来的？嘿，我还想哪天想法子给你打个电话，把事情解释解释呢。自打到伦敦，我忙得脚都没沾过地。」

「你这个小畜生！」

===Original===

My gleaming eye, travelling past him, met that of the Hon. Augustus Beckford, causing that youth to jump guiltily. The Nugget looked over his shoulder.

“I guess we don’t want him around if we’re to talk business,” he said. “I’ll go and tell him to beat it.”

“You’ll do nothing of the kind. I don’t propose to lose sight of either of you.”

“Oh, he’s all right. You don’t have to worry about him. He was going back to the school anyway tomorrow. He only ran away to go to this party. Why not let him enjoy himself while he’s here? I’ll go and make a date for you to meet at the end of the show.”

He approached his friend, and a short colloquy ensued, which ended in the latter shuffling off in the direction of the other revellers. Such is the buoyancy of youth that a moment later he was dancing a two-step with every appearance of careless enjoyment. The future, with its storms, seemed to have slipped from his mind.
===Chinese===
我那目光灼灼的眼睛越过他，与尊敬的奥古斯塔斯·贝克福德的那一双撞了个正着，惊得那位少年做贼心虚地一跳。小金块回头望了望。

「我琢磨着，咱们要谈买卖，就不能要他在跟前，」他说。「我去叫他开路。」

「这种事你想也别想。我可不打算让你们俩哪一个走出我的视线。」

「哦，他没事的。你用不着为他操心。反正他明天横竖要回学校。他逃出来，不过是为了赶这个会。趁他人在这里，何不让他乐一乐？我去替你们定个约，散场的时候碰头。」

他走到他朋友跟前，短短一番会谈随之而来，以后者拖着脚步朝其余的寻欢客走去收场。青春的浮力便是这样：不消片刻，他已带着一副满不在乎的欢快神情跳起两步舞（two-step）来。未来，连同它的几场风暴，似乎已从他心里滑出去了。

===Original===

“That’s all right,” said the Nugget, returning to me. “He’s promised he won’t duck away. You’ll find him somewhere around whenever you care to look for him. Now we can talk.”

“I hardly like to trespass on your valuable time,” I said. The airy way in which this demon boy handled what should have been﻿—to him﻿—an embarrassing situation irritated me. For all the authority I seemed to have over him I might have been the potted palm against which he was leaning.

“That’s all right.” Everything appeared to be all right with him. “This sort of thing does not appeal to me. Don’t be afraid of spoiling my evening. I only came because Becky was so set on it. Dancing bores me pallid, so let’s get somewhere where we can sit down and talk.”

I was beginning to feel that a children’s party was the right place for me. Sam Fisher had treated me as a child, and so did the Little Nugget. That I was a responsible person, well on in my thirty-first year, with a narrow escape from death and a hopeless love-affair on my record, seemed to strike neither of them. I followed my companion to a secluded recess with the utmost meekness.
===Chinese===
「妥了，」小金块回到我身边，说。「他答应了不溜号。你什么时候想找他，在这一带转转总能找着。现在咱们可以谈了。」

「我真不敢占用你宝贵的时光，」我说。这个小魔王处理这个——对他而言本该——尴尬的处境时那副轻飘飘的做派，把我激恼了。论我对他似乎有的那点权威，我还不如他此刻倚着的那盆盆栽棕榈。

「没事儿。」在他那里，样样事情看来都是没事儿的。「这类玩意儿引不起我的兴致。别怕扫了我今晚的兴。我来，纯粹是因为贝基（Becky）死活非要来。跳舞把我烦得脸色发白，咱们找个能坐下来说话的地方吧。」

我开始觉得，一场儿童茶会恰恰是我该待的地方。山姆·费舍尔拿我当小孩，小金块也拿我当小孩。我明明是个有担当的人，三十一岁的一年都快走完了，履历上还记着一回死里逃生和一场无望的恋爱——这一层，他们两位似乎谁也没有意识到。我以十二分的温顺，跟着我这位同伴走向一处僻静的幽角。

===Original===

He leaned back and crossed his legs.

“Got a cigarette?”

“I have not got a cigarette, and, if I had, I wouldn’t give it to you.”

He regarded me tolerantly.

“Got a grouch tonight, haven’t you? You seem all flittered up about something. What’s the trouble? Sore about my not showing up at your apartment? I’ll explain that all right.”

“I shall be glad to listen.”
===Chinese===
他往椅背上一仰，翘起了二郎腿。

「有烟卷吗？」

「我没有烟卷，就算有，也不会给你。」

他宽厚地打量我。

「今晚憋着气呢，是不是？你好像为什么事心浮气躁的。出什么岔子了？是恼我没去你公寓赴约？这个我自会解释清楚。」

「本人乐意一听。」

===Original===

“It’s like this. It suddenly occurred to me that a day or two one way or the other wasn’t going to affect our deal and that, while I was about it, I might just as well see a bit of London before I left. I suggested it to Becky, and the idea made the biggest kind of a hit with him. I found he had only been in an automobile once in his life. Can you beat it? I’ve had one of my own ever since I was a kid. Well, naturally, it was up to me to blow him to a joyride, and that’s where the money went.”

“Where the money went?”

“Sure. I’ve got two dollars left, and that’s all. It wasn’t altogether the automobiling. It was the meals that got away with my roll. Say, that kid Beckford is one swell feeder. He’s wrapping himself around the eats all the time. I guess it’s not smoking that does it. I haven’t the appetite I used to have. Well, that’s how it was, you see. But I’m through now. Cough up the fare and I’ll make the trip tomorrow. Mother’ll be tickled to death to see me.”

“She won’t see you. We’re going back to the school tomorrow.”
===Chinese===
「是这么回事。我忽然想到，早一天晚一天，对咱们的买卖横竖没影响；既然人已在伦敦，不如趁走之前看一看伦敦。我跟贝基一提，他喜欢得没治了。我这才发现，他活了这么大，统共就坐过一回汽车。你说邪门不邪门？我自己还是小毛孩子的时候就有了车。那还用说，请他兜风的事就落在我头上了，钱就都花在那上头了。」

「钱都花在那上头了？」

「可不。我就剩两美元了，统共这些。也不全是汽车兜的风。把我那一卷钞票吃空的，是一顿顿的饭。嘿，贝克福德那小子真是一等一的吃主。他一天到晚缠在吃食上不撒嘴。我看这还真不是抽烟抽的。我的胃口大不如前了。喏，事情就是这样。不过我现在收心了。把路费掏出来，我明天就走。妈见着我，准乐开了花。」

「她见不着你。咱们明天就回学校去。」

===Original===

He looked at me incredulously.

“What’s that? Going back to school?”

“I’ve altered my plans.”

“I’m not going back to any old school. You daren’t take me. Where’ll you be if I tell the hot-air merchant about our deal and you slipping me the money and all that?”

“Tell him what you like. He won’t believe it.”
===Chinese===
他难以置信地看着我。

「什么？回学校去？」

「我的计划变了。」

「我才不回那个破学校去呢。你不敢把我怎么样。要是我把咱们的交易、你偷偷塞我钱这些事，一古脑儿都告诉那位空话贩子，你往哪儿站？」

「随你去告什么状。他不会信的。」

===Original===

He thought this over, and its truth came home to him. The complacent expression left his face.

“What’s the matter with you? Are you dippy, or what? You get me away up to London, and the first thing that happens when I’m here is that you want to take me back. You make me tired.”

It was borne in upon me that there was something in his point of view. My sudden change of mind must have seemed inexplicable to him. And, having by a miracle succeeded in finding him, I was in a mood to be generous. I unbent.
===Chinese===
他把这话掂量了一遍，掂出了这话的斤两。那副洋洋自得的表情，从他脸上撤了下去。

「你这是怎么了？脑子出了毛病，还是怎么的？是你把我大老远弄上伦敦来的，我人一到，你头一件事就是想把我带回去。你真叫我腻味。」

我不由得承认，他的话有几分道理。我突然变卦，在他看来必定莫名其妙。何况我奇迹般地找着了他，正是一副乐得大度的胸襟。我放下了架子。

===Original===

“Ogden, old sport,” I said cordially, “I think we’ve both had all we want of this children’s party. You’re bored and if I stop on another half hour I may be called on to entertain these infants with comic songs. We men of the world are above this sort of thing. Get your hat and coat and I’ll take you to a show. We can discuss business later over a bit of supper.”

The gloom of his countenance melted into a pleased smile.

“You said something that time!” he observed joyfully; and we slunk away to get our hats, the best of friends. A note for Augustus Beckford, requesting his presence at Waterloo Station at ten minutes past twelve on the following morning, I left with the butler. There was a certain informality about my methods which I doubt if Mr. Abney would have approved, but I felt that I could rely on Augustus.

Much may be done by kindness. By the time the curtain fell on the musical comedy which we had attended all was peace between the Nugget and myself. Supper cemented our friendship, and we drove back to my rooms on excellent terms with one another. Half an hour later he was snoring in the spare room, while I smoked contentedly before the fire in the sitting-room.
===Chinese===
「奥格登，老伙计，」我热热乎乎地说，「我看这儿童茶会，咱们俩都受够了。你闷得发慌，我要是再耗上半个钟头，说不定还得被拉去给这帮小娃娃唱滑稽歌。咱们这等见过世面的男子汉，是不屑于这个的。去拿你的帽子和外套，我带你去看一场戏。回头吃点夜宵，咱们再谈正事。」

他脸上的阴霾，化成了一副欢喜的笑。

「这话可说到点子上了！」他欢天喜地地说；于是我们蹑手蹑脚溜出去取帽子，成了顶好的朋友。我给奥古斯塔斯·贝克福德留了一张字条托男管家转交，请他第二天上午十二点十分到滑铁卢车站（Waterloo Station）候着。我这套办法有几分不合规矩，阿布尼先生怕是不会首肯，不过我觉得，奥古斯塔斯这个人靠得住。

善意能办成许多事。我们看的那出音乐喜剧落幕时，小金块与我之间已是一派太平。一顿夜宵巩固了我们的交情，我们彼此好得没话说，一路坐车回到我的寓所。半小时后，他已在客房里打起呼噜，我则坐在客厅的炉火前，心满意足地抽着烟。

===Original===

I had not been there five minutes when the bell rang. Smith was in bed, so I went to the door myself and found Mr. Fisher on the mat.

My feeling of benevolence towards all created things, the result of my successful handling of the Little Nugget, embraced Sam. I invited him in.

“Well,” I said, when I had given him a cigar and filled his glass, “and how have you been getting on, Mr. Fisher? Any luck?”

He shook his head at me reproachfully.
===Chinese===
我坐定还不到五分钟，门铃就响了。史密斯已经上床，我便亲自去开门，只见蹭鞋垫上站着费舍尔先生。

我顺利料理了小金块，由此对天下万物生出的一片仁爱之情，把山姆也拥抱了进去。我请他进屋。

「喏，」我递给他一支雪茄、给他斟满酒杯之后，说，「费舍尔先生，您这一向混得怎么样？交了好运没有？」

他责怪似的朝我摇了摇头。

===Original===

“Young man, you’re deep. I’ve got to hand it to you. I underestimated you. You’re very deep.”

“Approbation from Smooth Sam Fisher is praise indeed. But why these stately compliments?”

“You took me in, young man. I don’t mind owning it. When you told me the Nugget had gone astray, I lapped it up like a babe. And all the time you were putting one over on me. Well, well!”

“But he had gone astray, Mr. Fisher.”

He knocked the ash off his cigar. He wore a pained look.
===Chinese===
「年轻人，你水很深。我不得不服你。我把你估低了。你真是深得很。」

「能得圆滑山姆·费舍尔的赞许，确乎是无上的称誉。不过，何必行此一番庄严的恭维？」

「你把我骗了，年轻人。我不妨招认。你告诉我小金块走失了，我像奶娃娃一样照单全吞。而你从头到尾都在涮我。唉，唉！」

「可他确实走失了呀，费舍尔先生。」

他弹了弹雪茄上的烟灰。他一脸痛心的神色。

===Original===

“You needn’t keep it up, sonny. I happened to be standing within three yards of you when you got into a cab with him in Shaftesbury Avenue.”

I laughed.

“Well, if that’s the case, let there be no secrets between us. He’s asleep in the next room.”

Sam leaned forward earnestly and tapped me on the knee.
===Chinese===
「不必接着演了，小伙子。你在沙夫茨伯里大道（Shaftesbury Avenue）跟他一块儿上马车的时候，我恰好就站在离你三码远的地方。」

我笑了出来。

「好吧，既然如此，咱们之间就不必留什么秘密了。他就在隔壁屋里睡着呢。」

山姆一脸恳切地探身过来，拍了拍我的膝盖。

===Original===

“Young man, this is a critical moment. This is where, if you aren’t careful, you may undo all the good work you have done by getting chesty and thinking that, because you’ve won out so far, you’re the whole show. Believe me, the difficult part is to come, and it’s right here that you need an experienced man to work in with you. Let me in on this and leave the negotiations with old man Ford to me. You would only make a mess of them. I’ve handled this kind of thing a dozen times, and I know just how to act. You won’t regret taking me on as a partner. You won’t lose a cent by it. I can work him for just double what you would get, even supposing you didn’t make a mess of the deal and get nothing.”

“It’s very good of you, but there won’t be any negotiations with Mr. Ford. I am taking the boy back to Sanstead, as I told you.” I caught his pained eye. “I’m afraid you don’t believe me.”

He drew at his cigar without replying.

It is a human weakness to wish to convince those who doubt us, even if their opinion is not intrinsically valuable. I remembered that I had Cynthia’s letter in my pocket. I produced it as exhibit A in my evidence and read it to him.
===Chinese===
「年轻人，这是危急关头。就在这个节骨眼上，你要是一不留神，翘了尾巴自满起来，以为到目前为止都赢了，自己就是整台戏的全部，那你先前的全部功劳就可能毁于一旦。相信我，难啃的还在后头，恰恰在这里，你需要一位有经验的人与你协同行事。让我入伙，把对福特老头子的谈判交给我。你去谈只会谈砸。这类事我经手过一打，该怎么办我一清二楚。你收我做合伙人，绝不后悔。你一个子儿也亏不了。我能从他身上榨出来的，恰是你的两倍——这还是算着你没把买卖弄砸、没落个两手空空。」

「多谢您的美意，可是跟福特先生之间，谈判是不会有啦。正像我告诉过您的，我要带孩子回桑斯特德去。」我撞见他那只痛心的眼睛。「恐怕您是不信我。」

他抽了一口雪茄，没有作答。

哪怕对方的见解本身就值不了几个钱，也总想让怀疑我们的人信服，这是人类的一个弱点。我记起口袋里带着辛西娅的信。我把它作为证物甲（exhibit A）掏了出来，念给他听。

===Original===

Sam listened carefully.

“I see,” he said. “Who wrote that?”

“Never mind. A friend of mine.”

I returned the letter to my pocket.

“I was going to have sent him over to Monaco, but I altered my plans. Something interfered.”
===Chinese===
山姆仔细地听。

「明白了，」他说。「这是谁写的？」

「不劳细问。我的一位朋友。」

我把信放回口袋。

「我本来是要把他送去摩纳哥的，可我改了计划。半路上有点事岔开了。」

===Original===

“What?”

“I might call it coincidence, if you know what that means.”

“And you are really going to take him back to the school?”

“I am.”

“We shall travel back together,” he said. “I had hoped I had seen the last of the place. The English countryside may be delightful in the summer, but for winter give me London. However,” he sighed resignedly, and rose from his chair, “I will say goodbye till tomorrow. What train do you catch?”

“Do you mean to say,” I demanded, “that you have the nerve to come back to Sanstead after what you have told me about yourself?”
===Chinese===
「什么事？」

「不妨管它叫巧合——不知你可明白这是什么意思。」

「你当真要把他带回学校去？」

「当真。」

「那我们就结伴同路了，」他说。「我本指望那地方从此再也不见。英国乡间，夏天也许宜人，可一到冬天，还是给我伦敦吧。不过，」他听天由命地叹了口气，从椅子里起身，「我就此告辞，明天再见。你赶哪一班火车？」

「你是说，」我质问道，「你把自己的底细都对我招了，居然还有脸皮回桑斯特德去？」

===Original===

“You entertain some idea of exposing me to Mr. Abney? Forget it, young man. We are both in glass houses. Don’t let us throw stones. Besides, would he believe it? What proof have you?”

I had thought this argument tolerably sound when I had used it on the Nugget. Now that it was used on myself I realized its soundness even more thoroughly. My hands were tied.

“Yes,” said Sam, “tomorrow, after our little jaunt to London, we shall all resume the quiet, rural life once more.”

He beamed expansively upon me from the doorway.

“However, even the quiet, rural life has its interest. I guess we shan’t be dull!” he said.

I believed him.
===Chinese===
「你是不是盘算着向阿布尼先生揭发我？死了这条心吧，年轻人。咱们俩都住在玻璃房子里，谁也别扔石头。再说，他会信吗？你手里有什么凭据？」

这套论证，当初我拿它对付小金块时，还觉得相当站得住；如今落到我自己头上，我对它的坚实程度，体认得愈发透彻了。我的手被捆住了。

「是啊，」山姆说，「明天，咱们在伦敦小小地游荡过这一遭之后，就又要一齐重过那宁静的田园生活了。」

他站在门口，朝我满面春风地咧出一个宽广的笑。

「不过，宁静的田园生活也有它的趣味。我估摸着，咱们闷不着！」他说。

我信了他的话。

## XI / XI

===Original===

Considering the various handicaps under which he laboured﻿—notably a cold in the head, a fear of the Little Nugget, and a reverence for the aristocracy﻿—Mr. Abney’s handling of the situation, when the runaways returned to school, bordered on the masterly. Any sort of physical punishment being out of the question﻿—especially in the case of the Nugget, who would certainly have retaliated with a bout of window-breaking﻿—he had to fall back on oratory, and he did this to such effect that, when he had finished, Augustus wept openly and was so subdued that he did not ask a single question for nearly three days.

One result of the adventure was that Ogden’s bed was moved to a sort of cubbyhole adjoining my room. In the house, as originally planned, this had evidently been a dressing-room. Under Mr. Abney’s rule it had come to be used as a general repository for lumber. My boxes were there, and a portmanteau of Glossop’s. It was an excellent place in which to bestow a boy in quest of whom kidnappers might break in by night. The window was too small to allow a man to pass through, and the only means of entrance was by way of my room. By night, at any rate, the Nugget’s safety seemed to be assured.

The curiosity of the small boy, fortunately, is not lasting. His active mind lives mainly in the present. It was not many days, therefore, before the excitement caused by Buck’s raid and the Nugget’s disappearance began to subside. Within a week both episodes had been shelved as subjects of conversation, and the school had settled down to its normal humdrum life.

===Chinese===

把阿布尼先生身负的种种不利条件考虑在内——大者有三：一脑袋的伤风、一份对小金块的畏惧、一片对贵族的崇敬——那么，两个出逃的孩子返校之际，他对局面的处置已近乎大师手笔。体罚是无论如何谈不上的——尤其对小金块，那一位准会回敬上一场砸窗子——他只得退而仰仗雄辩；而此策收效之宏，竟至于待他演讲完毕，奥古斯塔斯便当众涕泪滂沱，整个人被震慑得服服帖帖，将近三天不曾提出一个问题。

这场风波的一桩结果，是奥格登的床挪进了与我房间相邻的一个小格子间。就这宅子最初的设计来看，它显然原是一间更衣室。在阿布尼先生治下，它渐渐沦为堆放杂七杂八家什的总货栈。我的几口箱子存放在那儿，还有格拉索普的一只旅行皮包。要安置一个绑匪可能连夜破屋来寻的男孩，这是个再好不过的去处。窗子小得容不得一个成年人钻过，而唯一的入口必须打我的房间经过。至少到了夜里，小金块的安全看来是有保障了。

所幸，小男孩的好奇心并不长久。他那颗活跃的头脑，主要活在眼前。因此没过几天，巴克的夜袭与小金块的失踪所激起的骚动，便开始退潮了。不出一个星期，这两桩公案作为谈资都已束之高阁，学校安顿下来，重归它那寻常的枯燥日子。

===Original===

To me, however, there had come a period of mental unrest more acute than I had ever experienced. My life, for the past five years, had run in so smooth a stream that, now that I found myself tossed about in the rapids, I was bewildered. It was a peculiar aggravation of the difficulty of my position that in my world, the little world of Sanstead House, there should be but one woman, and she the very one whom, if I wished to recover my peace of mind, it was necessary for me to avoid.

My feelings towards Cynthia at this time defied my powers of analysis. There were moments when I clung to the memory of her, when she seemed the only thing solid and safe in a world of chaos, and moments, again, when she was a burden crushing me. There were days when I would give up the struggle and let myself drift, and days when I would fight myself inch by inch. But every day found my position more hopeless than the last.

At night sometimes, as I lay awake, I would tell myself that if only I could see her or even hear from her the struggle would be easier. It was her total disappearance from my life that made it so hard for me. I had nothing to help me to fight.

And then, one morning, as if in answer to my thoughts her letter came.

The letter startled me. It was as if there had been some telepathic communion between us.

===Chinese===

然而我这边，却迎来了一段心绪动荡的时期，其剧烈为我生平所未历。过去五年，我的生命一直在一条如此平顺的溪流里流淌；如今发觉自己被抛在急流里颠来簸去，我便乱了方寸。我的处境之难，还有一层特别的加深：在我这个世界里——桑斯特德宅这个小世界——统共只有一个女人，而她恰恰就是那一位——我若想收回我内心的安宁，便必须避开的人。

这个时候，我对辛西娅的感情，已非我的分析之力所能企及。有些时刻，我紧攀着对她的回忆，那时节，她仿佛是混沌乱世中唯一坚实牢靠的东西；也有些时刻，她又成了一副把我压垮的重担。有些日子，我会放弃挣扎，任凭自己漂着走；也有些日子，我会同自己一寸一寸地搏斗。可是每一天，都发觉我的处境比前一天更无望。

有时夜里，我醒着躺在床上，暗自对自己说：只要能见她一面，哪怕只听到她一点音信，这场仗也会好打一些。正是她从我生活里的彻底销声匿迹，才把我逼到这般难挨。我手里没有任何东西，可以助我一战。

于是，一天早晨，仿佛应答着我的念头似的，她的信到了。

这封信叫我吃了一惊。恍若我们之间真有过一场心灵感应的交流。

===Original===

It was very short, almost formal:

> **My dear Peter,**﻿—I want to ask you a question. I can put it quite shortly. It is this. Are your feelings towards me still the same? I don’t tell you why I ask this. I simply ask it. Whatever your answer is, it cannot affect our friendship, so be quite candid.
> **Cynthia**

I sat down there and then to write my reply. The letter, coming when it did and saying what it said, had affected me profoundly. It was like an unexpected reinforcement in a losing battle. It filled me with a glow of self-confidence. I felt strong again, able to fight and win. My mood bore me away, and I poured out my whole heart to her. I told her that my feelings had not altered, that I loved her and nobody but her. It was a letter, I can see, looking back, born of fretted nerves; but at the time I had no such criticism to make. It seemed to me a true expression of my real feelings.

===Chinese===

信写得极短，口气几乎一本正经：

> **亲爱的彼得：**——我想问你一个问题。可以问得很简短。就是这一句：你对我的感情，还和从前一样吗？我不告诉你我为什么问这个。我只是问了。无论你怎么回答，都影响不到我们的友谊，所以尽管说真心话。
> **辛西娅**

我当场便坐下来写回信。这封信来得恰逢其时，说的又是那样一番话，把我撼动到了极深处。它像一场败局之中不期而至的援兵。它使我周身涨起一团自信的暖光。我又觉得自己有力了，能战，而且能胜。情绪驮着我一路疾走，我把整颗心向她和盘倾出。我告诉她，我的感情不曾改变，我爱的是她，除她之外再无别人。如今回头一看，那是一封从磨焦的神经里生出来的信；可当时我做不出这样的批评。在我看来，它是我真实感情的一次忠实表达。

===Original===

That the fight was not over because in my moment of exaltation I had imagined that I had conquered myself was made uncomfortably plain to me by the thrill that ran through me when, returning from posting my letter, I met Audrey. The sight of her reminded me that a reinforcement is only a reinforcement, a help towards victory, not victory itself.

For the first time I found myself feeling resentful towards her. There was no reason in my resentment. It would not have borne examination. But it was there, and its presence gave me support. I found myself combating the thrill the sight of her had caused, and looking at her with a critical and hostile eye. Who was she that she should enslave a man against his will? Fascination exists only in the imagination of the fascinated. If he have the strength to deny the fascination and convince himself that it does not exist, he is saved. It is purely a matter of willpower and calm reasonableness. There must have been sturdy, levelheaded Egyptian citizens who could not understand what people saw to admire in Cleopatra.

Thus reasoning, I raised my hat, uttered a crisp “Good morning,” and passed on, the very picture of the brisk man of affairs.

===Chinese===

寄信归来，迎面遇上奥黛丽的那一瞬，一阵战栗流遍我的全身——它不痛快地向我和盘托出了一个事实：仗并没有打完，尽管我在得意的一刻曾想象自己已然战胜了自己。她的形貌提醒了我：援兵终究只是援兵，是通向胜利的一臂之助，而不是胜利本身。

我头一回发觉，自己竟对她怀起了怨气。这怨气毫无道理，经不起推究。可它确凿在场，而它在场，便给了我支撑。我发觉自己正在对抗她的形貌激起的那阵战栗，正拿一种挑剔而怀敌意的眼光打量她。她算何许人，竟能违背一个男人的意愿把他奴役了去？魅惑只存在于被魅惑者的想象之中。他若有力量否认这魅惑、说服自己它并不存在，他便是得救了。这纯粹是意志力与冷静的理性问题。古埃及想必也必有几位筋骨结实、头脑冷静的公民，全然不解众人究竟在克利奥帕特拉（Cleopatra）身上看出了哪一点可慕之处。

如此这般推理已毕，我举了举帽子，脆生生道一声「早上好」，便扬长而过，活脱脱一幅干练忙人的写真。

===Original===

“Peter!”

Even the brisk man of affairs must stop when spoken to. Otherwise, apart from any question of politeness, it looks as if he were running away.

Her face was still wearing the faint look of surprise which my manner had called forth.

“You’re in a great hurry.”

I had no answer. She did not appear to expect one.

We moved towards the house in silence, to me oppressive silence. The force of her personality was beginning to beat against my defences, concerning the stability of which, under pressure, a certain uneasiness troubled my mind.

===Chinese===

「彼得！」

干练忙人再怎么忙，人家开口唤他，也总得停下脚步。不然的话，且撇开礼貌不谈，那样子倒像是他在逃跑。

她的脸上，还挂着我那副做派招出来的一抹讶异。

「你忙得很哪。」

我无言以对。她似乎也并不指望对答。

我们朝宅子走去，一路无言——于我，是压迫人的无言。她人格的力量开始拍击我的防线了；至于这条防线在压力之下到底稳当不稳当，已有几分不安在搅扰我的心。

===Original===

“Are you worried about anything, Peter?” she said at last.

“No,” I said. “Why?”

“I was afraid you might be.”

I felt angry with myself. I was mismanaging this thing in the most idiotic way. Instead of this bovine silence, gay small-talk, the easy eloquence, in fact, of the brisk man of affairs should have been my policy. No wonder Smooth Sam Fisher treated me as a child. My whole bearing was that of a sulky schoolboy.

The silence became more oppressive.

We reached the house. In the hall we parted, she to upper regions, I to my classroom. She did not look at me. Her face was cold and offended.

===Chinese===

「你在为什么事发愁吗，彼得？」她终于开口。

「没有，」我说。「怎么啦？」

「我是怕你正发着愁。」

我对自己动起气来。我把这件事办得一塌糊涂，蠢得无以复加。本该拿来当方针的，是轻快的寒暄，是从容的口才——总而言之，是干练忙人的那一套——而不是这副牛一样的沉默。难怪圆滑山姆·费舍尔拿我当小孩子看待。我通身的做派，整个是一个赌气的学童。

沉默愈发压人了。

我们到了宅前。在大厅里两人分手，她往楼上的地界去，我往我的教室去。她不看我。她的脸冷淡，含着见怪。

===Original===

One is curiously inconsistent. Having created what in the circumstances was a most desirable coldness between Audrey and myself, I ought to have been satisfied. Reason told me that this was the best thing that could have happened. Yet joy was one of the few emotions which I did not feel during the days which followed. My brief moment of clear-headedness had passed, and with it the exhilaration that had produced the letter to Cynthia and the resentment which had helped me to reason calmly with myself on the intrinsic nature of fascination in woman. Once more Audrey became the centre of my world. But our friendship, that elusive thing which had contrived to exist side by side with my love, had vanished. There was a breach between us which widened daily. Soon we hardly spoke.

Nothing, in short, could have been more eminently satisfactory, and the fact that I regretted it is only a proof of the essential weakness of my character.

===Chinese===

人真是出奇地自相矛盾。在当时的处境下，我在奥黛丽与自己之间造成了那样一种求之不得的冷淡，本该心满意足。理性告诉我，这是所能发生的再好不过的事。然而欢乐，偏偏是随后那几天里我少数几种不曾感受到的情绪之一。我那一小阵的头脑清明已经过去；随之而去的，还有催生出给辛西娅那封信的亢奋，以及那份助我就「女性魅惑之内在本质」对自己冷静推理过一番的怨气。奥黛丽重新成为我世界的中心。但我们的友谊——那件难以捉摸的东西，曾一路设法与我的爱情比邻共存——消失了。我们之间裂开一道口子，日宽一日。不多时，我们便几乎不交一言了。

简而言之，再没有比这更称心遂意的了；而我居然还为此懊恼，这一事实，只不过再次证明了我性格骨子里的软弱而已。

### XII / XII

#### I / I

===Original===
In those grey days there was one thought, of the many that occupied my mind, which brought with it a certain measure of consolation. It was the reflection that this state of affairs could not last forever. The school term was drawing to a close. Soon I should be free from the propinquity which paralysed my efforts to fight. I was resolved that the last day of term should end forever my connection with Sanstead House and all that was in it. Mrs. Ford must find some other minion. If her happiness depended on the recovery of the Little Nugget, she must learn to do without happiness, like the rest of the inhabitants of this horrible world.

Meanwhile, however, I held myself to be still on duty. By what tortuous processes of thought I had arrived at the conclusion I do not know, but I considered myself responsible to Audrey for the safeguarding of the Little Nugget, and no altered relations between us could affect my position. Perhaps mixed up with this attitude of mind, was the less altruistic wish to foil Smooth Sam. His continued presence at the school was a challenge to me.

Sam’s behaviour puzzled me. I do not know exactly what I expected him to do, but I certainly did not expect him to do nothing. Yet day followed day, and still he made no move. He was the very model of a butler. But our dealings with one another in London had left me vigilant, and his inaction did not disarm me. It sprang from patience, not from any weakening of purpose or despair of success. Sooner or later I knew he would act, swiftly and suddenly, with a plan perfected in every detail.

But when he made his attack it was the very simplicity of his methods that tricked me, and only pure chance defeated him.

===Chinese===
在那些灰暗的日子里，占据我头脑的诸多念头之中，有一个能带来几分慰藉。那便是想到：这样的局面不会永世长存。学期正在接近尾声。不久，我便可脱出那份近在咫尺的煎熬——正是它，瘫掉了我抗争的气力。我已下定决心，学期的最后一天，要把我同桑斯特德宅及宅中一切人事的瓜葛，永远做个了断。福特太太只得另觅一位爪牙。倘若她的幸福系于夺回小金块，那她就得学会缺了幸福过日子——像这个可怕世界上其余的居民一样。

然而与此同时，我仍认为自己尚在值勤之中。我究竟循着怎样九曲十八弯的思路才得出这个结论，我说不上来，但我认定，在保全小金块这件事上，我对奥黛丽负有责任，我们两人之间的关系起任何变化，都动不得我的这个立场。掺在这份心态里的，或许还有一层不那么无私的愿望：挫败圆滑山姆。他赖在学校里不走，便是递到我面前的一纸挑战书。

山姆的行止叫我纳闷。我说不清究竟指望他做什么，但我绝对没指望他无所作为。然而日子一天天过去，他始终按兵不动。他简直是男管家的完满楷模。但我们在伦敦彼此打过的那番交道，使我存了戒心；他的不动，解除不了我的武装。那不动源自耐心，而非决心的松动，也不是对成功断了念。我知道他迟早要动手，迅雷不及掩耳，带着一份每个细节都打磨到家的方案。

可是等到他真的发动进攻，恰恰是那手段的简单质朴骗过了我；而打败他的，纯属碰巧。

===Original===
I have said that it was the custom of the staff of masters at Sanstead House School﻿—in other words, of every male adult in the house except Mr. Fisher himself﻿—to assemble in Mr. Abney’s study after dinner of an evening to drink coffee. It was a ceremony, like most of the ceremonies at an establishment such as a school, where things are run on a schedule, which knew of no variation. Sometimes Mr. Abney would leave us immediately after the ceremony, but he never omitted to take his part in it first.

On this particular evening, for the first time since the beginning of the term, I was seized with a prejudice against coffee. I had been sleeping badly for several nights, and I decided that abstention from coffee might remedy this.

I waited, for form’s sake, till Glossop and Mr. Abney had filled their cups, then went to my room, where I lay down in the dark to wrestle with a more than usually pronounced fit of depression which had descended upon me. Solitude and darkness struck me as the suitable setting for my thoughts.

===Chinese===
我说过，桑斯特德宅学校的教师班子——换句话说，全宅除费舍尔先生本人之外的每一个成年男人——有个惯例：每晚正餐后，聚到阿布尼先生的书房里喝咖啡。同一切按日程表运转的学校这类机构里的大多数仪式一样，这是一项不知变通的仪式。有时阿布尼先生会在仪式一毕立刻离开我们，但仪式本身，他从不曾省略过自己应尽的那一份。

偏偏在这个晚上，学期开始以来头一遭，我对咖啡生出了一种偏见。接连几夜我都睡得很坏，于是我断定，戒除咖啡或许能治好这个毛病。

为了不失体统，我等格拉索普和阿布尼先生都斟满了各自的杯盏，才回自己房里，摸黑躺下，同一阵比往常更加沉重的消沉搏斗——它方才压到了我的头上。孤单与黑暗，在我看来，正配做我这些念头的布景。

===Original===
At this moment Smooth Sam Fisher had no place in my meditations. My mind was not occupied with him at all. When, therefore, the door, which had been ajar, began to open slowly, I did not become instantly on the alert. Perhaps it was some sound, barely audible, that aroused me from my torpor and set my blood tingling with anticipation. Perhaps it was the way the door was opening. An honest draught does not move a door furtively, in jerks.

I sat up noiseless, tense, and alert. And then, very quietly, somebody entered the room.

There was only one person in Sanstead House who would enter a room like that. I was amused. The impudence of the thing tickled me. It seemed so foreign to Mr. Fisher’s usual cautious methods. This strolling in and helping oneself was certainly kidnapping *de luxe*. In the small hours I could have understood it; but at nine o’clock at night, with Glossop, Mr. Abney and myself awake and liable to be met at any moment on the stairs, it was absurd. I marvelled at Smooth Sam’s effrontery.

I lay still. I imagined that, being in, he would switch on the electric light. He did, and I greeted him pleasantly.

===Chinese===
此时此刻，圆滑山姆·费舍尔在我的冥想里没有位置。我脑子里压根儿没想他。因此，当那扇原本虚掩的门开始缓缓开启时，我没有立即警觉起来。把我从昏沉里唤起、叫血液带着预感微微发麻的，也许是一丝勉强可闻的声响。也许是门开启的方式。一阵光明正大的过堂风，不会那样鬼鬼祟祟、一顿一顿地推门。

我悄没声地坐起身来，绷紧了每一根弦，警醒着。接着，有人轻轻悄悄地进了屋。

桑斯特德宅里只有一个人会这样进屋。我给逗乐了。这份胆大包天搔得我心头发痒。这同费舍尔先生平素那套谨慎的章法太不相干了。这样遛达进来自己动手取货，确乎是*豪华级*的绑架。倘在深更半夜，我尚可理解；可这是晚上九点，格拉索普、阿布尼先生和我都还醒着，随时可能在楼梯上撞个正着，这就荒唐了。圆滑山姆的厚脸皮令我叹为观止。

我躺着未动。我料想他既然进来，总要开电灯的。他开了，我便和颜悦色地招呼他。

===Original===
“And what can I do for *you*, Mr. Fisher?”

For a man who had learned to control himself in difficult situations he took the shock badly. He uttered a startled exclamation and spun round, open-mouthed.

I could not help admiring the quickness with which he recovered himself. Almost immediately he was the suave, chatty Sam Fisher who had unbosomed his theories and dreams to me in the train to London.

“I quit,” he said pleasantly. “The episode is closed. I am a man of peace, and I take it that you would not keep on lying quietly on that bed while I went into the other room and abstracted our young friend? Unless you have changed your mind again, would a fifty-fifty offer tempt you?”

===Chinese===
「这回我能替*您*效点什么劳呢，费舍尔先生？」

以一个学会了在难局中克制自己的人而论，这个惊吓他受得相当狼狈。他失声惊呼，霍地旋过身来，嘴巴张着。

我不由得佩服他回神的迅捷。几乎转眼之间，他又成了那个在去伦敦的火车上对我倾吐过理论与梦想的、圆滑健谈的山姆·费舍尔。

「我认输了，」他心情颇佳地说。「这一段插曲就此翻篇。我是个爱太平的人，而且我估摸着，我要是到隔壁屋里把咱们那位年轻朋友顺走，你总不至于还安安静静躺在那张床上吧？除非你又变了主意——来一份对半分账，动不动心？」

===Original===
“Not an inch.”

“Just so. I merely asked.”

“And how about Mr. Abney, in any case? Suppose we met him on the stairs?”

“We should not meet him on the stairs,” said Sam confidently. “You did not take coffee tonight, I gather?”

===Chinese===
「一寸也不。」

「正是。我不过问一声。」

「那么阿布尼先生呢，怎么说？万一咱们在楼梯上撞见他？」

「咱们不会在楼梯上撞见他，」山姆信心十足地说。「我估摸着，今晚你没喝咖啡吧？」

===Original===
“I didn’t﻿—no. Why?”

He jerked his head resignedly.

“Can you beat it! I ask you, young man, could I have foreseen that, after drinking coffee every night regularly for two months, you would pass it up tonight of all nights? You certainly are my jinx, sonny. You have hung the Indian sign on me all right.”

His words had brought light to me.

“Did you drug the coffee?”

“Did I! I fixed it so that one sip would have an insomnia patient in dreamland before he had time to say ‘Good night.’ That stuff Rip Van Winkle drank had nothing on my coffee. And all wasted! Well, well!”

===Chinese===
「没喝——是的。怎么了？」

他认命似的猛一甩头。

「你说邪门不邪门！我问你，年轻人，一个人每晚规规矩矩喝了整整两个月的咖啡，偏偏今晚——千挑万选挑中今晚——不喝了，这叫我如何未卜先知？你真是我命里的扫把星，小老弟。你算是把印第安符咒牢牢贴到我身上了。」

他这话给我送来了光亮。

「你在咖啡里下了药？」

「怎么没下！我在里头做了手脚，只消一口，失眠病人来不及说完一声『晚安』，人已在梦乡。瑞普·凡·温克尔（Rip Van Winkle）当年喝的那玩意儿，比起我这份咖啡也要甘拜下风。可全白瞎了！唉，唉！」

===Original===
He turned towards the door.

“Shall I leave the light on, or would you prefer it off?”

“On please. I might fall asleep in the dark.”

“Not you! And, if you did, you would dream that I was there, and wake up. There are moments, young man, when you bring me pretty near to quitting and taking to honest work.”

===Chinese===
他转身朝门口走去。

「灯我给你留着呢，还是你乐意我关掉？」

「留着吧，劳驾。黑灯瞎火的，我说不定倒睡着了。」

「你才睡不着呢！真睡着了，也会梦见我在跟前，一惊就醒。年轻人，有些时刻，你简直把我逼得快要洗手不干、改投正经营生了。」

===Original===
He paused.

“But not altogether. I have still a shot or two in my locker. We shall see what we shall see. I am not dead yet. Wait!”

“I will, and some day, when I am walking along Piccadilly, a passing automobile will splash me with mud. A heavily furred plutocrat will stare haughtily at me from the tonneau, and with a start of surprise I shall recognize﻿—”

“Stranger things have happened. Be flip while you can, sonny. You win so far, but this hoodoo of mine can’t last forever.”

===Chinese===
他顿了顿。

「不过，也还没到那一步。我弹药箱里还存着一两发子弹。咱们走着瞧，且看下回分解。我还没死呢。等着！」

「我等着。而且早晚有一天，我正沿着皮卡迪利散步，一辆过路的汽车会溅我一身泥。一位裹着厚毛皮的财阀，将从车后座上倨傲地瞪我一眼，而我会猛吃一惊，认出那是——」

「比这更离奇的事都有过。趁现在还能耍嘴皮子，尽管耍吧，小老弟。到目前为止是你赢了，可我这场霉运，总不能背到地老天荒。」

===Original===
He passed from the room with a certain sad dignity. A moment later he reappeared.

“A thought strikes me,” he said. “The fifty-fifty proposition does not impress you. Would it make things easier if I were to offer my cooperation for a mere quarter of the profit?”

“Not in the least.”

“It’s a handsome offer.”

“Wonderfully. I’m afraid I’m not dealing on any terms.”

===Chinese===
他带着几分哀伤的庄严，出了这间房。片刻之后，他又出现了。

「我忽然想到一件事，」他说。「对半分账的方案打动不了你。那要是把我的合作，降到只求四分之一的利润，事情会不会好办些？」

「丝毫不。」

「这可是份大方的开价。」

「大方得不得了。只怕我什么条件都不谈。」

===Original===
He left the room, only to return once more. His head appeared, staring at me round the door, in a disembodied way, like the Cheshire Cat.

“You won’t say later on I didn’t give you your chance?” he said anxiously.

He vanished again, permanently this time. I heard his steps passing down the stairs.

===Chinese===
他出了房，却又回来了一趟。他的脑袋从门边探出来瞪着我，一副身首分离的架势，活像柴郡猫（Cheshire Cat）。

「你日后总不会说，我没给过你机会吧？」他焦灼地问。

他又消失了，这一回是永久性的。我听见他的脚步声一路下了楼梯。

#### II / II

===Original===
We had now arrived at the last week of term, at the last days of the last week. The holiday spirit was abroad in the school. Among the boys it took the form of increased disorderliness. Boys who had hitherto only made Glossop bellow now made him perspire and tear his hair as well. Boys who had merely spilt ink now broke windows. The Little Nugget abandoned cigarettes in favour of an old clay pipe which he had found in the stables.

As for me, I felt like a spent swimmer who sees the shore almost within his reach. Audrey avoided me when she could, and was frigidly polite when we met. But I suffered less now. A few more days, and I should have done with this phase of my life forever, and Audrey would once more become a memory.

Complete quiescence marked the deportment of Mr. Fisher during these days. He did not attempt to repeat his last effort. The coffee came to the study unmixed with alien drugs. Sam, like lightning, did not strike twice in the same place. He had the artist’s soul, and disliked patching up bungled work. If he made another move, it would, I knew, be on entirely fresh lines.

Ignoring the fact that I had had all the luck, I was inclined to be self-satisfied when I thought of Sam. I had pitted my wits against his, and I had won. It was a praiseworthy performance for a man who had done hitherto nothing particular in his life.

===Chinese===
我们如今已到了学期的最后一周，最后一周的最后几天。放假的情绪在学校里四处飘荡。落到男孩们身上，这情绪表现为变本加厉的无法无天。先前只惹得格拉索普吼叫的孩子，如今还添上了让他冒汗揪头发的功效。先前不过泼洒墨水的孩子，如今改砸窗户了。小金块撇开了纸烟，改抽一支在马厩里觅得的旧陶土烟斗。

至于我，我觉得自己像个气力耗尽的泳者，眼看着海岸几乎伸手可及。奥黛丽能避我时便避，避不开碰上面时，客气得冷若冰霜。但我眼下受的罪轻些了。再熬几天，我便可以同人生的这个阶段永远清账，奥黛丽也将重新变回一段回忆。

这几天里，费舍尔先生的行止以彻底的静止为标志。他没有企图重演上一回的手筋。送进书房的咖啡，再不掺任何外来的药物。山姆同闪电一样，从不在同一处击打两次。他怀有艺术家的灵魂，不爱缝补砸了锅的活计。他若再动，我知道，必定是全然崭新的路数。

全不理会占尽运气的其实是我这一事实，一想到山姆，我便有几分自满。我同他斗了智，并且赢了。对一个此前一生没干成过什么名堂的人而言，这是一场值得称道的演出。

===Original===
If all the copybook maxims which had been drilled into me in my childhood and my early disaster with Audrey had not been sufficient, I ought to have been warned by Sam’s advice not to take victory for granted till the fight was over. As Sam had said, his luck would turn sooner or later.

One realizes these truths in theory, but the practical application of them seldom fails to come as a shock. I received mine on the last morning but one of the term.

Shortly after breakfast a message was brought to me that Mr. Abney would like to see me in his study. I went without any sense of disaster to come. Most of the business of the school was discussed in the study after breakfast, and I imagined that the matter had to do with some detail of the morrow’s exodus.

I found Mr. Abney pacing the room, a look of annoyance on his face. At the desk, her back to me, Audrey was writing. It was part of her work to take charge of the business correspondence of the establishment. She did not look round when I came in, nor when Mr. Abney spoke my name, but went on writing as if I did not exist.

===Chinese===
倘若童年时灌进我脑袋的那一整套习字簿格言，外加我同奥黛丽那场早期的灾难，都还不足以警戒我，那么山姆的忠告——仗没打完，休把胜利当作囊中之物——总该叫我清醒了。诚如山姆所言，他的运气迟早要转。

这些道理，人在理论上个个都懂，可它们一到实际应用，几乎没有不把人吓一跳的。我那一跳，来在学期结束前的倒数第二个早晨。

早饭后不一会儿，有人给我捎来口信，说阿布尼先生想在书房见我。我去时没有半点大祸将至的预感。学校的事务多半在早饭后于书房里商议，我料想这桩事同明日大撤离的某项细节有关。

我见阿布尼先生正在屋里踱步，脸上带着愠色。书桌前，奥黛丽背对着我，正写着字。掌管这所机构的往来商务函件，本是她的职分之一。我进屋时她没有回头，阿布尼先生念出我名字时她也没有回头，只管写下去，仿佛我并不存在。

===Original===
There was a touch of embarrassment in Mr. Abney’s manner, for which I could not at first account. He was stately, but with the rather defensive stateliness which marked his announcements that he was about to pop up to London and leave me to do his work. He coughed once or twice before proceeding to the business of the moment.

“Ah, Mr. Burns,” he said at length, “might I ask if your plans for the holidays, the﻿—ah﻿—earlier part of the holidays are settled? No? ah﻿—excellent.”

He produced a letter from the heap of papers on the desk.

“Ah﻿—excellent. That simplifies matters considerably. I have no right to ask what I am about to﻿—ah﻿—in fact ask. I have no claim on your time in the holidays. But, in the circumstances, perhaps you may see your way to doing me a considerable service. I have received a letter from Mr. Elmer Ford which puts me in a position of some difficulty. It is not my wish﻿—indeed, it is foreign to my policy﻿—to disoblige the parents of the boys who are entrusted to my﻿—ah﻿—care, and I should like, if possible, to do what Mr. Ford asks. It appears that certain business matters call him to the north of England for a few days, this rendering it impossible for him to receive little Ogden tomorrow. It is not my custom to criticize parents who have paid me the compliment of placing their sons at the most malleable and important period of their lives, in my﻿—ah﻿—charge, but I must say that a little longer notice would have been a﻿—in fact, a convenience. But Mr. Ford, like so many of his countrymen, is what I believe is called a hustler. He does it now, as the expression is. In short, he wishes to leave little Ogden at the school for the first few days of the holidays, and I should be extremely obliged, Mr. Burns, if you should find it possible to stay here and﻿—ah﻿—look after him.”

===Chinese===
阿布尼先生的态度里带着一丝窘迫，起初我说不出个所以然。他仪态庄严，但那庄严颇含守势，同他宣布自己即将蹿上伦敦、把工作撂给我代办时的庄严，如出一辙。转入眼下的正题之前，他咳嗽了一两声。

「啊，伯恩斯先生，」他终于开了口，「我可否一问，你假期——啊——假期前半段的计划，定下了没有？没有？啊——甚好。」

他从桌上一堆文件里抽出一封信。

「啊——甚好。这样一来，事情便简单多了。我本无权过问我即将——啊——事实上要问的事。假期里，你的时间我无权支配。但在此情形下，你或许愿意设法帮我一个大忙。我收到埃尔默·福特先生一封来信，把我搁进了相当为难的境地。让家长不痛快，本非我所愿——也确实有违我的方针——他们把儿子在最可塑、最要紧的人生阶段托付到我这——啊——照管之下，我总希望能尽量照福特先生的请求办。似乎是几桩生意上的事务，要召他往英格兰北部去几天，害得他明日无法接待小奥格登。对这样抬爱、把儿子托付到我这——啊——照管之下的家长，我素来不加指摘，但我必须说，稍稍提早一点知会，于事——事实上，于便利大有补益。不过福特先生，同他的许多同胞一样，是我相信人们所谓『说干就干』的人物。用那句口号讲，他讲究『现在就办』。简而言之，他希望让小奥格登留校度过假期的头几天，而你，伯恩斯先生，倘若觉得有可能留下照——啊——看护他，我将感激不尽。」

===Original===
Audrey stopped writing and turned in her chair, the first intimation she had given that she had heard Mr. Abney’s remarks.

“It really won’t be necessary to trouble Mr. Burns,” she said, without looking at me. “I can take care of Ogden very well by myself.”

“In the case of an﻿—ah﻿—ordinary boy, Mrs. Sheridan, I should not hesitate to leave you in sole charge as you have very kindly offered to stay and help me in this matter. But we must recollect not only﻿—I speak frankly﻿—not only the peculiar﻿—ah﻿—disposition of this particular lad, but also the fact that those ruffians who visited the house that night may possibly seize the opportunity to make a fresh attack. I should not feel﻿—ah﻿—justified in thrusting so heavy a responsibility upon you.”

There was reason in what he said. Audrey made no reply. I heard her pen tapping on the desk and deduced her feelings. I, myself, felt like a prisoner who, having filed through the bars of his cell, is removed to another on the eve of escape. I had so braced myself up to endure till the end of term and no longer that this postponement of the day of release had a crushing effect.

===Chinese===
奥黛丽停了笔，在椅子里转过身来——这是她头一回表示，她听见了阿布尼先生的话。

「实在不必劳烦伯恩斯先生，」她说，眼睛不朝我看。「照顾奥格登，我一个人完全照管得来。」

「倘若是位——啊——寻常的孩子，谢里丹太太，您既如此好意，提出留下在此事上帮我，我本会毫不犹豫把全责交给您。但我们必须记挂的，不单是——我直言相告——不单是这位少年那古怪——啊——脾性，还有那一节：那晚光临过本宅的那伙歹徒，说不定会抓住机会再来一回攻击。把这样一付重担压到您肩上，我——啊——于心不安。」

他的话在理。奥黛丽没有作声。我听见她的笔在桌面上笃笃地敲，由此推断出她的心境。至于我自己，我觉得像个囚犯，好不容易锉断了牢房的铁栏，却在越狱的前夜被移进了另一间。我原本咬紧牙关，只打算撑到学期结束、多一天也不撑，这一来释放之日横遭推迟，对我便有一种粉碎性的打击。

===Original===
Mr. Abney coughed and lowered his voice confidentially.

“I would stay myself, but the fact is, I am called to London on very urgent business, and shall be unable to return for a day or so. My late pupil, the﻿—ah﻿—the Earl of Buxton, has been﻿—I can rely on your discretion, Mr. Burns﻿—has been in trouble with the authorities at Eton, and his guardian, an old college friend of mine﻿—the﻿—in fact, the Duke of Bessborough, who, rightly or wrongly, places﻿—er﻿—considerable reliance on my advice, is anxious to consult me on the matter. I shall return as soon as possible, but you will readily understand that, in the circumstances, my time will not be my own. I must place myself unreservedly at﻿—ah﻿—Bessborough’s disposal.”

He pressed the bell.

“In the event of your observing any suspicious characters in the neighbourhood, you have the telephone and can instantly communicate with the police. And you will have the assistance of﻿—”

===Chinese===
阿布尼先生咳嗽一声，推心置腹地压低了嗓音。

「我本愿亲自留下，无奈实情是，伦敦有十万火急的事务召我前去，一天半载回不来。我从前的学生，那位——啊——那位巴克斯顿伯爵（Earl of Buxton），一直在——此事我可以仰仗你的口风，伯恩斯先生——一直在伊顿（Eton）同校方惹着麻烦，而他的监护人，我的一位大学老友——那位——确切地说，贝斯伯勒公爵（Duke of Bessborough），不论对错，对我的忠告——呃——颇为倚重，急欲就此事咨询于我。我将尽早赶回，但你不难理解，在此情形下，我的时间由不得我自己。我必须把自己毫无保留地交由——啊——贝斯伯勒差遣。」

他按了铃。

「万一你们在左近发现任何形迹可疑之徒，屋里有电话，可以立刻同警方联络。而且你们还将得到——」

===Original===
The door opened and Smooth Sam Fisher entered.

“You rang, sir?”

“Ah! Come in, White, and close the door. I have something to say to you. I have just been informing Mr. Burns that Mr. Ford has written asking me to allow his son to stay on at the school for the first few days of the vacation.”

He turned to Audrey.

“You will doubtless be surprised, Mrs. Sheridan, and possibly﻿—ah﻿—somewhat startled, to learn the peculiar nature of White’s position at Sanstead House. You have no objection to my informing Mrs. Sheridan, White, in consideration of the fact that you will be working together in this matter? Just so. White is a detective in the employment of Pinkerton’s Agency. Mr. Ford”﻿—a slight frown appeared on his lofty brow﻿—“Mr. Ford obtained his present situation for him in order that he might protect his son in the event of﻿—ah﻿—in fact, any attempt to remove him.”

===Chinese===
门开了，圆滑山姆·费舍尔走了进来。

「您按铃了，先生？」

「啊！进来，怀特，把门关上。我有话对你说。我方才正告知伯恩斯先生，福特先生来信，请求我准许他儿子留校度过假期的头几天。」

他转向奥黛丽。

「您无疑会感到惊讶，谢里丹太太，还可能——啊——多少有些骇异，当您得知怀特在桑斯特德宅那特殊的身份。念及你们二位将在此事中协同办事，怀特，我想你不至于反对我把这层告诉谢里丹太太吧？正是。怀特是受雇于平克顿侦探社的一名侦探。福特先生」——他那高耸的额头上微微蹙起一道纹——「福特先生替他谋得了眼下的职位，为的是一旦有——啊——一旦有事实上任何移走他儿子的企图，他好从中保护他的儿子。」

===Original===
I saw Audrey start. A quick flush came into her face. She uttered a little exclamation of astonishment.

“Just so,” said Mr. Abney, by way of comment on this. “You are naturally surprised. The whole arrangement is excessively unusual, and, I may say﻿—ah﻿—disturbing. However, you have your duty to fulfil to your employer, White, and you will, of course, remain here with the boy.”

“Yes, sir.”

I found myself looking into a bright brown eye that gleamed with genial triumph. The other was closed. In the exuberance of the moment, Smooth Sam had had the bad taste to wink at me.

“You will have Mr. Burns to help you, White. He has kindly consented to postpone his departure during the short period in which I shall be compelled to be absent.”

===Chinese===
我看见奥黛丽一震。一阵红晕飞快涌上她的脸。她发出一声轻轻的惊叹。

「正是，」阿布尼先生说，权当是对此的评注。「您自然要惊讶。这整套安排极不寻常，而且我可以说——啊——扰人心神。不过，怀特，你对你的雇主负有应尽的职责，你当然要同这孩子一起留下。」

「是，先生。」

我发觉自己正对上一只亮晶晶的棕色眼睛，眼里闪着和蔼的得胜光芒。另一只眼睛闭着。一时忘形之下，圆滑山姆竟大煞风景地朝我挤了挤眼。

「你还将有伯恩斯先生从旁协助，怀特。他已慨然应允，在我不得不离开的这段短短的日子里，推迟自己的行期。」

===Original===
I had no recollection of having given any kind consent, but I was very willing to have it assumed, and I was glad to see that Mr. Fisher, though Mr. Abney did not observe it, was visibly taken aback by this piece of information. But he made one of his swift recoveries.

“It is very kind of Mr. Burns,” he said in his fruitiest voice, “but I hardly think it will be necessary to put him to the inconvenience of altering his plans. I am sure that Mr. Ford would prefer the entire charge of the affair to be in my hands.”

He had not chosen a happy moment for the introduction of the millionaire’s name. Mr. Abney was a man of method, who hated any dislocation of the fixed routine of life; and Mr. Ford’s letter had upset him. The Ford family, father and son, were just then extremely unpopular with him.

He crushed Sam.

===Chinese===
我全不记得自己慨然应允过什么，但我很乐意让人这样认定；而且我高兴地看到，费舍尔先生——虽说阿布尼先生未曾察觉——被这条消息结结实实地吓了一跳。不过他随即来了个他的招牌式迅速复原。

「伯恩斯先生真是太好心了，」他用他那副最醇美的嗓音说，「不过我以为，实在不必劳他改动计划，平添不便。我确信，福特先生更愿意此案的整个管辖，都握在我一个人的手里。」

他偏偏挑了个不巧的时机，抬出这位百万富翁的名号。阿布尼先生是个行事有章法的人，最恨固定生活日程横遭扰乱；而福特先生的来信正把他搅得心绪不宁。福特父子，此时在他那里都极不得人心。

他把山姆碾了个粉碎。

===Original===
“What Mr. Ford would or would not prefer is, in this particular matter, beside the point. The responsibility for the boy, while he remains on the school premises, is﻿—ah﻿—mine, and I shall take such precautions as seem fit and adequate to﻿—him﻿—myself, irrespective of those which, in your opinion, might suggest themselves to Mr. Ford. As I cannot be here myself, owing to﻿—ah﻿—urgent business in London, I shall certainly take advantage of Mr. Burns’s kind offer to remain as my deputy.”

He paused and blew his nose, his invariable custom after these occasional outbursts of his. Sam had not wilted beneath the storm. He waited, unmoved, till all was over:

“I am afraid I shall have to be more explicit,” he said: “I had hoped to avoid scandal and unpleasantness, but I see it is impossible.”

===Chinese===
「福特先生中意什么、不中意什么，就眼下这桩事而言，无关宏旨。这孩子只要还在本校地界之内，责任便——啊——是我的，自当由——呃——由我本人，采取我认为妥当而充分的防范措施，而不必理会照你看来，福特先生或许会属意的那一些。既然我本人因——啊——伦敦的紧急事务无法在此，我当然要领受伯恩斯先生慨然留任我代理的这番好意。」

他顿了顿，擤了擤鼻子——每逢这几回难得的发作之后，他例必如此。山姆没有被这场风暴打蔫。他岿然不动，静候一切归于平息：

「恐怕我只好把话说得更露骨些，」他说。「我本指望避免丑闻和不愉快，但我看，这是不可能了。」

===Original===
Mr. Abney’s astonished face emerged slowly from behind his handkerchief.

“I quite agree with you, sir, that somebody should be here to help me look after the boy, but not Mr. Burns. I am sorry to have to say it, but I do not trust Mr. Burns.”

Mr. Abney’s look of astonishment deepened. I, too, was surprised. It was so unlike Sam to fling away his chances on a blundering attack like this.

“What do you mean?” demanded Mr. Abney.

“Mr. Burns is after the boy himself. He came to kidnap him.”

===Chinese===
阿布尼先生那张惊愕的脸，从手帕后面缓缓浮现出来。

「我完全同意您的话，先生，此地理应留一个人，帮我照看这孩子——但不能是伯恩斯先生。我很抱歉不得不这么说，可我信不过伯恩斯先生。」

阿布尼先生的惊愕之色加深了。我也感到意外。拿这样一场莽撞的乱攻去糟蹋自己的胜算，太不似山姆的为人。

「你这是什么意思？」阿布尼先生厉声质问。

「伯恩斯先生自己就在打这孩子的主意。他就是为绑架这孩子才来的。」

===Original===
Mr. Abney, as he had every excuse for doing, grunted with amazement. I achieved the ringing laugh of amused innocence. It was beyond me to fathom Sam’s mind. He could not suppose that any credence would be given to his wild assertion. It seemed to me that disappointment had caused him momentarily to lose his head.

“Are you mad, White?”

“No, sir. I can prove what I say. If I had not gone to London with him that last time, he’d have got away with the boy then, for certain.”

For an instant an uneasy thought came to me that he might have something in reserve, something unknown to me, which had encouraged him to this direct attack. I dismissed the notion. There could be nothing.

===Chinese===
阿布尼先生发出一声惊愕的咕噜——他有一百个理由如此。我则挤出了一串被逗乐的无辜者的朗朗大笑。山姆的心思，我实在摸不透。他总不至于指望有谁会信他这句疯断言。在我看来，是失望叫他一时昏了头。

「你疯了吗，怀特？」

「没疯，先生。我说的话，拿得出证据。上一回要不是我跟他去了伦敦，那一回他准就把孩子带跑了，十拿九稳。」

有一瞬间，一个不安的念头掠过我心头：他手里或许还压着一张牌，一件我所不知的东西，才给他壮了这场直攻的胆。我把这念头挥开了。不可能有。

===Original===
Mr. Abney had turned to me with a look of hopeless bewilderment. I raised my eyebrows.

“Ridiculous,” I said.

That this was the only comment seemed to be Mr. Abney’s view. He turned on Sam with the pettish anger of the mild man.

“What do you *mean*, White, by coming to me with such a preposterous story?”

===Chinese===
阿布尼先生已转向我，一脸无计可施的迷乱。我扬了扬眉毛。

「荒唐，」我说。

「荒唐」二字便是仅有的评语——这似乎也正是阿布尼先生的见解。他带着温和人发作起来时那股愠怒，转身抢白山姆。

「你到底安的*什么*心，怀特，拿这样一段荒诞不经的鬼话，到我面前来献？」

===Original===
“I don’t say Mr. Burns wished to kidnap the boy in the ordinary way,” said Sam imperturbably, “like those men who came that night. He had a special reason. Mr. and Mrs. Ford, as of course you know, sir, are divorced. Mr. Burns was trying to get the boy away and take him back to his mother.”

I heard Audrey give a little gasp. Mr. Abney’s anger became modified by a touch of doubt. I could see that these words, by lifting the accusation from the wholly absurd to the somewhat plausible, had impressed him. Once again I was gripped by the uneasy feeling that Sam had an unsuspected card to play. This might be bluff, but it had a sinister ring.

“You might say,” went on Sam smoothly, “that this was creditable to Mr. Burns’s heart. But, from my employer’s viewpoint and yours, too, it was a chivalrous impulse that needed to be checked. Will you please read this, sir?”

He handed a letter to Mr. Abney, who adjusted his glasses and began to read﻿—at first in a detached, judicial way, then with startled eagerness.

===Chinese===
「我并不是说，伯恩斯先生想照寻常的路子绑这孩子，」山姆不动声色地说，「像那晚来的那伙人那样。他有一个特殊的理由。福特先生和福特太太，您自然是知道的，先生，离了婚。伯恩斯先生一直在设法把孩子弄走，好把他送回他母亲身边。」

我听见奥黛丽轻轻倒抽了一口气。阿布尼先生的怒气，被掺进去的一丝疑虑冲淡了几分。我看得出，这几句话把指控从十足荒唐的境地，抬高到了颇有几分可信的境地，打动了他。山姆手里还有一张没人料到的牌——那阵不安的感觉又一次攫住了我。这也许是在唬人，可它带着一股凶险的音色。

「您或许要说，」山姆圆滑地接了下去，「这于伯恩斯先生的心肠，是一桩增光的事。可是，从我的雇主的立场看，也从您的立场看，那是一股必须加以遏制的侠义冲动。请您过目这个，先生，劳驾。」

他递给阿布尼先生一封信。后者扶正眼镜，读了起来——起初一副超然的法官做派，继而带着吃惊的急切。

===Original===
“I felt it necessary to search among Mr. Burns’s papers, sir, in the hope of finding﻿—”

And then I knew what he had found. From the first the blue-grey notepaper had had a familiar look. I recognized it now. It was Cynthia’s letter, that damning document which I had been mad enough to read to him in London. His prediction that the luck would change had come amazingly true.

I caught Sam’s eye. For the second time he was unfeeling enough to wink. It was a rich, comprehensive wink, as expressive and joyous as a college yell.

Mr. Abney had absorbed the letter and was struggling for speech. I could appreciate his emotion. If he had not actually been nurturing a viper in his bosom, he had come, from his point of view, very near it. Of all men, a schoolmaster necessarily looks with the heartiest dislike on the would-be kidnapper.

As for me, my mind was in a whirl. I was entirely without a plan, without the very beginnings of a plan, to help me cope with this appalling situation. I was crushed by a sense of the utter helplessness of my position. To denounce Sam was impossible; to explain my comparative innocence was equally out of the question. The suddenness of the onslaught had deprived me of the power of coherent thought. I was routed.

===Chinese===
「我觉得有必要，在伯恩斯先生的文件里搜查一番，先生，指望能找出——」

于是我明白他找出的是什么了。那张蓝灰色的信纸，打一开头便透着眼熟。此刻我认出了它。那是辛西娅的信，那份我在伦敦竟疯得念给他听过的、要命的文件。他那句运气将转的预言，灵验得惊人。

我撞上山姆的目光。他第二回狠下心肠，朝我挤了挤眼。那一挤饱满而包罗万象，其表情之丰富、兴致之欢腾，赛得过一嗓子大学生拉拉队的呐喊。

阿布尼先生消化完了那封信，正挣扎着寻找言语。他的激动，我能够体谅。即便说他还不曾真的在胸口揣养过一条毒蛇，照他自己的看法，也已相差无几。天下的人里头，当校长的看那意图绑架之徒，必然怀着最由衷的厌恶。

至于我，脑子里一团乱麻。要应付这骇人的局面，我全然没有一个计划，连一个计划的开头都没有。处境彻底无依无靠的感觉，把我压垮了。揭发山姆，不可能；解释我相对的无辜，同样办不到。这场突袭的迅雷之势，剥夺了我连贯思考的能力。我全线溃败了。

===Original===
Mr. Abney was speaking.

“Is your name Peter, Mr. Burns?”

I nodded. Speech was beyond me.

“This letter is written by﻿—ah﻿—by a lady. It asks you in set terms to﻿—ah﻿—hasten to kidnap Ogden Ford. Do you wish me to read it to you? Or do you confess to knowing its contents?”

He waited for a reply. I had none to make.

===Chinese===
阿布尼先生开口了。

「你名叫彼得吗，伯恩斯先生？」

我点了点头。言语是我力所不及的。

「这封信出自——啊——出自一位女士之手。它以明确的措辞，要求你——啊——从速绑架奥格登·福特。你要我念给你听吗？还是你招认，知道信里的内容？」

他等候答复。我无可奉告。

===Original===
“You do not deny that you came to Sanstead House for the deliberate purpose of kidnapping Ogden Ford?”

I had nothing to say. I caught a glimpse of Audrey’s face, cold and hard, and shifted my eyes quickly. Mr. Abney gulped. His face wore the reproachful expression of a codfish when jerked out of the water on the end of a line. He stared at me with pained repulsion. That scoundrelly old buccaneer Sam did the same. He looked like a shocked bishop.

“I﻿—ah﻿—trusted you implicitly,” said Mr. Abney.

Sam wagged his head at me reproachfully. With a flicker of spirit I glared at him. He only wagged the more.

It was, I think, the blackest moment of my life. A wild desire for escape on any terms surged over me. That look on Audrey’s face was biting into my brain like an acid.

===Chinese===
「你并不否认，你来桑斯特德宅，正是抱着绑架奥格登·福特的既定目的？」

我无话可说。我瞥见奥黛丽的脸，又冷又硬，赶紧把目光挪开。阿布尼先生哽了一下。他的脸上，带着鳕鱼被钓线猛地拽出水面时那副责备的表情。他怀着痛心的嫌恶瞪着我。那个恶棍老海盗山姆也照样瞪着。他活像一位骇然的主教。

「我——啊——先前对你是深信不疑的，」阿布尼先生说。

山姆责备似的朝我一摇一摇地颔首。我凭着一星残存的气性瞪了他一眼。他反而摇得愈发起劲。

我想，那是我此生最黑暗的时刻。一股不惜任何代价、只求脱身的狂愿，从我头顶漫过。奥黛丽脸上那副神情，正像酸液一般，咬进我的脑子。

===Original===
“I will go and pack,” I said.

“This is the end of all things,” I said to myself.

I had suspended my packing in order to sit on my bed and brood. I was utterly depressed. There are crises in a man’s life when Reason fails to bring the slightest consolation. In vain I tried to tell myself that what had happened was, in essence, precisely what, twenty-four hours ago, I was so eager to bring about. It amounted to this, that now, at last, Audrey had definitely gone out of my life. From now on I could have no relations with her of any sort. Was not this exactly what, twenty-four hours ago, I had wished? Twenty-four hours ago had I not said to myself that I would go away and never see her again? Undoubtedly. Nevertheless, I sat there and groaned in spirit.

It was the end of all things.

===Chinese===
「我去收拾行李，」我说。

「这是一切的终结，」我对自己说。

我中止了收拾行李，坐到床沿上发闷。我沮丧到了极点。人的一生中有些关口，理性连一丝一毫的安慰也拿不出来。我徒劳地想告诉自己：眼下发生的事，就实质而言，恰恰是二十四小时前我那样急切巴望促成的。归结起来便是：如今，奥黛丽总算确凿无误地走出了我的生活。从今往后，我同她再不能有任何往来。而这，不正是二十四小时前我所期望的吗？二十四小时前，我难道不曾对自己说，我要远走，从此不再见她？毫无疑问。然而，我还是坐在那儿，心里一声声地哀号。

这就是一切的终结。

===Original===
A mild voice interrupted my meditations.

“Can I help?”

Sam was standing in the doorway, beaming on me with invincible good-humour.

“You are handling them wrong. Allow me. A moment more and you would have ruined the crease.”

I became aware of a pair of trousers hanging limply in my grasp. He took them from me, and, folding them neatly, placed them in my trunk.

===Chinese===
一个温和的嗓音打断了我的冥想。

「要帮忙吗？」

山姆正站在门口，带着战无不胜的好脾气，冲我满面堆笑。

「你叠的手法不对。让我来。再晚一刻，这条裤线就要毁在你手里了。」

我这才察觉，自己手里正软塌塌地提着一条裤子。他从我手中接过，叠得方方正正，放进了我的行李箱。

===Original===
“Don’t get all worked up about it, sonny,” he said. “It’s the fortune of war. Besides, what does it matter to you? Judging by that very snug apartment in London, you have quite enough money for a young man. Losing your job here won’t break you. And, if you’re worrying about Mrs. Ford and her feelings, don’t! I guess she’s probably forgotten all about the Nugget by this time. So cheer up. *You’re* all right!”

He stretched out a hand to pat me on the shoulder, then thought better of it and drew it back.

“Think of *my* happiness, if you want something to make you feel good. Believe me, young man, it’s *some*. I could sing! Gee, when I think that it’s all plain sailing now and no more troubles, I could dance! You don’t know what it means to me, putting through this deal. I wish you knew Mary! That’s her name. You must come and visit us, sonny, when we’re fixed up in the home. There’ll always be a knife and fork for *you*. We’ll make you one of the family! Lord! I can see the place as plain as I can see you. Nice frame house with a good porch.﻿ ﻿… Me in a rocker in my shirtsleeves, smoking a cigar and reading the baseball news; Mary in another rocker, mending my socks and nursing the cat! We’ll sure have a cat. Two cats. I like cats. And a goat in the front garden. Say, it’ll be *great*!”

And on the word, emotion overcoming prudence, he brought his fat hand down with a resounding smack on my bowed shoulders.

===Chinese===
「别为这事上火，小老弟，」他说。「打仗嘛，总有输赢。再说，这与你有何相干？照你在伦敦那套舒舒服服的公寓来判断，年纪轻轻，你的钱已尽够使了。在这儿丢了差事，也塌不了你的台。还有，你要是在替福特太太和她的心情操心——大可不必！我估摸着，这会儿她多半早把小金块忘到脑后了。所以，乐观点儿。*你*没事儿！」

他伸出一只手来拍我的肩膀，转念一想又觉不妥，缩了回去。

「要是想找点什么宽宽心，就想想*我的*幸福。相信我，年轻人，那可*了不得*。我都能唱歌！乖乖，一想到如今一帆风顺，再没有磕绊，我都能跳个舞！这票买卖做成，对我意味着什么，你是不知道的。真希望你能认识认识玛丽（Mary）！她叫这个名。等我们把家安顿妥了，你务必上我们家做客，小老弟。家里永远给*你*留着一副刀叉。咱们把你当自家人！老天爷！那地方我能看得清清楚楚，就像看见你本人一样真切。一座漂亮的木板房，带一个像样的门廊。……我穿着衬衫坐在摇椅里，抽着雪茄，看棒球新闻；玛丽坐在另一把摇椅里，给我补袜子，怀里搂着猫！咱们准得养一只猫。两只。我喜欢猫。前院再养一只山羊。嘿，那日子才叫*美*呢！」

话音落地，感情压倒了审慎，他抬起那只胖手，带着一声脆响，一巴掌拍在我低垂的肩膀上。

===Original===
There is a limit. I bounded to my feet.

“Get out!” I yelped. “Get out of here!”

“Sure,” he replied agreeably. He rose without haste and regarded me compassionately. “Cheer up, son! Be a sport!”

There are moments when the best of men become melodramatic. I offer this as excuse for my next observation.

===Chinese===
忍耐是有限度的。我一蹦而起。

「滚出去！」我尖声嚷道。「给我滚出去！」

「行啊，」他和和气气地应道。他从容不迫地起了身，怜悯地望着我。「乐观点，孩子！做个输得起的好汉！」

再好的人，也有堕入情节剧腔调的时刻。我把这话摆出来，权当我下一句发言的托辞。

===Original===
Clenching my fists and glaring at him, I cried, “I’ll foil you yet, you hound!”

Some people have no soul for the dramatic. He smiled tolerantly.

“Sure,” he said. “Anything you like, Desperate Desmond. Enjoy yourself!”

And he left me.

===Chinese===
我攥紧双拳，怒目而视，喊道：「我总有一天要挫败你的，你这狗东西！」

有些人天生没有戏剧的灵魂。他宽厚地微微一笑。

「行啊，」他说。「随你怎么说都成，亡命徒德斯蒙德（Desperate Desmond）。尽管乐你的吧！」

说罢，他就撇下我走了。

### XIII / XIII

===Original===
I evacuated Sanstead House unostentatiously, setting off on foot down the long drive. My luggage, I gathered, was to follow me to the station in a cart. I was thankful to Providence for the small mercy that the boys were in their classrooms and consequently unable to ask me questions. Augustus Beckford alone would have handled the subject of my premature exit in a manner calculated to bleach my hair.

It was a wonderful morning. The sky was an unclouded blue, and a fresh breeze was blowing in from the sea. I think that something of the exhilaration of approaching spring must have stirred me, for quite suddenly the dull depression with which I had started my walk left me, and I found myself alert and full of schemes.

Why should I feebly withdraw from the struggle? Why should I give in to Smooth Sam in this tame way? The memory of that wink came back to me with a tonic effect. I would show him that I was still a factor in the game. If the house was closed to me, was there not the Feathers? I could lie in hiding there, and observe his movements unseen.

I stopped on reaching the inn, and was on the point of entering and taking up my position at once, when it occurred to me that this would be a false move. It was possible that Sam would not take my departure for granted so readily as I assumed. It was Sam’s way to do a thing thoroughly, and the probability was that, if he did not actually come to see me off, he would at least make inquiries at the station to find out if I had gone. I walked on.

He was not at the station. Nor did he arrive in the cart with my trunk. But I was resolved to risk nothing. I bought a ticket for London, and boarded the London train. It had been my intention to leave it at Guildford and catch an afternoon train back to Stanstead; but it seemed to me, on reflection, that this was unnecessary. There was no likelihood of Sam making any move in the matter of the Nugget until the following day. I could take my time about returning.

I spent the night in London, and arrived at Sanstead by an early morning train with a suitcase containing, among other things, a Browning pistol. I was a little ashamed of this purchase. To the Buck MacGinnis type of man, I suppose, a pistol is as commonplace a possession as a pair of shoes, but I blushed as I entered the gun-shop. If it had been Buck with whom I was about to deal, I should have felt less self-conscious. But there was something about Sam which made pistols ridiculous.

===Chinese===
我不动声色地撤出了桑斯特德宅，徒步走上那条长长的车道。据我估摸，我的行李随后自会有大车运去车站。孩子们都在教室里上课，因而无从向我发问——上天这份小小的仁慈，我感激不尽。单是一个奥古斯塔斯·贝克福德，就足以把我提前退场这个题目接过去大做文章，那做法，保管能把我的头发漂得雪白。

那天早晨妙极了。天空是一碧到底的蓝，一股清新的风正从海上吹进来。我想，必是渐近的春天那份振奋劲儿拨动了我，因为动身时压在心头的那份沉闷阴郁，忽然间便离我而去，我发觉自己精神抖擞，满肚子都是计策。

我何必这样有气无力地退出这场争斗？我何必用这等窝囊的方式向圆滑山姆服输？对那一记眨眼的回忆，带着一剂补药的效力，重新浮上我心头。我要让他瞧瞧，在这局牌里，我仍是个算数的角色。宅子既已对我关了门，不是还有羽饰旅馆吗？我可以藏在那里，人不露面地监视他的动静。

走到旅馆门口，我停了步，正要进去即刻占下阵地，忽然想到，这将是一步错棋。山姆未必会像我想当然的那样，轻轻松松便认定我已走人。山姆的做派，是办事务求彻底，十有八九，他纵然不真的前来送行，至少也要到车站打听一番，查实我确已动身。我便接着往前走了。

他不在车站。运我衣箱的大车到站时，他也没有跟来。但我打定主意，一点风险也不冒。我买了一张去伦敦的车票，登上开往伦敦的火车。我原打算在吉尔福德（Guildford）中途下车，赶一趟下午的火车回桑斯特德；可转念一想，觉得大可不必。山姆在小金块这桩事上，第二天之前多半不会有什么动作。回去的事，我可以从从容容。

我在伦敦过了一夜，次日清晨搭早班车回到桑斯特德，随身一只手提箱，里头装着几样东西，其中有一把勃朗宁（Browning）手枪。这桩采购，让我有点难为情。我料想，在巴克·麦金尼斯那一类人看来，一支手枪就跟一双鞋一样，是再寻常不过的家当；可我走进枪械铺的时候，脸是红的。倘若我要对付的是巴克，我这会儿的扭捏准会少些。奈何山姆这个人身上自有某种东西，愣是把手枪都衬得滑稽了。

===Original===
My first act, after engaging a room at the inn and leaving my suitcase, was to walk to the school. Before doing anything else, I felt I must see Audrey and tell her the facts in the case of Smooth Sam. If she were on her guard, my assistance might not be needed. But her present state of trust in him was fatal.

A school, when the boys are away, is a lonely place. The deserted air of the grounds, as I slipped cautiously through the trees, was almost eerie. A stillness brooded over everything, as if the place had been laid under a spell. Never before had I been so impressed with the isolation of Sanstead House. Anything might happen in this lonely spot, and the world would go on its way in ignorance. It was with quite distinct relief that, as I drew nearer the house, I caught sight of the wire of the telephone among the trees above my head. It had a practical, comforting look.

A tradesman’s cart rattled up the drive and disappeared round the side of the house. This reminder, also, of the outside world was pleasant. But I could not rid myself of the feeling that the atmosphere of the place was sinister. I attributed it to the fact that I was a spy in an enemy’s country. I had to see without being seen. I did not imagine that Johnson, grocer, who had just passed in his cart, found anything wrong with the atmosphere. It was created for me by my own furtive attitude.

Of Audrey and Ogden there were no signs. That they were out somewhere in the grounds this mellow spring morning I took for granted; but I could not make an extended search. Already I had come nearer to the house than was prudent.

My eye caught the telephone wire again and an idea came to me. I would call her up from the inn and ask her to meet me. There was the risk that the call would be answered by Smooth Sam, but it was not great. Sam, unless he had thrown off his role of butler completely﻿—which would be unlike the artist that he was﻿—would be in the housekeeper’s room, and the ringing of the telephone, which was in the study, would not penetrate to him.

I chose a moment when dinner was likely to be over and Audrey might be expected to be in the drawing-room.

===Chinese===
在旅馆里订下一间房、放下手提箱之后，我的头一件事，便是步行到学校去。在做任何别的事之前，我认定必须先见到奥黛丽，把圆滑山姆一案的实情告诉她。她若有了防备，我的协助也许就用不着了。可她如今对他那副深信不疑的样子，是致命的。

学校一旦没了学生，便是个冷清的所在。我小心翼翼地从树木间穿行时，园子里那股人去楼空的气象，几乎有几分瘆人。一片沉寂伏在万物之上，仿佛这地方中了妖法。我从未如此深切地体认到桑斯特德宅的与世隔绝。在这处僻静的角落里，什么事情都可能发生，而外面的世界照旧赶它的路，一无所知。待到走近宅子，我望见头顶树木间的电话线时，心里那份宽慰，真真切切。它有一副讲求实用的、叫人安心的模样。

一辆送货人的大车辘辘驶上车道，绕过宅子侧面不见了。这一份来自外部世界的提醒，也叫人愉快。可我甩不掉一种感觉：这地方的空气里有股凶邪。我把它归因于一个事实——我是在敌国潜伏的间谍，必须看人而人不看我。方才赶车过去的杂货商约翰逊（Johnson），我料想并不觉得这空气有什么不对。这空气，是专为我和我这副鬼祟做派调制出来的。

奥黛丽和奥格登踪影全无。这个温软的春晨，他们准是到园子里什么地方去了——这一点我视为当然；可我没法儿展开一场大搜查。我已经走得比审慎所允许的更近了。
